# EXODUS.

---

## CHAPTER NINETEENTH.

### THE DOCTRINE OF CHARITY.

8742. Everyone has an internal man and an external man; the internal is what is called the spiritual man, and the external is what is called the natural man. Both must be regenerated so that the person may be regenerated.

8743. With the person who is not regenerated, the external or natural man rules, and the internal or spiritual serves; but with the person who is regenerated, the internal or spiritual man rules, and the external or natural serves. This inversion cannot in any way be brought about except through regeneration by the Lord.

8744. As long as the external man is not regenerated, he places all good in pleasure, in gain, and in pride, and burns with hatred and revenge against those who oppose him; and then the internal man not only consents, but also provides reasons which confirm and promote these things: thus the internal man serves and the external rules.

8745. When however the external man has been regenerated, the internal places all good in thinking well of the neighbor and willing well to him, and the external places all good in speaking well of him and acting well toward him; and at length each has as its goal to love the neighbor—

<vocab>Exodus, internal man, external man, spiritual man, natural man, regeneration, Doctrine of Charity</vocab>

<summary>This page introduces the Doctrine of Charity as it relates to Exodus 19, explaining the distinction between the internal spiritual man and the external natural man. It describes how regeneration reverses the hierarchy of these two, allowing the spiritual man to rule over natural impulses and direct them toward the love of the neighbor.</summary>
<keywords>Exodus, Doctrine of Charity, internal man, external man, spiritual man, natural man, regeneration, spiritual hierarchy, love of neighbor, the Lord</keywords>

--- Page 14 ---
<meta>This page continues the Doctrine of Charity, concluding section 8745 and completing sections 8746 and 8747. It then transitions into the biblical text of Exodus Chapter 19, covering verses 1 through 6.</meta>

neighbor and to love the Lord, and not as before to love self and to love the world. In this case the external or natural man serves, and the internal or spiritual rules.

8746. The internal man is first regenerated by the Lord, and afterward the external man, and the latter by means of the former. The internal man is regenerated by thinking those things which are of faith and willing them, but the external man by a life according to them. The life of faith is charity.

8747. The person who is regenerated is in heaven as to their internal man, and is an angel there with angels, among whom they come after death. They can then live the life of heaven, love the Lord, love the neighbor, understand truth, enjoy good, and perceive blessedness from them. These things are the happiness of eternal life.

---

### CHAPTER XIX.

1. In the third month after the sons of Israel had gone out of the land of Egypt, on that same day they came into the wilderness of Sinai.
2. And they journeyed from Rephidim, and came to the wilderness of Sinai, and encamped in the wilderness; and there Israel camped before the mountain.
3. And Moses went up to God, and Jehovah called to him out of the mountain, saying, "Thus shall you say to the house of Jacob, and tell the sons of Israel:
4. 'You have seen what I did to the Egyptians, and how I carried you on eagles' wings, and brought you to Myself.
5. Now therefore, if you will indeed obey My voice and keep My covenant, then you shall be My own possession above all peoples; for all the earth is Mine.
6. And you shall be to Me a kingdom of priests and a holy nation.' These are the words which you shall speak to the sons of Israel."

<vocab>internal man, external man, regenerated, charity, Sinai, Rephidim, Jehovah, house of Jacob, covenant, kingdom of priests, holy nation</vocab>

<summary>This page explains that regeneration allows the internal spiritual man to rule over the external natural man, preparing a person for eternal life as an angel. It then records the arrival of the Israelites at Mount Sinai and Jehovah's offer to make them a holy nation and His own possession if they keep His covenant.</summary>
<keywords>regeneration, internal man, external man, charity, Sinai, covenant, Moses, Jehovah, house of Jacob, kingdom of priests</keywords>

<header>VERSE 16.] CHAPTER XIX.</header>

7. And Moses came and called the elders of the people, and set before them all these words which Jehovah commanded him.<note>The Latin text has *illis* (them) here, but uses *illi* in sections 8759 and 8775.</note>

8. And all the people answered together and said, "All that Jehovah has spoken we will do." And Moses reported the words of the people to Jehovah.

9. And Jehovah said to Moses, "Look, I am coming to you in a thick cloud, so that the people may hear when I speak with you and may also believe you forever." And Moses told the words of the people to Jehovah.

10. And Jehovah said to Moses, "Go to the people and sanctify them today and tomorrow, and let them wash their clothes,

11. And be ready for the third day; for on the third day Jehovah will come down in the sight of all the people upon Mount Sinai.

12. And you shall set boundaries for the people all around, saying, 'Take care not to go up onto the mountain or touch its border; whoever touches the mountain shall surely die.

13. No hand shall touch it, but he shall surely be stoned or shot through with arrows; whether it is an animal or a man, he shall not live.' When the sound of the trumpet is prolonged, they shall come up to the mountain."

14. And Moses went down from the mountain to the people and sanctified the people; and they washed their clothes.

15. And he said to the people, "Be ready for the third day; do not go near a woman."

16. And it happened on the third day, when it was morning, that there were thunders and lightnings, and a heavy cloud upon the mountain, and the sound of an exceedingly loud trumpet; and all the people who were in the camp trembled.

<keywords>Moses, Jehovah, Mount Sinai, elders, sanctification, boundaries, trumpet, thunders, lightnings, Exodus 19</keywords>

<header>EXODUS. [VERSE 17.]</header>

17. And Moses brought the people out of the camp to meet God; and they took their stand at the foot of the mountain.

18. And Mount Sinai was completely covered in smoke because Jehovah descended upon it in fire; and its smoke rose up like the smoke of a furnace, and the whole mountain quaked greatly.

19. And when the sound of the trumpet grew louder and louder, Moses spoke, and God answered him with a voice.

20. And Jehovah came down upon Mount Sinai, to the top of the mountain; and Jehovah called Moses to the top of the mountain, and Moses went up.

21. And Jehovah said to Moses, "Go down, warn the people, lest they perhaps break through to Jehovah to gaze, and many of them perish.

22. And let the priests also, who come near to Jehovah, sanctify themselves, lest Jehovah perhaps break out against them."

23. And Moses said to Jehovah, "The people cannot come up to Mount Sinai; for You warned us, saying, 'Set boundaries around the mountain, and sanctify it.'"

24. And Jehovah said to him, "Go, get down; and you shall come up—you and Aaron with you; but do not let the priests and the people break through to come up to Jehovah, lest He perhaps break out against them."

25. So Moses went down to the people and told them.

### CONTENTS.

8748. In what now follows, the subject in the *internal sense* is the revelation of *Divine truth* from the Lord out of heaven, and in this chapter, the preparation for receiving it.

<vocab>Exodus, Moses, Jehovah, Sinai, Aaron, internal sense, Divine truth</vocab>

It introduces the spiritual theme of the chapter as the preparation for receiving Divine truth.</summary>
<keywords>Mount Sinai, Moses, Jehovah, Aaron, Divine truth, internal sense, revelation, sanctification, holy boundaries</keywords>

--- Page 17 ---
<meta>This page transitions from the literal narrative of Exodus 19 to the "Internal Sense," which provides a spiritual interpretation of the biblical text. It specifically begins the analysis of verses 1 and 2, shifting focus from the physical journey of the Israelites to the spiritual states of those in the church.</meta>

No. 8750.] CHAPTER XIX. VERSES 1, 2. 11

### INTERNAL SENSE.

8749. *Verses 1, 2. In the third month after the sons of Israel had gone out from the land of Egypt, on the same day they came into the wilderness of Sinai. And they journeyed from Rephidim, and came to the wilderness of Sinai, and encamped in the wilderness; and there Israel camped before the mountain.* "In the third month" signifies fullness of state; "after the sons of Israel had gone out from the land of Egypt" signifies after those who were of the spiritual church were freed from infestations; "the same day" signifies that it was then; "they came into the wilderness of Sinai" signifies a state of good in which truths of faith were to be implanted. "And they journeyed from Rephidim" signifies continuity of life from the former state; "and came to the wilderness of Sinai" signifies to a state of good in which truths were to be implanted; "and encamped in the wilderness" signifies orderly arrangement in that state; "and there Israel camped before the mountain" signifies the orderly arrangement with those who are of the spiritual church, by Divine celestial good.

8750. *In the third month.* That this signifies fullness of state is evident from the signification of month, as a state—for all periods of time, such as days, weeks, months, and years, signify states (see n. 2788); and from the signification of three and third, as that which is complete (n. 1825, 2788, 4495, 5159); and thus "in the third month" means in fullness of state. What fullness of state is will be briefly explained. <margin>2</margin> Every state has its beginning, its progress, and its end. When a state comes to its end, it is then full, and is called fullness. In the other life all things are measured by progressions of state and their successive changes from beginning to end, just as in the world by times. This is because in heaven there are no times, but states in their place. The

<summary>This page explains the spiritual meaning of the Israelites' arrival at Mount Sinai in the third month, interpreting "the third month" as a fullness of state and their journey as a progression toward spiritual good.</summary>

<keywords>Internal Sense, Exodus 19, Sinai, fullness of state, spiritual church, infestations, progressions of state, Divine celestial good</keywords>

--- Page 18 ---
<meta>This page continues the explanation of "fullness of state" by contrasting the fixed spiritual Sun of heaven with the moving physical sun of the world, explaining how spiritual states replace time in the afterlife.</meta>

# EXODUS.
<meta>[No. 8750.</meta>

The reason is that the Sun in the other life, which is the Lord, remains constantly in its place and does not, as in the world, distinguish the day into morning, midday, evening, and night through apparent daily movements, nor distinguish the year into spring, summer, autumn, and winter through apparent annual movements. This is why there are no times in heaven, but states instead. And yet, because in the inmost sphere of heaven there is a certain progression according to the Divine heavenly form, which no one perceives, and because the states of everyone in heaven undergo changes according to that progression, the angels there alternate between being in the good of love, the truth of faith, and in obscurity regarding both. Consequently, there is a correspondence between the states there and the times in the world; namely, the state of the good of love corresponds with morning, the state of the truth of faith with midday, and an obscure state with evening and night. Moreover, the heat which comes from the Sun there is the good of love, and the light which comes from the Sun there is the truth of faith. Because of this, there is also a correspondence of heat in the world with love, which is therefore called spiritual heat, and of light in the world with faith, which is therefore called <margin>3</margin> spiritual light. In general, there are two states of life: a state of thought, which belongs to the understanding, and a state of affection, which belongs to the will. The state of thought, belonging to the understanding, refers to the truth that comes from faith. This state is enlightened by light from the Sun of heaven, which is the Lord. Similarly, the light of understanding in man originates from that light. But the state of affection, belonging to the will, refers to the good that comes from charity; also, the heat of the will in man, which is love, comes from the heat from that Sun in heaven, which is the Lord. From these things, it can now be seen how it is with states and their changes in the other life, and what is meant by the fullness

<summary>This page explains that time does not exist in heaven; instead, there are spiritual states that correspond to the times and seasons of the natural world. It describes how the Lord, as the spiritual Sun, provides the heat of love and the light of faith to the will and understanding of those in the afterlife.</summary>
<keywords>spiritual Sun, heaven, states of life, correspondence, spiritual heat, spiritual light, understanding, will, good of love, truth of faith, the Lord</keywords>

--- Page 19 ---
<meta>This page continues the explanation of spiritual states, transitioning from the first state of the spiritual church (led by truth) to the second state (led by good).</meta>

<header>No. 8753.] CHAPTER XIX. VER. 1, 2.</header>

of state which is signified by the third month. By state here, of which fullness is predicated, is meant the former state which those passed through who were of the spiritual church. That such people pass through two states—the first when they are led by the Lord by truth, the second when they are led by Him by good—may be seen above (n. 8643, 8648, 8658, 8685, 8690, 8701). Here is understood the fullness of the former state which has been described in what goes before; now the second state is described.

8751. *After the sons of Israel were gone forth out of the land of Egypt.* That this signifies after those who were of the spiritual church were liberated from infestations, is evident from the signification of "going forth," as being liberated; from the representation of the "sons of Israel," as those who were of the spiritual church (see n. 6426, 6637, 6868, 7035, 7062, 7198, 7201, 7215, 7223); and from the signification of the "land of Egypt," as infestations (n. 7278). What infestations are may be seen above (n. 7474), and also that those who were of the spiritual church were detained in the lower earth until the coming of the Lord into the world, and were liberated by the Lord (n. 6854, 6914, 7035, 7091, 7474, 7828, 7932, 8018, 8054, 8099).

8752. *The same day.* That this signifies that it was then, is evident from the signification of "the same day," as then; for by "day" is signified state (see n. 23, 487, 488, 493, 2788, 7680). Therefore "the same day" is then, when there was fullness of state.

8753. *Came they into the wilderness of Sinai.* That this signifies a state of good in which truths of faith were to be implanted, is evident from the signification of the "wilderness of Sinai," as a state of good in which truths of faith are to be implanted. "Wilderness" here is good in which truths are not yet implanted, and "Sinai" is the truths themselves. For a wilderness has several significations (see n. 3900): in general it signifies what is uninhabited and uncultivated, thus in the spiritual sense, good in which there are not yet

<summary>This page explains the spiritual transition of the Israelites from Egypt to Sinai, representing the soul's movement from being led by truth to being led by good. It clarifies the symbolic meanings of "fullness of state," the "land of Egypt" as infestations, and the "wilderness of Sinai" as a state where faith is implanted.</summary>
<keywords>sons of Israel, land of Egypt, spiritual church, wilderness of Sinai, fulness of state, infestations, truths of faith, spiritual states</keywords>

<header>EXODUS.</header>
<page-type>text</page-type>
<meta>[No. 8753.</meta>

truths, for good without truths is spiritually uncultivated. Thus a wilderness signifies the new voluntary which has <margin>2</margin>not yet been formed by the truths of faith (n. 8457). As regards Mount Sinai, it signifies in the supreme sense Divine truth from Divine good—mount signifies Divine good, and Sinai Divine truth. In the internal sense, it signifies the truth of faith from good; here, the truth of faith which is to be implanted in good, because the law was not yet promulgated from it. Mount Sinai has these significations because the law was promulgated by the Lord from there, and the law is Divine truth from Divine good, and is also truth of faith from good (n. 6752, 7463, 8695). For this reason, the sons of Israel encamped in the wilderness before that mountain, for from it were promulgated not only the ten commandments, which are the law in a restricted sense, but also all the statutes of the church, which contained in themselves—because they represented—the spiritual and celestial truths and goods of the Lord’s kingdom. That the law was promulgated from there is evident from the following chapter, and that the statutes of the church were also promulgated from there is evident from the succeeding chapters, and also in Leviticus (chap. vii. 37, 38; xxvii. 34). The same thing is signified by Sinai in David: *O God, when You went forth before Your peoples, when You marched through the wilderness; the earth trembled, the heavens also dropped at the presence of God: this Sinai at the presence of God, the God of Israel. You, O God, make the rain of Your good will to drop* (Ps. lxviii. 7-9). Here Sinai stands for truth which is from good, for such is the signification of the heavens dropping at the presence of God, <margin>3</margin>and of God making the rain of His good will to drop. And in the Book of Judges: *Jehovah, when You went forth out of Seir, when You marched out of the field of Edom, the earth trembled, the heavens also dropped, the clouds also dropped waters. The mountains flowed down before Jehovah, Sinai itself before Jehovah, the God of Israel.* In

<summary>This page explains that the wilderness signifies a new will not yet formed by faith, while Mount Sinai represents Divine truth proceeding from Divine good. It details how the Law and church statutes were promulgated from Sinai to represent the implantation of spiritual truths into a state of good.</summary>
<keywords>Mount Sinai, wilderness, Divine truth, Divine good, the Law, Ten Commandments, voluntary, Israel, David, Judges, correspondence, spiritual cultivation</keywords>

--- Page 21 ---
<meta>This page continues the exposition of the spiritual significance of Sinai, specifically how it represents Divine truth from Divine good and the implantation of faith within the heart during the process of regeneration.</meta>

<page-num>15</page-num>
<header>No. 8754.] CHAPTER XIX. VER. 1, 2.</header>

*in the days of Shamgar the son of Anath, in the days of Jael, the highways ceased, and they that walked in by-paths went through crooked paths. The lanes ceased in Israel, they ceased, until I, Deborah, arose, until I arose a mother in Israel* (v. 4–7). Here also Sinai stands for the law or Divine truth from Divine good, by virtue of which the truths of faith were implanted in its good; these things are also signified by the heavens dropping, and the clouds dropping waters. That the truths of faith were lacking and were perverted is signified by "the highways ceased" and "they that walked in by-paths went through crooked paths"—that highways, by-paths, and lanes are truths may be seen above (n. 627, 2333, 3123, 3477); for the subject in that prophetic song, which is the song of Deborah and Barak, is the perversion of the truth of the church and its restoration. So in Moses: *Jehovah came from Sinai, and rose* **4**
*from Seir unto them; He shone forth from mount Paran, and He came from the ten thousands of holiness: from His right hand was a fire of law unto them* (Deut. xxxiii. 2). In this chapter where the sons of Jacob are blessed by Moses before his death, he begins his blessing in prophecy with, "Jehovah came from Sinai," and by Sinai are signified here the truths of faith in their complex. The reason he begins this way is because the sons of Jacob signify all the truths and goods of faith (n. 3858, 3862, 3926, 3939, 6335), and in like manner so do the sons of Israel (n. 5414, 5951, 5879).

# EXODUS.

...comes to the good in which the truths of faith are not yet implanted. For truths cannot be implanted until a person is in good. Before this, they are known indeed, because they are in the memory, but then they are not in good but outside of [*extra*] good. Afterward, when a person receives new life—which they first receive when they are in good—the truths of faith are implanted and, in the internal man, enter into a kind of marriage with good. This good, in which the truths of faith have not yet been implanted, but which is still prepared so that they may be received, is what is meant here by the wilderness of Sinai. No other good is meant than Christian good, regarding which see above (n. 8635-8638).

8755. *And they journeyed from Rephidim.* That this signifies continuity of life from the previous state is evident from the meaning of journeying as continuity of life (see n. 4375, 4554, 4585, 5996, 8181, 8345, 8397, 8557); and from the meaning of Rephidim as the quality of the state of temptation with respect to truth (see n. 8561), and thus the quality of the previous state—mentioned just above (n. 8754). For in the previous state, the person of the spiritual church undergoes temptations; therefore, from that state, there is the continuity of life which is signified by journeying from Rephidim.

8756. *And came to the wilderness of Sinai.* That this signifies a state of good in which truths were to be implanted is evident from what was said above (n. 8753), where similar words occur, and concerning the quality of that good (8754).

8757. *And encamped in the wilderness.* That this signifies an orderly arrangement in that state is evident from the meaning of encamping as the setting in order of the truth and good which belong to life (see n. 8103), and thus also their orderly arrangement; and from the meaning of the wilderness as a state of good in which the truths of faith are not yet present—as mentioned above (n. 8753).

# No. 8761.] CHAPTER XIX. VER. 3-8.

...is spoken of, by this is meant being conjoined with the Lord, and the opposite is meant by coming down from heaven. The Divine truth in heaven, with which there was conjunction, is meant by God, for in the Word the Lord is called God when referring to Divine truth, and Jehovah when referring to Divine good (n. 2586, 2769, 2807, 2822, 3921, 4402, 7010, 7268, 7873, 8301); and because the conjunction of Divine truth with Divine good is treated here, He is therefore first called God and then Jehovah in this verse—in the words, Moses went up to God, and Jehovah called to him out of the mountain. It mentions the Divine truth in heaven, **2** and afterward the Divine good in heaven, for the reason that the Divine itself is far above the heavens—not only the Divine good itself, but also the Divine truth itself which proceeds immediately from the Divine good. The reason these are far above heaven is that the Divine in itself is infinite, and the infinite cannot be conjoined with what is finite; thus it cannot be conjoined with angels in the heavens except by the putting on of something finite, and so by accommodation for reception. The Divine good itself is also in itself a flame of infinite ardor—that is, of love—and no angel in heaven can bear this flame, for he would be consumed like a man in the flame of the sun if it were to blaze upon him without intermediate tempering. The light also proceeding from the flame of Divine love, which light is Divine truth, would blind all who are in heaven if it were to flow in without abatement from its own fiery splendor. From these considerations it may be evident what the difference is between the Divine good and Divine truth above the heavens, and the Divine good and Divine truth in the heavens, which are treated of here.

8761. *And Jehovah called to him out of the mountain.* That this signifies the union of the Divine good in heaven with the Divine truth there, is evident from the signification of "calling to him," or calling anyone to himself, when said of the Divine, as conjunction—in this case, union—because it

That calling someone to Himself signifies conjunction, and also presence, can be seen above (as in no. 6047, 6177, 7390, 7451, 7721). The reason it is Divine good with which there was conjunction is that the name used here is *Jehovah*—on which subject see just above (no. 8760). The meaning is further evident from that of *mountain*, which represents Divine good in heaven (no. 8758), and here represents heaven; for whether we say Divine good in heaven or heaven itself, it is the same thing, because heaven exists from that good. The situation here is like all other things abstracted from their subjects; when they are spoken of, they still lead to a perception of the subjects in which they exist—for example, when the truth of faith or good of charity is mentioned, the person of the church in whom they exist is understood. This is especially true in heaven, for there Divine good united with Divine truth is all in all, and thus the life or soul of heaven.

8762. *Thus shall you say to the house of Jacob, and tell the sons of Israel.* That this signifies the salvation of those who belong to the external and internal spiritual church is evident from the meaning of *saying* and *telling*, which in this instance signifies salvation, for the things that are now said and told by Jehovah through Moses involve salvation; and from the meaning of the *house of Jacob* and the *sons of Israel* as the external and internal church (see no. 3305, 4286). What is meant by the external church and the internal church has been explained before in several places: namely, that the external of the Ancient Church consisted of everything that represented the internal, and the internal of the church was that which was represented by the externals. For example, those who made Divine worship consist of sacrifices and the rites and statutes that represented the spiritual and heavenly things of the Lord’s kingdom were in externals; but those who made Divine worship consist at the same time in the heavenly and

<meta>This page continues the distinction between the external and internal church, focusing on how true spiritual life requires both internal principles (love and charity) and external practices (worship and obedience).</meta>

<header>No. 8763.] CHAPTER XIX. VER. 3-8. 21</header>

spiritual things which were represented were in the internals. In the same way today, those who make Divine worship consist of attending church, hearing sermons, and participating in the Holy Supper, and who do these things with devotion without thinking any further about them than that they ought to be done because they are instituted and commanded—these belong to the external church. But those who simultaneously believe that such things are to be done, and yet that the essential part of worship is the life of faith—that is, charity toward the neighbor and love to the Lord—these belong to the internal church. Consequently, those also belong to the external church who do good to their neighbor and worship the Lord merely out of the obedience of faith; but those belong to the internal church who do good to their neighbor and worship the Lord from love. This applies in all other cases.

<margin>**2**</margin>
Nevertheless, in everyone who belongs to the church there ought to be both—namely, an external and an internal; otherwise, there is no spiritual life in them, for the internal is like the soul, and the external is like the body of the soul. But those who belong to the external church are clearly in its externals and obscurely in its internals, whereas those who belong to the internal church are clearly in internals and obscurely in externals. However, those who are in externals and not simultaneously in internals do not belong to the church. All those are in both who live a good life according to the doctrines of their church; but those who are in worship and not simultaneously in a good life according to the doctrines of the church are in externals without internals. Few people know this, and the reason is that they make the whole of worship, and thereby the whole of salvation, consist of faith and not at all of charity; therefore it is also that those who think about eternal salvation make it consist of a life of piety and not at all of a life of charity (see no. 8252–8257).

8764. *And how I bore you on eagles' wings.* That this signifies that thus by truths they were elevated to heavenly light, is clear from the meaning of carrying anyone on eagles' wings, as being elevated on high even to heavenly light; for by carrying is signified being elevated, by wings are signified spiritual truths, and by an eagle the rational part of the mind regarding truth. That an eagle has this meaning may be seen above (n. 3901), and for the reason that eagles fly on high. For the ancients by the visible heaven understood the angelic heaven. Indeed, the simple believed that the home of the angels was there, and also that heavenly light itself was on high, because it was nearer the sun and stars. Therefore it is that to be carried on eagles' wings means to be carried on high into that light. That elevation there is effected by the truths of faith, is because the truth of faith is what elevates a person even to heaven, where its good is. That the rational regarding truth is an eagle, is because the rational part of a person is his heaven, and the natural part is respectively as earth; for the rational constitutes 
<margin>2</margin> the internal person and the natural the external person. That wings are spiritual truths, is because birds in general signify intellectual things and thoughts (n. 40, 745, 776, 3219, 5149, 7441); and for this reason wings are spiritual truths, since everything intellectual comes from these truths. The intellect derived from falsehoods, however discerning and sharp it appears, is not truly intellectual; for the real intellect sees from the light of heaven, and the light of heaven is spiritual truth, that is, the truth of faith. Therefore, where there is no truth of faith, there is no light,

Afterward, it is described by the prophet in the same chapter how that church established among the ancients was perverted among the Jews.

(Isaiah 40:31). In *David*: *God rode upon a cherub and flew; yes, He flew swiftly upon the wings of the wind* (Psalms 18:10; 104:3)—describing Divine truth and its power. 8765. *And brought you unto Myself.* That this signifies being brought to the good of love which is in heaven is evident from this: Jehovah, who says that they were brought to Himself, is the Divine good of Divine love (see n. 2586, 2769, 2807, 2822, 3921, 4402, 7010, 7268, 7873, 8301, 8760), here meaning the Divine good of Divine love in heaven (n. 8761). This is because a person is brought to good through the truths of faith, and thus into heaven and to the Lord there. For a person is not in heaven—and thus not with the Lord—until he is in good, that is, in the affection of

...of charity, believe that anyone can come into heaven and to the Lord, regardless of how they may have lived; for they do not know what the life of man is, and because they do not know this, they suppose that the life is nothing. Therefore, if they are asked whether an evil man can be among the good, they say that he can by the mercy of God, because it is a work of omnipotence. Indeed, if they are asked whether a devil can become an angel of heaven, they answer affirmatively, if only he is willing to receive faith; for they have no doubt about his being able to receive it. But if they are told that evil cannot be turned into good, and thus hell cannot be turned into heaven within a person, and that this is impossible because it is contrary to order, and therefore contrary to Divine truth—and thus contrary to God Himself, who is order—they answer to this that such ideas are merely reasonings about salvation, for which they do not care. From these and from countless other cases, it may be evident into what blindness concerning salvation and eternal life the doctrine of faith alone leads.

8766. *Now therefore, if in hearing you shall hear My voice.* That this signifies the reception of truth is evident from the meaning of hearing as obedience (see n. 2542, 3869, 4652-4660); and because hearing is obedience, it is also reception (n. 5471, 5475, 7216); and from the meaning of the voice of Jehovah as the Word, and thus Divine truth (n. 219, 220, 6971, 7573).

8767. *And keep My covenant.* That this signifies life in what is good and thereby conjunction, is evident from the meaning of keeping the covenant as living according to the commandments—thus in what is good—and so being conjoined to the Lord. That keeping the covenant is living according to the commandments, and thus in what is good and so being conjoined to the Lord, is because the articles of the covenant were all the things commanded, which were called testimonies, judgments, laws, and statutes; specifically, the Ten Commandments. The Lord, who was represented by the kings, signifies Divine
<margin>**2**</margin> truth (n. 1728, 2015, 3670, 6148). In the representative church among the descendants of Jacob, there was first a kingdom of judges, afterward a kingdom of priests, and lastly a kingdom of kings. By the kingdom of judges was represented Divine truth from Divine good; but by the kingdom of priests, who were also judges, was represented Divine good from which Divine truth comes; and by the kingdom of kings was represented Divine truth without Divine good. But when something of the priesthood was also added to the kingly office, then Divine truth was also represented by the kings, in which there was as much good as there was of the priesthood added to the kingly
<margin>**3**</margin> office. All these things were instituted in the Jewish Church so that through them the states of heaven might be represented; for in heaven there are two kingdoms, one of which is called the celestial kingdom and the other of which is called the spiritual kingdom. The celestial kingdom is what is called the priestly function, and the spiritual kingdom is what is called the kingly function of the Lord. In the latter, Divine truth reigns, and in the former, Divine good. And because the representation of the celestial kingdom began to perish when they sought a king, therefore—so that a representation of the Lord's kingdom in the heavens might still be continued—the tribe of Judah was separated from the Israelites, and the celestial kingdom of the Lord was represented by the kingdom of Judah, and
<margin>**4**</margin> His spiritual kingdom by the kingdom of Israel. Those who know these things can understand the reasons why the forms of government with the descendants of Jacob were successively changed, and why, when they asked for a king, it was said to them by Jehovah through Samuel that by doing so they rejected Jehovah, so that He should not be king over them (1 Sam. viii. 7); and then they were told the manner of the king who would rule over them (verse 11 and following), by which Divine truth without good is described.

8771. *And a holy nation.* That this signifies the spiritual kingdom is evident from the meaning of a "nation" as those who are in good (see nos. 1259, 1260, 1416, 1849, 6005); and from the meaning of "holy" as predicated of truth which comes from good (nos. 6788, 7499, 8127, 8302, 8330); therefore, a "holy nation" is good from which truth comes. It is called "a kingdom of priests" and "a holy nation," and each expression signifies the spiritual kingdom, but with a difference. A "kingdom of priests" signifies those who are in good from truth, but a "holy nation" signifies those who are in good and consequently in truth. Those who are in good from truth look through truths up to the Lord, but those who are in good and consequently in truth are in the Lord, and from Him, they regard truths. These two states succeed each other with those who are being regenerated, in whom the spiritual kingdom—that is, the life of heaven—is being implanted by the Lord; for by truth they are introduced into good, and thus into heaven, since heaven is good. And when they are in heaven, then from that state and from there, they regard truths.

That it is for the receiving of truths in good is because it is said of the sons of Israel, by whom the spiritual church is signified, and the spiritual church exists with those who are in good in which truths are present. <meta>This page continues the explanation of the internal sense of Exodus 19:8, specifically discussing the process of spiritual influx and how the Lord forms good within a person through the truths of faith.</meta>

<page-num>32</page-num>
<header>EXODUS.</header>
<meta>[No. 8772.</meta>

...the formation of good from truths knows the most profound arcana of heaven, for he knows the arcana of the formation of man made new—that is, the formation of heaven or the Lord's kingdom within him. All Christian good, or spiritual good, contains within itself the truths of faith, for the quality of that good is derived from the truths which belong to faith. Good which does not derive its quality from the truths of faith is not Christian good, but natural good, which does not grant eternal life. The reason is that natural good has within it only natural life, and this life is not unlike the life of animals, for they also are in a state of good when they are gentle; however, animals cannot receive spiritual life. From this, it is clear that spiritual life is acquired <margin>2</margin> only through the truths of faith. This life—namely, spiritual life—is acquired first by knowing the truths of faith, then by acknowledging them, and finally by believing them. When they are only known, they are, as it were, at the entrance; when they are acknowledged, they are in the reception hall; but when they are believed, they are in the inner chamber. Thus, they advance from outer things toward inner things successively. In the internal man, there is good which is continually flowing in from the Lord, and there it joins itself with truths and causes them to become faith and, afterward, charity. This good attracts truths to itself, for it has a desire for them, so that by using them it may procure for itself a quality and thereby exist in a specific form. <margin>3</margin> When, therefore, those truths are joined to good, then man is regenerated; for then he no longer looks from truths at what is to be believed and what is to be done, but he looks from good, because he is imbued with truths and has them within himself. Nor does he care for truths of any other nature than those he can see from his own good; and he sees more and more continually, since they are produced from that good as offspring are from their parents. These offspring come from the marriage of good and truth, which is called the heavenly marriage. The truths which are produced from this have good within themselves because they are born from it.

<vocab>arcana, truths of faith, spiritual good, natural good, regeneration, heavenly marriage, charity</vocab>

It distinguishes this spiritual life from merely natural good, which lacks the internal truths necessary for eternal life.</summary>

8773. *And Moses came and called the elders of the people.* That this signifies the choice of those who were primarily in the intelligence of truth, is evident from the meaning of "calling to himself" as choice; and from the meaning of "the elders of the people" as those who are primarily in the intelligence of truth—and in a sense abstracted from person, primary truths (see n. 6524, 6525, 6890, 8578, 8585). The case here is this: those who are in good not yet formed by truths (the good which is treated of in this chapter) are first formed by the Lord through primary truths—that is, through general truths, in which and from which all others exist. The primary truths are: that God is one; that the Lord was born a man so that He might save the human race; that there is a heaven and that there is a hell; that those who have lived well go to heaven and those who have lived an evil life go to hell; also that love to God and love toward the neighbor are the commandments on which all others depend, and that this love cannot be given except through faith. These and similar principles are primary truths, and these are first implanted by the Lord within the good in the man who is being regenerated. When these truths have been implanted and have become truths of good, then the rest are implanted; they are arranged in order within and under those general or primary truths according to the heavenly form. Thus, by degrees, they cause the life of heaven to be in that man and cause him to be, as it were, a heaven in a small image; that is, they cause all things belonging to his understanding and will to be in consociation with the goods and truths of angels, and thus to be with the angels.

By revelation here in the internal sense is not meant revelation such as was made to the Israelite people from Mount Sinai, when the Lord spoke in a loud voice and the people standing around heard; but such a revelation is meant as is not made with a loud voice, but inwardly in man. This revelation is made by the enlightening of the internal sight, which belongs to the understanding, when a person who is in affection for truth from good reads the Word. This enlightening is then effected by the light of heaven, which is from the Lord as the Sun there. By this light, the understanding is enlightened in the same way as the external sight of the eye is enlightened by the light from the sun of the world. When the understanding is enlightened by that Divine light, it then perceives what is true as being true; it acknowledges it inwardly within itself and, as it were, sees it. Such is the revelation of those who are in affection for truth from good <margin>3</margin> when they read the Word. But those who are in affection for truth from evil—that is, those who desire to know truth merely for the sake of honors, gain, reputation, and the like—do not see truths. Instead, they only see things that confirm the doctrines of their church, whether those doctrines be true or false. The light which then enlightens these people is not Divine light from heaven, but the light of the senses, such as that of the infernals; this light, in the presence of heavenly light, becomes mere thick darkness. For when these people read the Word, they are completely blind to the truth that does not align with their doctrine—for example, those who place salvation in faith alone, when they read the Word, do not pay any attention to the things said there concerning love and charity; indeed, they do not even see them, for such things fall into the shade of their vision, like things

8781. *Lo, I come unto thee in the thickness of the cloud.* That this signifies that it shall be made in a most natural form is evident from the meaning of "coming to you," when it is said by Jehovah to Moses (who represents truth from the Divine which belongs to the spiritual church, n. 8760), as revelation; and from the meaning of "the thickness of the cloud," as being in a most natural form, such as the Word of the Old Testament is in its literal sense. 8782. *That the people may hear when I speak with you.* That this signifies that those who are of the spiritual church may apprehend Divine things is evident from the meaning of hearing as perceiving (see n. 5017), and thus apprehending; from the representation of the sons of Israel (who are the people here) as those who are of the spiritual church—of which much has been said above; and from the meaning of "when I speak" as Divine truths; for the voice of Jehovah is Divine truth (n. 219, 220, 3563, 6971), and so also is His speech.

8783. *And may also believe you forever.* That this signifies that there may be a faith in truth that will endure is evident from the representation of Moses as truth from the Divine (see n. 6771, 6827, 7014); from the meaning of believing as faith; and from the meaning of "forever" as that which shall endure. Regarding this: Divine truth is not received by anyone unless it is accommodated to his understanding—that is, unless it is presented in a natural form and appearance. For human minds at first apprehend nothing but earthly and worldly things, and not at all spiritual and heavenly things. Therefore, if spiritual and heavenly things were exposed in their naked form, they would be rejected as if they were nothing—according to the Lord's words in John: *If I have told you earthly things and you do not believe, how will you believe if I tell you heavenly things?* (3:12). Still less could those people apprehend spiritual

For those who regard earthly and worldly things as their goal—that is, who love them above all things—feel an aversion toward spiritual things and almost abhor the very mention of them. It is nearly the same at the present day. The learned of the world indeed believe that they would receive the Word more readily if heavenly things were presented plainly, and if it were not written with such simplicity. 8786. *And Jehovah said to Moses.* That this signifies revelation concerning preparation, is evident from the meaning of saying, when it is by Jehovah, as revelation from the Divine. That it is revelation concerning preparation is evident from what follows, for there the subject is the manner in which they were to be prepared to receive truth from the Divine.

8787. *Go to the people.* That this signifies conjunction, is evident from the representation of Moses at this time as truth from the Divine which is beneath heaven (see above, no. 8760); and from the representation of the sons of Israel, who are here the people he was to go to, as those who are of the spiritual church—of which much has been said above. That "go to the people," when said by Jehovah, means conjunction, is because truth from the Divine, which is represented by Moses, is a mediator of the Divine with those who are of the church; for in order that conjunction may be achieved, which is here represented by the covenant between Jehovah and the people, there must be mediation. Therefore, Moses also represents truth from the Divine beneath heaven conjoined with Divine truth in heaven (no. 8760), and this is for the sake of mediation.

8788. *And sanctify them today and tomorrow.* That this signifies the veiling of the internal aspects so that they may appear in the holiness of faith now and afterward, is evident from the meaning of sanctifying as arranging things so that they appear in holiness regarding the external; and since this is achieved by the veiling of the internal aspects, this is also understood by sanctifying. That "today" and "tomorrow" mean now and afterward is evident. It may be briefly explained how these things are: the church established among the Jews was not, as far as they were concerned, a church, but only the representative of a church; for in order for there to be a church, the person of the church must have faith in the Lord, as well as love for Him, and likewise love toward the

Thus, their external practices could be communicated with spirits, and through them with angels, without the internal aspects. However, if the internal aspects had not been veiled, they would have been exposed, and then the representation would have perished, because unclean things would have burst forth and contaminated it. The Israelitish people, above all others, could be veiled in this way because, more than any others, they worshipped external things and placed holiness, even the Divine, within them. This coming of the Lord is understood by the coming down of Jehovah in the sight of all the people. The enlightenment which then took place is signified by "in the sight of all the people," for in the internal sense the eye is the understanding, and therefore the sight of the eye is the perception belonging to faith, which comes from the light of heaven. For this reason, "in the sight" means enlightenment. That the eye is the understanding, and its sight the perception of faith, may be seen above (n. 4403-4421, 4523-4534).

8793. *Upon Mount Sinai.* That this signifies in the good in which truth is to be implanted is evident from the meaning of Mount Sinai as the good in which the truths of faith are to be implanted (see n. 8753). The truth that is to be implanted in good is made known in the following chapter. The Ten Commandments, which were proclaimed from Mount Sinai at that time, are internal truths; and the laws and statutes commanded in the following chapters are external truths. All of these signify the truths that are to be implanted in good.

8794. *And you shall set bounds for the people all around.* That this signifies extension into heaven no further than to the spiritual spheres of good is evident from the meaning of setting bounds all around as the extension of a sphere into heaven to certain limits, which are determined by the good of each person; and from the representation of the sons of Israel (who are the people spoken of here) as those who are in spiritual good in which truth is to be implanted; for this state of good of those who belong to the spiritual church is described in this and the following chapters (see n. 8753). Therefore, "you shall set bounds for the people all around" signifies extension into heaven no further than to the spiritual
<margin>2</margin> spheres of good. What is meant by extension into heaven to the spiritual spheres of good cannot be known to anyone except through revelation. The situation is this: every good given to a person by regeneration from the Lord has an extension to societies in heaven. This extension differs in both extent and quality for each person—in extent, when it reaches to more remote limits; and in quality, when it reaches more toward the interior or more toward the exterior parts of heaven. Good itself flows into a person from the Lord through the societies of heaven that are all around, as good is not given without influx through these societies. The societies of heaven are in continuous connection all around, and no discontinuity is seen anywhere. It is the same with everything and each

<margin>3</margin> Furthermore, this has been frequently shown through living experience; for sometimes communication with certain societies was removed, and then only as much life and such a quality of life remained as corresponded to the degree and quality of extension into the remaining societies. Every angel, spirit, and person has a spiritual sphere, which exists according to their extension into societies; such extension is not into the societies themselves, but into the spheres of their good. These are <term>arcana</term> that are unknown to people, but in the other life they are very well known. But in this, they are completely deceived, for all human life is from the Lord through societies.

<margin>4</margin> It should further be known that the extension of life for those who belong to the spiritual church reaches to the angelic spheres in the second heaven (which is called the spiritual heaven), but not to the third heaven where the celestial are. The reason is that a person of the spiritual church cannot receive the Divine as it exists in the inmost or third heaven where the celestial are, except in such a general way that it does not reach their perception; this is because those who are spiritual cannot reach even the outer threshold of the good in which the celestial reside (n. 2718, 3833, 6500, 8521). From these things it is clear what is meant by extension into heaven no further than to the spiritual spheres of good, which is signified by the command, *you shall set bounds for the people all around*; it also clarifies what follows.

8795. *Saying, Take heed to yourselves, that you do not go up into the mountain.* That this signifies no extension at all to the celestial societies which are in the love of good, is evident from the signification of going up, as pertaining to the Divine which is in a higher heaven; and from the signification of mountain, as the good of love there (n. 4210, 6435, 8327, 8758). From this it is plain that intermediates are borders, and that those who are of the spiritual kingdom cannot reach even to the first boundaries of the good of the celestial kingdom (n. 2718, 3833, 6500, 8521). Therefore it is that by taking heed not to touch the border of the mountain, it is signified that there shall be no extension to the celestial societies which are in the love of good, and not even to the intermediates.

anyone who forces themselves as far as the celestial societies will perish. This is evident from the meaning of "mountain" as the good of love (see just above, n. 8795)—here representing the Divine good of Divine love in the inmost heaven, because the people who are below represent the Lord's spiritual kingdom, which is the middle heaven; from the meaning of "touching" as penetrating there, in this case forcing themselves in (which will be discussed shortly); and from the meaning of "being put to death" as perishing.

<margin>2</margin>The situation is this: those who belong to the Lord's spiritual kingdom cannot flow into the celestial kingdom, because the latter is higher or interior and the former is lower or exterior; for what is exterior or lower cannot flow into what is higher or interior (n. 5259, 5779). Yet it sometimes happens that those who are in a lower heaven are eager to ascend into a higher heaven, driven by a wrong desire originating in pride, the love of dominion, or envy. Those who make the attempt from such a desire are consequently allowed in, but they then undergo suffering. They are seized with anxiety and eventually with grief, as well as with blindness, so that they lose both their intelligence and happiness. Therefore, they throw themselves down as if they were about to die, and they do not recover life until they are cast down out of heaven.

<margin>3</margin>Such is the fate of those who, from the lust for pride, dominion, or envy, force themselves from a lower heaven into a higher one. The reason is that the good of love in the higher heaven is like a consuming fire to them, and the truth of faith there is a fiery light that blinds the sight of those who rise into it. This is what is meant in the internal sense by the command that the Israelite people should not touch Mount Sinai, where Jehovah was. The situation would be similar for those in the celestial kingdom if they should happen to attempt to ascend higher to the Divine; and likewise for those who are below heaven if they desire to ascend into heaven. That these latter suffer severely has been shown above (see n. 4225, 4226, 4299, 5057, 5058).

# EXODUS. [No. 8798.

8798. *No hand shall touch it.* That this signifies those who force themselves in out of self-confidence is evident from the meaning of "touching"—namely, the mountain—as forcing themselves into Divine celestial good (see just above, n. 8797); and from the meaning of "hand" as power (n. 4931-4937, 5327, 5328, 6292, 6947, 7188, 7189, 7518, 7673, 8050, 8153, 8281), and thus also self-confidence (n. 878, 5544).

It has been shown just above (n. 8797) that in such a case they are seized with blindness.

8800. *Or by shooting with darts he shall be shot.* That this signifies that spiritual good shall also perish is evident from the meaning of "being shot" as perishing in respect to spiritual good; for an "archer" signifies the spiritual man (see n. 2686, 2709, 6422).

8801. *Whether it be beast or man, it shall not live.* That this signifies that good and truth shall lose their spiritual life is evident from the meaning of "beast" as an affection for good (see n. 45, 46, 142, 143, 246, 714, 715, 776, 2179, 2180, 3218, 3519, 5198, 7523); from the meaning of "man" as truth (n. 3134, 3459, 7716); and from the meaning of "living" as spiritual life (n. 5890). Therefore, "not to live" is to lose that life. Good and truth lose life when influx from the Lord is no longer perceived, for it is from this that they have their life. At that point, good and truth indeed appear in their outward appearance as good and truth, but no differently than a painting, which in itself is not alive.

# No. 8802.] CHAPTER XIX. VER. 10—13.

*out.* That this signifies those who have a general perception of celestial good is evident from the meaning of "when the sound of the trumpet shall be drawn out," or what is the same, "when the sound of the horn is heard," as a general perception of celestial good; for by being heard is signified perception (n. 5017, 8361), and by the trumpet or the sound of the horn is signified celestial good. The reason why celestial good is signified by the sound of a horn or trumpet is that musical instruments, because of their sounds, correspond to affections for truth and good—stringed instruments to affections for truth, and wind instruments to affections for good (n. 418-420, 4138, 8337). The trumpet, being a wind instrument and of a full tone, corresponded to an affection for celestial good; therefore also the jubilee, by which was represented the marriage of good and truth which is in the inmost heaven, was named from it <note>from *jobel*, a trumpet</note>. Therefore, it is for this reason that "when the sound of the trumpet shall be drawn out" signifies those who have a general perception of celestial good. By those **2** who are in a general perception of celestial good are meant those who are intermediate between the celestial kingdom and the spiritual kingdom of the Lord, through whom, therefore, influx, communication, and conjunction are effected (see just above, n. 8787, 8796). These have extension even into celestial societies, which extension is signified by "they shall come up to the mount." These who are intermediate are represented by Moses (n. 8787) and by Aaron, to whom it was granted to go up into the mount (see verse 24). What is meant by extension into the spheres of angelic societies, and that everyone's extension is according to his good, may be seen above (n. 8794). General perception is found with those who are in spiritual good and can receive a general influx of the good of the higher heaven—in this case, of the inmost heaven. Such are meant by those who shall come up to the mount when the trumpet is heard.

<meta>This page continues the internal sense analysis of Exodus 19, focusing on the preparation of the people to receive Divine truth and the specific symbolic roles of Moses and Mount Sinai.</meta>

<header>EXODUS. [No. 8803.</header>

8803. *They shall come up to the mountain.* That this signifies extension even to celestial societies is evident from what was said and shown above (nos. 8794, 8795), and also just above (no. 8802).

8804. Verses 14, 15. *And Moses went down from the mountain to the people, and sanctified the people; and they washed their clothes. And he said to the people, "Be ready for the third day; do not come near a woman."* “And Moses went down from the mountain to the people” signifies application and preparation by truth from the Divine for receiving truths in good; “and sanctified the people” signifies the veiling of the internal aspects so that they may appear in the holiness of faith; “and they washed their clothes” signifies the purification of the truths of faith. “And he said to the people, 'Be ready for the third day'” signifies full preparation; “do not come near a woman” signifies the purification of the good of faith.

<margin>2</margin> 8805. *And Moses went down from the mountain to the people.* That this signifies application and preparation by truth from the Divine for receiving truths in good is evident from the meaning of “going down,” when said of Moses (who represents truth from the Divine), as application and also preparation through that truth; from the representation of Moses as truth from the Divine (see nos. 8760, 8787), and also as that which mediates (no. 8787), thus likewise that which prepares and applies; from the meaning of “mountain” as the Divine in heaven, and thus heaven itself; and from the representation of the children of Israel as those who belong to the spiritual church—those who are in truth through which there is good and in good from which there is truth—in this case, in the good in which truths are to be received. It is important to know what is specifically signified by Mount Sinai, what is represented by the people of Israel, and also what is represented by Moses in this chapter and in the following chapters of the Book of Exodus. By Mount Sinai is specifically signified heaven, from which truths flow in from the Lord;

<summary>Moses' descent from the mountain symbolizes the preparation of the spiritual church to receive Divine truth, involving a process of sanctification and the purification of faith. Mount Sinai represents heaven as the source of these truths.</summary>

<header>No. 8806.] CHAPTER XIX. VER. 14, 15.</header>

Therefore, by the coming down of Jehovah upon that mountain, His presence in heaven is signified. And since heaven—where Jehovah is, that is, the Lord—is signified by Mount Sinai, it also signifies Divine good united to Divine truth there; for it is from this that heaven is heaven. <margin>3</margin> But by the people of Israel near this mountain, the spiritual church is represented as to the good in which the truths of faith are to be implanted. For it has been shown before that those who are of the spiritual church pass through two states: the first when they are led through truths to good, and the second when they are in good and, from good, in truths. In the present case, they are in the good in which truths are to be implanted, which is a middle state between the first and the second. The truths which are implanted in good are contained in those things that were promulgated from Mount Sinai by the Lord and were communicated by Moses to the people. In this and the following chapters, Moses represents truth from the Divine below heaven, conjoined to Divine truth in heaven, and therefore mediating between the Divine in heaven and the good in which truths are to be implanted (which is that of the spiritual church), thus mediating between the Lord and the people. It is important to know these things for the sake of understanding what follows in the Book of Exodus. From this it may also be evident that "Moses went down from the mountain to the people" signifies application and preparation by means of truth from the Divine for receiving truths in good.

8806. *And sanctified the people.* This signifies veiling the internal aspects so that they may appear in the holiness of faith, as is evident from what was explained above (no. 8788). However, it was shown there what sanctification was with the Israelite people: namely, that it was a veiling of the internal aspects so that, regarding their external aspects, they might appear in what was holy when they were in representative worship;

And that unless their <term>interiors</term> had then been veiled, they would not only have defiled the holiness of worship through unclean thoughts and evil affections, but would also have totally destroyed it, so that nothing celestial and spiritual, which was represented in the external forms, would have been per-
2 ceived from them in heaven. But since by the sons of Israel are here meant those who belong to the <term>spiritual church</term>, it shall be briefly explained what is meant by <term>sanctification</term> in their case. Those who belong to the <term>spiritual church</term> and live a life of truth, and thereby a life of good, are withheld from evils and are kept in good by the Lord. The good which comes from the Lord is holy within them. Therefore, as far as they receive good from the Lord, they are holy; and they receive as much good from the Lord—that is, they are holy to that degree—as they live a life of good according to the genuine truths of faith and, at the same time, believe that all the good which they then think and do is from the Lord. Then, too, their evils are separated so as not to appear at all, especially those which have been inherited from parents and are filthy, because they have been successively accumulated through several previous generations and thus amassed in the offspring. These are the <term>interiors</term> which, for those who belong to the <term>spiritual church</term>, are hidden away and, as it were, veiled (n. 966, 1667, 2307, 2308, 3701, 4317, 8550). Therefore, it is that all the <term>voluntary</term> part which they possess by inheritance is destroyed. For this reason, a new <term>voluntary</term> part is formed within them in the <term>intellectual part</term> by means of the truths of faith from the Lord. This is why, when they are being <term>regenerated</term>, they are led by truths to good, and afterward, when they are <term>regenerated</term>, from good into the truths of good. Hence it is plain how a new <term>voluntary</term> <note>This corrects the original spelling error in the manuscript.</note> part is created in them by the Lord. That this is formed in the <term>intellectual part</term> for those who belong to the <term>spiritual church</term> may be seen above (n. 863, 875, 895, 927, 1023, 1043, 1044, 1555, 2256, 4328, 4493, 5113, 6125).

signifies the purification of the truths of faith, as is evident from what was said above (n. 8789), where similar words occur.

8808. *And he said to the people, "Be ready for the third day."* That this signifies full preparation is evident from what was said above (n. 8790), where similar words appear.

8809. *Do not come near a woman.* That this signifies the purification of the good of faith is evident from the fact that marriages among the Israelite race were impure because their <term>interiors</term> were unclean; for marriage love descends from the marriage of good and truth. Therefore, those whose <term>interiors</term> are not in good and truth cannot be in a pure marriage; and those whose <term>interiors</term> are in evil and falsity, as that nation was, are in an impure marriage. This is why not coming near a woman means to abstain from what is impure. That marriage love descends from the heavenly marriage—which is the marriage of good and truth—may be seen above (n. 2727–2759, 2803, 3132, 4434, 4835), and that among the people descended from Jacob there was nothing of true marriage, which is why they were permitted to have more than one wife (n. 3246). But as for those who belong to the <term>spiritual church</term> and are represented here by the sons of Israel, they also are not in anything of true marriage until truth is implanted in good within them. Before this happens, there is indeed marriage, but not pure marriage, for there is no internal marriage of good and truth prior to this. This is why not coming near a woman signifies, in the internal sense, to be purified regarding the good of faith; for this chapter treats of the good in which truth has not yet been implanted, while the following chapters treat of that implantation.

8810. Verses 16–19. *And it happened on the third day, when it was morning, that there were voices and lightnings, and a heavy cloud upon the mountain, and the voice of a trumpet exceeding loud; and all the people in the camp trembled. And Moses brought out the people...*

8812. *When it was morning.* That this signifies a state when they were in good is evident from the meaning of morning, as a state of the good of love (see n. 8426). From what is said here—that Jehovah, that is, the Lord, descended upon Mount Sinai on the third day, and also in the morning of that day—it is very clear that this represented something Divine in heaven. This cannot be known to anyone except from the correspondence of such things as exist in the natural world with things that are in the spiritual world, and from the meaning following from that, as in the present case regarding what morning signifies and what the third day signifies. The reason the third day is the end of a former state is because by "three" is signified what is full from beginning to end (8790). The reason morning is a state of the good of love is because the Sun which gives light to the angels and to the whole heaven in the other life is the Lord. The fire there is His Divine love, which gives the heat of life to every living being, and the light there is the Divine truth, which enlightens all who receive it—altogether different from the sun of this world, whose fire is physical fire and not love, and whose light is physical light and not truth. From these things it may be evident what <margin>2</margin> the effects of the fire and also of the light from the sun of the world are, and what the effects of the fire and light from the Sun of heaven are: namely, that from the former, the heat and light are without life, but from the latter, the heat and light are accompanied by life. These latter—namely, those which come from the Sun of heaven—are therefore called spiritual because they have life in them, and the former, which are from the sun of the world, are called natural and have no life in them. The life which is perceived in living beings within heat and from heat is not from the heat of the sun of the world, but is from the heat of

When this heat flows into the heat of the world, it produces that effect and is felt in the body as elementary heat; but within it is vital heat that has its origin from love, which is heat from the Sun of heaven. That the origin of the heat of life is from another source, and that it is in love and according to the measure and quality of love, everyone may know if they will only reflect rightly—except for those who do not acknowledge anything internal <margin>3</margin> in man and who ascribe all things to nature. Since, therefore, the heat from the Sun of heaven, which is the Lord, is the good of love, and the light from it is the truth of faith, it may be evident what is signified by morning and what by noon, by evening, and by night in the other life: namely, that they are states of good and truth, or of love and faith—morning a state of the good of love, noon a state of the truth of faith, and evening and night the deprivation of these, which is ignorance and blindness in the things which are of faith, as well as torpor and cold in the things <margin>4</margin> which are of heavenly love. Moreover, the case is similar with the Sun of heaven as with the sun of the world: namely, that it is fixed and does not cause those states by any revolution of its own; but that they are caused by the revolution of the surrounding bodies, as by the earth revolving around the sun and at the same time around its own axis; from which comes the appearance that those changes proceed from the sun, when yet they are not from the sun but from the world revolving about it. So also in heaven, the changes of state there, to which morning, noon, evening, and night correspond, do not proceed from the Sun there, for the Sun is always sending forth heat and light, that is, the good of love and the truth of faith; but those changes arise with those who receive—namely, with angels and spirits who by regular turns according to their life are now in morning, that is, in the good of love, now in midday, that is, in the truth of faith, now in evening and night, <margin>5</margin> that is, in shade and torpor as to love and faith. The reason why the case is similar in the world as in heaven—with the difference that in the world they are states of time which so succeed each other, and in heaven states of life—is that all things in the world were created in the image of things that are in heaven, since natural things exist from spiritual things as effects from their causes. For this reason, there is a correspondence of all things in the world with those which are in heaven, and therefore universal nature is a representative theater of the kingdom of the Lord (n. 3483, 4939, 8211).

8813. *That there were voices and lightnings.* This signifies a Divine state which was revelation, as is evident from the signification of "voices" or sounds of thunder as Divine truths; and from the signification of "lightnings" as the glowing and flashing of those truths, for Divine truths flash and glow from the flaming light from the Sun of heaven. This is why by "voices" and "lightnings" a Divine state is signified in which there is revelation—a Divine state by such things descending from heaven, and revelation by the voices and lightnings being Divine truths. That by "voices of thunder" from heaven are signified Divine truths may be seen above (n. 7573); and that lightnings are the flashings issuing from them which beam upon the internal sight of man, enlightening those who are in truth from good and confounding those who are in falsity from evil, is evident from passages in the Word where lightnings are mentioned—as in David:

> *The clouds poured out water; the skies sent out a voice: Your arrows also went abroad; the voice of Your thunder into the world; the lightnings lit up the world: the earth trembled and shook* (Ps. 77:17, 18)—where the "voice of thunder" stands for Divine truth (n. 7573), "lightnings" for its flashing, and the "world" for the church; therefore the "lightnings lighting up the world" mean the enlightening of the church by Divine truth. 8814. *And a heavy cloud upon the mountain.* That this signifies a Divine state in relation to those who were about to receive is evident from the meaning of a cloud, which is truth adapted to those who are about to receive—and thus the Word in its literal sense (see nos. 8443, 8781). Here it is Divine truth adapted to the people descended from Jacob. Because this people was in great darkness regarding Divine truths, it is called a heavy cloud; and above (verse 9) it is called the thickness of a cloud, and below (verse 18) the smoke of a fire like the smoke of a furnace. Just how great their darkness was may be seen from what was related above (no. 8788) concerning that nation, and also from the fact that the things belonging to the internal man were not revealed to them, but only those belonging to the external man. For they did not understand internal things, nor were they willing to understand them, because these were contrary to their loves—which were to be the most exalted of all peoples in the world and the wealthiest of all, while all other nations were as nothing compared to them. Where these loves exist, heavenly loves cannot be received, for they are extinguished and suffocated by worldly loves. This is the reason that Jehovah—that is, the Lord—appeared to them in a thick and heavy cloud. This is just like the situation in the other life, where those who are in falsities from evil are surrounded by a thick and black cloud according to the quality and extent of their falsities. This appears before everyone's eyes there, even though the Sun there is continually shining. On this subject, see what has been

8815. *And the voice of a trumpet exceeding loud.* That this signifies a celestial state which was surrounding them is evident from the meaning of the voice or sound of a trumpet as the truth of celestial good—a voice being truth and a trumpet being celestial good (as above, no. 8802). That it is a celestial state which was surrounding them is because the Divine in heaven is in the center or the inmost—that is, in the highest place there; but heaven, in respect to the angels, is surrounding or external—that is, beneath; for what is surrounding is also external, and what is external is also beneath. Divine truth itself in heaven is signified by voices of thunder and lightnings, but celestial or angelic truth joined to the Divine, which is beneath or surrounding, is signified by the voice of a trumpet—as in Zechariah: *Jehovah shall be seen over them, and His arrow shall go forth like lightning; and the Lord Jehovih shall blow the trumpet, and shall go in whirlwinds of the south* (9:14); and in David: *God has gone up with a shout, Jehovah with the voice of a trumpet* (Psalm 47:5)—where "shout" stands for the truth of spiritual good, and "the voice of a trumpet" for the truth of celes-
<margin>2</margin>tial good. The Divine truth which passes through heaven is also meant by the trumpets which the angels sounded, as recorded in the Apocalypse (8:2, 6-8, 12, 13; 9:14). Divine truth coming down out of heaven was also represented by the seven trumpets which the seven priests blew before the ark or before Jehovah, when the walls of the city of Jericho fell (Joshua 6); also by the trumpets which the three hundred men who were with Gideon blew around the camp of Midian, Amalek, and the sons of the East (Judges 7). 8816. *And all the people who were in the camp trembled.* That this signifies a holy tremor on the part of those who were about to receive, is evident from the meaning of trembling, as a holy tremor; and from the meaning of the people who were in the camp, as those who were about to receive Divine truth. Often in the Word it is said that when Jehovah comes down, the earth trembles and the mountains melt—as in the Psalms (77:17, 18; 97:4, 5; 104:32), and elsewhere; by which is signified the commotion of everyone at the presence of the Divine. For the Divine is such that no one can bear it unless he is in a sort of cloud, and is thus adapted for its reception; for it is like the fire in the sun, which, if it were to fall directly upon anyone, would consume him in a moment. But reception depends on the good within each person. Those who are in good indeed tremble at the presence of the Divine, but it is a holy tremor that precedes reception. But those who are in evil are in terror at the presence of the Divine and so flee away; they are then enveloped in their falsities, as in a dark cloud, and are lost to sight. These things are signified by the caves of the rocks into which they then retreat. From this it may be evident what is signified by: all the people who were in the camp trembled.

That smoke is the obscurity of truth and also the thick darkness belonging to falsity is evident in Isaiah (ix. 18, 19; xxxiv. 9, 10), in Joel (ii. 30, 31), in Hosea (xiii. 3), and in the Apocalypse (ix. 17, 18; xviii. 18; xix. 3).
