8827. *To the head of the mount.* That this signifies in the inmost—namely, heaven—is evident from the signification of a mountain as heaven (see just above, n. 8826). That the "head of the mount" is the inmost heaven is because the head of a mountain is the highest part, and "highest" signifies inmost (n. 1735, 2148, 4210, 4599, 8153). There are three heavens: the lowest, which is called the first; the middle, which is called the second; and the inmost, which is called the third. In this last heaven, the Divine is present more than in the two lower heavens, for in this heaven they are under the immediate view of the Lord, because those who are there are in love to the Lord and, consequently, in peace and innocence above the rest. The angels who are there are called celestial angels, and the angels in the second heaven are called spiritual angels. Therefore, the Divine in the one heaven is called the "Divine celestial" and the Divine in the other heaven is called the "Divine spiritual." From this, it may be evident what is signified by Jehovah coming down upon the top of the mountain.

8828. *And Jehovah called Moses to the head of the mount; and Moses went up.* That this signifies the conjunction of truth from the Divine there is evident from what was explained above (n. 8760, 8761), where similar words occur. 8829. *And Jehovah said unto Moses.* That this signifies exhortation from the Divine is evident from the signification of "saying," as involving the things that follow, which are matters of exhortation that they should not break through the bounds with which the mountain was enclosed.

### CONTINUATION CONCERNING THE SPIRITS AND THE INHABITANTS OF THE PLANET JUPITER.

8846. There are also spirits among those from the planet Jupiter whom they call chimney-sweepers, because they appear like them in dress and also in face. They are among those who rebuke the men of their planet and afterward instruct them (see n. 7801–7812). What these refer to in the Greatest Man, and what their quality is, can be seen from the description of them above (n. 5056), which may be transcribed here.

8847. A certain spirit from another planet was with me (he was from the planet Jupiter) who earnestly begged me to intercede for him so that he might be admitted into heaven. He said that he did not know he had done any evil, only that he had scolded the inhabitants of that planet; adding that after he had scolded them, he instructed them. He positioned himself at my left side under the elbow and spoke with a cracked voice; he could also move one to pity. But I could only reply that I could offer him no help, and that this belongs to the Lord alone. I also said that I could not intercede because I did not know whether it would be useful or not, but if he were worthy, he might have hope. He was then sent back among the upright spirits from his own planet; but they said that he could not stay in their company because he was not like them. Then, because he still urged to be let into heaven out of his intense desire, he was sent into a society of upright spirits from this earth; but

<summary>This page concludes the internal sense of Moses's descent to the people as a representation of Divine influx and begins a description of the "chimney-sweeper" spirits from Jupiter who rebuke and instruct.</summary>

# EXODUS.
<meta>[No. 8847.</meta>

They also said that he could not stay with them. He appeared black in the light of heaven, but he said that he was not black, but brown. I was told that those who are initially like this are later received among those who constitute the province of the seminal vesicles in the Greatest Man, or heaven. For in those vesicles, the semen is collected and enclosed by a suitable material designed to preserve its prolific principle from being lost, but of a kind that can be discarded in the neck of the uterus, so that what is preserved inside can serve for conception or the impregnation of the ovum. Consequently, that seminal matter has an urge, a sort of burning desire, to cast itself off and leave the semen to perform its function—similar to what was seen in that spirit. He came to me again, in humble garments, and said once more that he burned with desire to enter heaven, and that he now realized he was in a state where he could. I was then allowed to tell him that perhaps this was an indication that he would soon be received. Angels then told him to cast off his clothes; because of his intense desire, he did this so quickly that almost nothing could be faster. This represented the desires of those who are in the province to which the seminal vesicles correspond.

8848. I was told that when such spirits are prepared for heaven, they are stripped of their garments and clothed with new and shining raiment, and they become angels. They are compared to larvae which, after passing through their low state, change into chrysalises and then into butterflies. These are then given different coverings and wings of blue, yellow, silver, or gold. They are also given the freedom to fly in the open air as if in their heaven, and to enter into marriages, lay eggs, and thus provide for the continuation of their species; sweet and pleasant food is provided for them from the juices and scents of various flowers.

<summary>This text describes the transformation of a spirit from Jupiter, explaining how his desire to enter heaven corresponds to the function of the seminal vesicles. It also likens the spiritual preparation for heaven to the metamorphosis of a caterpillar into a butterfly.</summary>

# [No. 8851.] CHAPTER XIX. 73

8849. One of the spirits from that earth also came to me, saying that he was seeking the one and only Lord and that he desired to enter heaven, but that he could not because he had done evil during his life in the body. He was asked what evil he had done. He said that he had taken something of little value from a companion, which he had supposed his companion would have given him, and that this weighed heavily on his conscience. From this, it was evident what sort of life they live on that earth, and that it torments them if they do even the slightest evil to anyone; for he had brought that thought with him from his own earth.

8850. A bald head was shown to me, but only its upper part, which was bony; and I was told that such an appearance is seen by those who are to die within a year, and that they then prepare themselves. On that earth they do not fear death, except on account of leaving their spouse, their children, or their parents; for they know that they will live after death and that they do not pass out of life, because they go into heaven. For this reason, when they are dying, they do not call it dying, but "becoming heaven-made." Those who have lived happily in married love on that earth and have taken such care of their children as is fitting for parents, do not die from diseases, but peacefully, as if in sleep, and thus pass from the world into heaven.

8851. The age to which the inhabitants live is generally about thirty years, as measured by years on our earth. Those who live beyond this age are said to be unteachable, and therefore the spirits who punish and instruct do not come to them. The reason why they die within a short time is by the Lord’s providence, so that their numbers do not increase beyond what that earth is capable of supporting. They also reach maturity more quickly there than on this earth. Even in the first flower of youth, they unite in marriage, and then it is their delight to love their spouse and take

<summary>This page describes the inhabitants of another earth (Jupiter), highlighting their tender consciences regarding even minor slights, their peaceful view of death as a transition to heaven, and their short Earth-year lifespan which serves to prevent overpopulation.</summary>

<header>No. 8857.] CHAPTER XX. 75</header>

# CHAPTER TWENTIETH.

## THE DOCTRINE OF CHARITY.

8853. Every person has a <term>proprium</term> <note>one's own selfhood</note>, which they love above all things. This <term>proprium</term> is called the ruling love, or if you prefer, the universally reigning love within them. It is present in their thought and also in their will continually, and constitutes the very essence of their life.

8854. As for example, someone who loves wealth above all things, whether money or possessions, is continually occupied in his mind with how he may acquire it; he rejoices at his core when he acquires it; he grieves at his core when he loses it; his heart is in it. Someone who loves himself above all things is mindful of himself in everything, thinks of himself, speaks of himself, and acts for his own sake; for his life is the life of self.

8855. A person has as their end <note>purpose</note> what they love above all things, and they regard it in each and every thing. It is in their will like the hidden current of a river which draws and carries them away, even when they are doing something different, for it is what animates them. This is what one person searches out in another, and also perceives, and according to it either leads them or acts with them.

8856. When a person is being <term>regenerated</term>, <term>charity</term> is implanted by means of <term>faith</term>, even until it becomes the ruling motive; and when <term>charity</term> has come to be ruling, then they have new life, for then it is continually present in their thoughts and continually in their will—indeed, in all their particulars—even when they are meditating about other things and when they are engaged in business.

8857. The case is similar with love to the Lord. When

<summary>This text introduces the concept of the "ruling love" or proprium, explaining that whatever a person loves most becomes the dominant force in their thoughts and will. It describes how regeneration involves shifting this ruling motive toward charity and love to the Lord.</summary>

<keywords>proprium, ruling love, charity, faith, regeneration, self-love, spiritual life, will and thought</keywords>

--- Page 82 ---
<meta>This page continues the discussion on the nature of a person's ruling love and begins the exposition of the Ten Commandments in Chapter XX.</meta>

<page-num>76</page-num>
<header>EXODUS.</header>
<meta>[No. 8857.</meta>

when this love is ruling, it is present in every single detail of a person's life—just as with someone who loves their king or their parent, their love toward them shines forth in their presence from every feature of their face, it is heard in every expression of their speech, and is seen in their every gesture. This is what is meant by having God continually before one's eyes and by loving Him above all things, with all the soul and with all the heart.

8858. A person is entirely defined by the ruling love of their life; by this, they are distinguished from others; according to this, their heaven is formed if they are good, and their hell if they are evil; for it is their very will and thus the very <term>esse</term> <note>essential being</note> of their life, which cannot be changed after death. From these things it is clear what the quality of a regenerate person's life is and what the quality of an unregenerate person's life is.

### CONTENTS.

8859. In this chapter the subject in the internal sense is the Divine truths which are to be implanted in the good among those who belong to the Lord's spiritual church. The ten commandments of the decalogue are those truths. The commandments concerning sacrifices and concerning the altar, which follow in this chapter, are the external truths which belong to worship.

### INTERNAL SENSE.

8860. Verse 1. *And God spoke all these words, saying*, signifies Divine truths for those in the heavens and for those on the earths.

8861. *And God spoke all these words, saying*. That this signifies Divine truths for those in the heavens and for those on the earths, is evident from the signification of words which God spoke, as Divine truths, for the things which God speaks are nothing else than truths. Therefore also Divine truth is called the Word, and the Word is the

<page-num>79</page-num>
# CHAPTER XX. VER. 1.

Lord, according to John (1:1), for the reason that the Lord was the *Divine* truth itself when He was in the world, and afterward, when He was glorified, He became Divine good; and from then on, all Divine truth proceeds from Him. This Divine truth is light to the angels, and this light is also what illuminates our internal sight, which belongs to the understanding. This sight, since it does not see natural **2** but spiritual things, has for its objects in the spiritual understanding truths which are called truths of faith; but in the natural understanding, it has for its objects truths of civil order which relate to what is just, and also truths of moral order which relate to what is reputable, and lastly natural truths which are conclusions from the objects of the external senses, especially of sight. From this it may be seen in what order truths follow, and that each and every one has its origin from Divine truths, which are the internal beginnings of all things. Moreover, the forms in which they exist originated from there, for these were created to receive and contain. From this it may be evident what is meant in John by all things being created by the Word (1:1–3); for Divine truth is the very essential and is the only substance by which all things exist.

8862. By the words which God spoke are meant Divine truths for those in the heavens and those on the earth, for the reason that the Ten Commandments, which are called the Decalogue, and the subsequent statutes proclaimed and commanded from Mount Sinai, are such truths as are not only for those who are on the earth, but also for those who are in the heavens; for all the words—that is, all the truths which are from the Lord—are not only for men, but also at the same time for angels, since they pervade heaven and thus pass to earth. But in the heavens they are not audible as they are on the earth; for in the heavens they are in a spiritual form, but on the earth they are in a natural form. What the nature of those things which are in a spiritual form is, as compared with those which are in a natural form,

<summary>This page explains that Divine truth, originating from the Lord, serves as the spiritual light for both angels and human understanding, forming the basis for faith, civil justice, and moral repute. It also establishes that the Ten Commandments are Divine truths that exist in a spiritual form in heaven and a natural form on earth.</summary>

<meta>This page continues the explanation of the Word (Divine truth) as it exists in both spiritual and natural realms, specifically addressing the dual nature of the Ten Commandments.</meta>

<page-num>80</page-num>
# EXODUS.
<meta>[No. 8862.</meta>

It is evident from the details of the Word in the internal sense and in the external sense. Those in the internal sense are spiritual, but those in the external sense, which is the sense of the letter, are natural. The latter are accommodated to people on the <term>earths</term>, but the former to angels in
<margin>2</margin>the heavens. That it is so may be seen from this: that the Word has been sent and has thus passed from the Divine Itself through heaven to earth; and that when it comes to the earth, it is truth accommodated to the human race, which is in earthly and physical things, but in the heavens it is accommodated to angels, who are in spiritual and heavenly things. Because the Word is such, it is in itself holy, for it contains within itself the heavenly and the Divine. This may be clearly evident from the ten commandments of the <term>Decalogue</term>. Every one may know that those commandments are such as have been known everywhere on the earths—such as that parents are to be honored, that murder, adultery, and theft are not to be committed, and that no one should bear false witness; consequently, the <term>Israelite</term> nation might have known these laws from natural light alone; for what nation is there which does not know them? And yet for their proclamation, Jehovah Himself came down and proclaimed them out of fire which burned even to the heart of heaven. From this it may be evident that those commandments contain in their inner depths more things than appear in the letter, namely, such things as are at the same time for the heavens and fill the heavens. All things of the Word are such, because they are from the Divine. From this it is plain why the Word is holy, and what is meant by the Word’s being inspired as to every <term>jot and tittle</term> and as to every <term>little horn</term> (Matthew 5:18; Luke 16:17). What the commandments of the Decalogue are in the spiritual sense, that is, what they are in the heavens, will be seen in what follows.

# CHAPTER XX. VER. 2-7.
<meta>No. 8863.]</meta>

*...brought you out of the land of <term>Egypt</term>, out of the house of <term>slaves</term>. You shall have no other gods before My faces. You shall not make for yourself a carved image, nor any likeness of anything that is in the heavens above, or that which is in the earth beneath, or that which is in the waters under the earth: you shall not bow down yourself to them, nor serve them: for I, <term>Jehovah</term> your God, am a zealous God, punishing the iniquity of the fathers upon the sons, upon the third and upon the fourth generation of those who hate Me; and showing mercy to thousands of those who love Me and keep My commandments. You shall not take the name of <term>Jehovah</term> your God in vain; for <term>Jehovah</term> will not hold him guiltless who takes His name in vain.*

"I am <term>Jehovah</term> your God" signifies the Lord as to the <term>Divine Human</term> universally reigning in all things and in every detail of good and truth; "Who brought you out of the land of <term>Egypt</term>, out of the house of <term>slaves</term>" signifies liberation by Him from hell. "You shall have no other gods before My faces" signifies that truths must not be thought of from any other source than from the Lord. "You shall not make for yourself a carved image" signifies not from self-intelligence; "nor any likeness" signifies a semblance of those things which are from the Divine; "of that which is in the heavens above, or that which is in the earth beneath" signifies those things which are in spiritual light or those which are in natural light; "or that which is in the waters under the earth" signifies those things which are in the sensual and physical plane; "you shall not bow down to them, nor serve them" signifies that no Divine worship is to be paid them; "for I, <term>Jehovah</term> your God" signifies the Divine from the Lord in each and every thing; "am a zealous God" signifies that falsity and evil are from it; "punishing the iniquity of the fathers upon the sons" signifies the proliferation of falsity derived from evil as a result; "upon the third and upon the fourth generation" signifies in a long series and their conjunction.

tion; "of those who hate Me" signifies those who absolutely reject the Divine of the <term>Lord</term>; "and showing mercy to thousands" signifies good and <term>truth</term> to them forever; "of those who love Me" signifies those who receive the good of <term>love</term>; "and keep My commandments" signifies those who receive the truths of <term>faith</term>. 8864. *I am <term>Jehovah</term> your God.* That this signifies the <term>Lord</term> as to the <term>Divine Human</term> universally reigning in everything and in every detail of good and <term>truth</term>, is evident from this: that in the Word no other than the <term>Lord</term> is meant by <term>Jehovah</term> (see n. 1343, 1736, 2921, 3023, 3035, 3448, 5663, 6280, 6281, 6303, 8274), likewise by <term>Jehovah</term> Zebaoth, by the <term>Lord</term> Jehovih, and by <term>Jehovah</term> God (n. 2921, 3023, 3448, 6303); and that the <term>Lord</term> is called <term>Jehovah</term> from the Divine good, which is the <term>Divine Esse</term>, but is called God from the Divine <term>truth</term>, which is the <term>Divine Existere</term> (n. 6905, also n. 709, 732, 1096, 2586, 2769, 2807, 2822, 3921, 4402). That it is the <term>Divine Human</term> of the <term>Lord</term> which is here meant by <term>Jehovah</term> God is because the <term>Lord</term>, as to this Human, is meant in the Word both by <term>Jehovah</term> and by God—the Divine good, which He is even as to the Human, is meant by <term>Jehovah</term>, and the Divine <term>truth</term>, which He is because it proceeds from Him, is meant by God.

<margin>2</margin> That the <term>Divine Human</term> of the <term>Lord</term> is meant by <term>Jehovah</term> God is because the Divine itself, which is in the <term>Lord</term>, cannot be seen in heaven and cannot even be perceived, and thus cannot be received by <term>faith</term> and <term>love</term>, except through the <term>Divine Human</term>. That the Divine itself cannot be communicated to angels in heaven, and still less to men on earth, except through the <term>Divine Human</term>, is known in the churches from the words of the <term>Lord</term> in the Gospels, where He says that

<meta>This page continues the spiritual explanation of Jehovah God as the Lord in His Divine Human, emphasizing how this truth must be the primary and universal principle underlying all the commandments and statutes that follow.</meta>

<header>No. 8864.] CHAPTER XX. VER. 2–7.</header>

He is the door, He is the mediator, no one can come to the Father except through Him, no one knows the Father except Him, and no one has seen the Father, not even any form of Him. From this it is clear that it is the Lord who is here meant by Jehovah God. It is also known that it is He who redeemed the human race and liberated it from hell. This is signified by the words which follow — *Who brought you out of the land of Egypt, out of the house of bondage.*

From these things it is now clear that Jehovah God who spoke from Mount Sinai is the Lord as to the Divine Human. <margin>3</margin> The reason this is the first thing said by the Lord from Mount Sinai is that it ought to reign universally in everything that follows; for what is said first must be kept in mind in what follows and must be regarded as universal within it. What is meant by "universally reigning" shall be seen in what follows. All the things said by the Lord are of this nature: the things said first are to reign in the things which follow and involve them, and so on successively with the things that follow in series. The things which follow in this chapter are the commandments of the Decalogue, which are internal truths, and then the statutes, which are external truths. In both the internal and the external, the Lord must reign as to the Divine Human, for they are from Him and are Himself, since all truths that are truly truths proceed from Him, and the things which proceed from Him are Himself. It is also known in the churches that the Lord as to the Divine Human must reign in everything regarding faith, for it is taught there that without the Lord there is no salvation, and that all the truth and good of faith are from Him. Thus, because He is the source of faith, He is the faith within a person; and if He is the faith, He is also every truth contained in the doctrine of faith, which is from the Word. This is also why the Lord is called the Word.

<summary>This page explains that Jehovah God, who spoke at Sinai and delivered Israel from Egypt, is the Lord in His Divine Human. This foundational truth is meant to be the universal principle that governs and resides within all the commandments and statutes of the Word.</summary>

<meta>This page continues the explanation of section number 8864, discussing how the first principles mentioned by the Lord must universally reign in everything that follows in the series of the Decalogue and the statutes.</meta>

# EXODUS.
<meta>[No. 8864.</meta>

The series, as stated above, is evident from everything which the Lord spoke, especially from His prayer, which is called the Lord's Prayer. In that prayer, all things follow in such a sequence that they constitute, as it were, a column increasing from top to bottom; in the interiors of this column are the things which precede in the series. What is first in that prayer is inmost, and whatever follows in order adds itself to the inmost successively and thereby increases. What is inmost reigns universally in those things which surround it—that is, in all things and in each thing—for because of this, it is the essential of the existence of all.

8865. What is meant by "universally reigning" may be clear from what has been said and shown above (see numbers 8853–8858); namely, that what is universally reigning with a person is that which is in all and every part of his thought and in all and every part of his will, and consequently, what constitutes his very mind or life. The Lord must reign in this way with a person, for the Lord reigns in this way with the angels in heaven, of whom it is therefore said that they are in the Lord. The Lord comes to reign when it is not only believed that all good and all truth are from Him, but also when people love it to be so. Angels are not only in the faith, but also in the perception, that it is so. Hence it is that their life is the Lord's life in them; the life of their will is the life of love from the Lord, and the life of their understanding is the life of faith from the Lord. From these things it is plain how it is that the Lord is the all-in-all of heaven, and that He is heaven. When the Lord universally reigns with a person of the church, as He does with the angels of heaven, then the Lord is in all the truths and goods of faith with that person, just as the heart is in all the blood vessels, since these have their origin from the heart, and the blood is their life.

<margin>2</margin>
Furthermore, it should be known that the spirits and angels present with a person are of the same quality as that person's universal reigning love. The reason is that what universally reigns is the <term>esse</term> of everyone's life (numbers 8853–8858).

<summary>This page defines "universally reigning" as a primary principle that permeates every aspect of a person's thought and will, using the Lord's Prayer as an example of this hierarchical structure. It explains that for the Lord to reign in a person as He does in heaven, He must be the source of all their perceived good and truth, acting as the heart and life of their faith.</summary>

--- Page 91 ---
<meta>This page continues the discussion on the "universally reigning" love, explaining how it is the source of human contentment and joy through the influence of angels, and begins the exposition on Exodus 20 regarding liberation from hell and the command to have no other gods.</meta>

-># No. 8867.] CHAPTER XX. VER. 2-7.<-

All the cheerfulness and all the contentment which a person has, even when he is thinking about other things, comes from that source; for within it, the angels and spirits who are with him dwell and, as it were, have their home, and their joy flows into the person and causes his cheerfulness and contentment. That such is its source does not come to the perception of the person because he does not know that his life flows in, nor that the universally reigning [love] constitutes his life, nor that when this reigning love of his life is touched, it is as if the pupil of the eye is touched—with delight when by beautiful objects, and with pain when by ugly objects. It is called universal because of this: that it is every single thing in the whole, and thus that which universally reigns is what is in all things and in each individual part (see n. 1919, 5949, 6159, 6338, 6482, 6483, 6571, 7648, 8067).

8866. *Who brought you out of the land of Egypt, out of the house of bondmen.* That this signifies liberation by Him from hell is evident from the meaning of bringing out as liberation; from the meaning of the land of Egypt as infestations from infernal spirits (see n. 7240, 7278); and from the meaning of the house of bondmen as spiritual captivity (n. 8049). The reason the house of bondmen is spiritual captivity and also hell is because it is bondage to be held captive and to be led by those who are in hell, while it is freedom to be led by the Lord (n. 892, 905, 2870–2875, 2882, 2884, 2892, 2893, 6205, 8209). That those who belong to the spiritual church and are represented by the sons of Israel were liberated from hell by the Lord—by His coming into the world and making the Human in Himself Divine—may be seen above (n. 6854, 6914, 7035, 7828, 7932, 8018, 8321).

8867. *You shall have no other gods before My face.* That this signifies that truths must not be thought of from any source other than from the Lord is evident from the meaning of gods as truths, and in the opposite sense as falsities (see n. 4402, 4544, 7268, 7873, 8301); and

--- Page 92 ---
<meta>This page continues the exposition of Exodus 20, specifically explaining the command to have "no other gods before My face" and defining what it means for truths to originate from the Lord versus other sources.</meta>

<page-num>86</page-num>
<header>EXODUS. [No. 8867.</header>

From the meaning of faces, when applied to God—such as love, mercy, peace, and goodness (n. 222, 223, 2434, 5585)—it follows that "faces" signifies the Lord Himself, for it is from the Lord that these qualities originate. The reason that "no other gods before My faces" signifies that truths must not be thought of from any source other than from the Lord is also because the Divine Human of the Lord, which is signified by *I am Jehovah your God*, is mentioned first. It therefore holds the primary position in order and must universally reign in every single truth that follows (n. 8864, 8865). For this reason, we now perceive those things that must be avoided, because they would destroy and prevent the universal reign of the Lord in each and every truth contained in the commandments and statutes that were dictated and commanded from Mount Sinai. The first thing that would destroy this reign is the thinking of truths from any source other than the Lord; this is signified by the command to have no other gods before the Lord's face. The remaining things that would destroy [the Lord's] universal reign are contained in the points that follow in order: namely, that they should not make for themselves a carved image, nor the likeness of anything that is in the heavens, on the earth, or in the waters, and that they should not bow down to them or serve them. After this, it therefore follows again, "*for I am Jehovah your God*," which signifies that the Lord must be in all things and in every individual thing.

8868. It must also be briefly explained what is meant by truths that come from a source other than the Lord. In general, these are truths in which the Lord is not present. The Lord is not in a person's truths when that person denies Him and His Divinity, and even when he acknowledges Him but still believes that he has goodness and truth from himself rather than from Him, and therefore claims righteousness as his own. Truths in which the Lord is not present are also those taken from the Word, especially from its literal sense, and explained in favor of self-dominion.

<summary>This section explains that "having no other gods" means recognizing the Lord as the sole source of all truth and goodness, ensuring His "universal reign" over all thoughts and commandments. Truths are considered to be from a "different source" when a person denies the Lord’s divinity, claims credit for their own righteousness, or uses the Word to seek power over others.</summary>

<meta>This page continues the discussion of truths originating from the Lord versus those from other sources, focusing on the internal essence of truth and how it is perceived in the spiritual world.</meta>

<page-num>87</page-num>
<header>No. 8868.] CHAPTER XX. VER. 2-7.</header>

and self-gain. These in themselves are truths because they are from the Word, but they are not truths because they are misinterpreted and thus perverted. They are the kind meant by the Lord by these words in Matthew: *If anyone says, Look, here is the Christ, or, There; do not believe it. For false Christs and false prophets shall arise and shall show great signs and wonders, so as to lead astray, if possible, even the elect* (xxiv. 23-26; see n. 3900). And in Luke: *Take heed that you are not led astray: for many shall come in My name, saying, I am he; and, The time is at hand; do not go therefore after them* (xxi. 8).

<margin>2</margin> Truths which are from the Lord are, in their internal form, still truths from the Lord. And truths which are not from the Lord only appear to be truths in their external form, but not in their internal form; for within they are either empty, or false, or evil. For truth to be truth, it must have life in it; for truth without life is not the truth of faith in man, and life is from no other source than from good—that is, through good from the Lord. If therefore the Lord is not in truth, it is truth without life, thus not truth; but if falsity or evil is in it, the truth itself in man is falsity or evil. For what is within constitutes the essence, and also in the other life shines through the external. From these things it may now be evident how it is to be understood that truths must not be thought of from any other source than from the Lord.

<margin>3</margin> Since few know how it is with truths which in their internal form are truths—that is, which live from the Lord—something shall be said concerning these from experience. In the other life, it is clearly perceived from everyone who speaks there what lies hidden within the words of his speech, such as whether it is closed within or whether it is open, also what kind of affection there is therein. If an affection for good is within, it is inwardly soft; if an affection for evil, it is inwardly hard, and so on. With the angels of heaven, all things of their speech are open even to the Lord, and this is both clearly per-

<summary>This page explains that truths only possess genuine life and essence when they originate from the Lord through good; without this internal life, truths are merely external appearances that may harbor falsity. <meta>[No. 8868</meta>

perceived and is also heard from its softness and quality. From this it is also known what lies hidden within truths—whether the Lord is present or not. Truths in which the Lord is present are truths that are alive, but truths in which the Lord is not present are truths that are not alive. Those which are alive are truths of faith from love for the Lord and from charity toward the neighbor. Those which are not alive are not truths, because inwardly they contain self-love and the love of the world. Spirits and angels in the other life may be discerned in this way, for everyone has truths according to his life, that is, according to what universally reigns within him.

8869. *You shall not make for yourself a graven image.* That this signifies not acting from self-intelligence is evident from the meaning of a graven image, which is that which is not from the Lord, but from the <term>proprium</term> of man. What is from the intellectual <term>proprium</term> is signified by a graven image, and what is from the voluntary <term>proprium</term> is signified by a molten image. To have either one or the other as a god, or to worship it, is to love everything that proceeds from the self above all things. Those who do this do not believe at all that any intelligence or wisdom flows in from the Divine, for they attribute all things to themselves; other things that happen to them they attribute to fortune or chance. They totally deny Divine providence in such matters. They suppose that if there is anything of the Deity present, it is within the order of nature, to which they ascribe all things. They say indeed with their mouths that some Creator God has impressed these things upon nature, but in their hearts they still deny any God above nature. Such are those who from the heart attribute everything to themselves and to their own prudence and intelligence, and nothing to the Divine; and those among them who love themselves worship what belongs to themselves and also desire to be worshipped by others—even as gods—and would openly desire it if the church did not forbid it.

<summary>This page explains that truths are only truly "alive" when they contain love for the Lord and the neighbor, rather than self-love. It also interprets the prohibition against graven images as a warning against relying on self-intelligence and attributing wisdom to oneself rather than the Divine.</summary>

<margin>3</margin>

Here the graven image stands for those things which are produced from self-intelligence, in which there is nothing of life from the Lord.

Semblances of those things which are from the Divine are made by men when they speak of Divine things with their mouths and also in action do such things as are commanded by the Divine, and thus create a belief that they are in good and truth, when yet at heart they think altogether otherwise and will nothing but evil: such are dissemblers, hypocrites, and the deceitful, and these are they who make semblances of those things which are from the Divine. In the other life, evil spirits make semblances of those things which are from the Divine when they put a likeness and appearance upon externals in which, internally, there is nothing of the Divine. Dissemblers, hypocrites, and the deceitful learn to do this in the other life, and in general all who from frequent use have contracted a habit of speaking otherwise than they think and of doing otherwise than they will. Some, by such practices, are desirous to acquire a reputation so that they may seem good and thereby deceive; some so that they may acquire authority.
<margin>3</margin>Moreover, such things there are abuses of correspondences. But the externals are gradually taken away from them, through which they have made a pretense of such things as belong to charity and faith, so that they come to act from their own nature acquired in the world, and no longer from any pretense or hypocrisy. 8871. *Of that which is in the heavens above, or that which is in the earth beneath.* That this signifies those things which are in spiritual light or those which are in natural light is evident from the meaning of a likeness of those things which are in the heavens above. It is also evident from the meaning of a likeness of those things which are in the earth beneath, which are those things that appear and are seen in natural light, such as refer to civil and moral good and truth—to feign and pretend these things is to make a likeness of those things which are in the earth beneath. In the <term>sense of the letter</term>, those things that appear in heaven are meant, such as the sun, the moon, and the stars; and such things as appear on the earth, such as animals of various kinds—flying, walking, and creeping. But in the <term>internal sense</term>, the things signified by these are meant, all of which refer to good and truth, as was said above. In the first degree in man are those things which are in spiritual light, which are signified by the things in the heavens above. In the second degree are those which are in natural light, which are signified by the things in the earth beneath. And in the third degree are those which are in the sensual corporeal plane, which are signified by the things in the waters under the earth. What the sensual corporeal is and what its quality is may be seen above (n. 5084, 5089, 5094, 5125, 5128, 5580, 5767, 6183, 6201, 6310—6316, 6318, 6598, 6612, 6614, 6622, 6624, 6844, 6845, 6948, 6949, 7442, 7693). To the sensual corporeal belong matters of knowledge which arise most immediately from the experience of the external senses and their enjoyments. These pieces of knowledge and their enjoyments are good with those who are good because they are applied to good ends, but with those who are evil they are evil because they are applied to evil ends. To deceive by means of these, as is done by dissemblers, hypocrites, and the deceitful, is to make a likeness of such things as are in the waters under the earth.

**8873.** *You shall not bow down yourself to them, nor serve them.* That this signifies that no Divine worship is to be paid to them is evident from the signification of bowing down oneself as humiliation (see n. 2153, 5682, 6266, 7068); and from the signification of serving as submission (n. 5164). That it means Divine worship is because humiliation and submission are the essentials of worship, for worship without them is not worship, but a gesture in imitation of those who are in the truth of worship; in such a gesture there is nothing of life, for life from the Lord flows only into a humble and submissive heart, since such a heart is adapted to receive it. The reason for this is that when the heart is truly humble, nothing of self-love and of love of

<meta>This page continues the scriptural examination of the "zeal of Jehovah," providing various biblical references to show that while God's zeal is essentially love and mercy, it is perceived as anger and destruction by those who are in a state of evil.</meta>

# CHAPTER XX. VER. 2 — 7.

*to rest upon them, I will relent; that they may know that I, Jehovah, have spoken in My zeal, when I have accomplished My fury upon them* (v. 13). And in Zechariah: *The angel of Jehovah with me said to me, "Cry out, saying, Thus says Jehovah of hosts: I have been zealous for Jerusalem and for Zion with a great zeal. For I am very deeply displeased with the nations that are at ease"* (i. 14, 15; viii. 2). And in Zephaniah: *I will pour upon them My indignation, all the fury of My anger; for all the earth shall be devoured with the fire of My zeal* (iii. 8). In Moses: *Jehovah will not be pleased to pardon him, but then the anger of Jehovah and His zeal shall smoke against that man, and all the curse . . . shall lie upon him* (Deut. xxix. 20). And in David: *How long, O Jehovah, will You be angry forever? Shall Your zeal burn like a fire? Pour out Your anger upon the nations that do not know You* (Ps. lxxix. 5, 6). In like manner, the zeal of Jehovah is described as anger in other passages (Ps. xxxviii. 1; Ezek. xvi. 42; xxiii. 25; xxxviii. 19). From these examples it may be evident what is meant by the zeal of Jehovah, and what by a zealous God—namely, that in the genuine sense, love and mercy are meant, but in a sense that is not genuine, such as is seen by those who are in evils and falsities, anger and vastation are signified. It should be known that Jehovah, that is, the Lord, is especially called zealous or an avenger when that which ought to reign universally in the person of the church—namely, the Divine—is brought to nothing; for this is to be loved, regarded, or feared above all things. When this is corrupted or destroyed, then instead of heavenly light, mere thick darkness takes its place; for there is no longer any inflow of this light from the Divine, because there is no reception. For this reason it is said, *I am Jehovah your God, a zealous God, visiting the iniquity of the fathers upon the sons, upon the third and upon the fourth generation of those who hate Me*, and this occurs in case they worship other

<summary>This page concludes the scriptural evidence that the "zeal" of God represents His intense love and mercy, which only appears as anger or punishment to those who reject the Divine. It explains that when the Divine is no longer the primary focus of a person's life, spiritual darkness replaces heavenly light.</summary>

<keywords>zeal of Jehovah, zealous God, divine love, mercy, vastation, divine inflow, iniquity of the fathers, spiritual light and darkness</keywords>

gods or made for themselves a graven image or likenesses; for these things destroy the Divine which ought universally to reign. Therefore it is stated in a similar way in other passages in Moses — *Take heed . . . lest you make for yourselves a graven image of any form, for Jehovah God is a devouring fire, a zealous God* (Deut. iv. 23, 24); and again, *You shall worship no other God: for Jehovah, whose name is Zealous, He is zealous* (Exod. xxxiv. 14). This was so severely prohibited in the case of the Israelite nation because the worship of other gods, of graven images and likenesses, was destructive of every representative of the church among them; for in heaven Jehovah, that is, the Lord, universally reigns, His Divine fills all things and makes the life of all. If anything were worshipped instead of the Divine, every representative would have perished, and thus all communication with heaven.

8876. *Visiting the iniquity of the fathers upon the sons.* That this signifies the proliferation of falsity derived from evil in consequence, is evident from the signification of "visiting the iniquity of the fathers," as the proliferation of evils—that "visiting" means proliferation is because the state of those who entirely reject the Divine from themselves is described here, who therefore are no longer receptive of good but of evil, and this continually, since evil with such people is continually increasing, and this is proliferation—that "father" stands for good, and in the opposite sense evil, may be seen above (n. 3703, 5902, 6050, 7833, 7834); and from the signification of "sons," as truths (n. 1147, 2623, 3373), therefore in the opposite sense, falsities. In the sense nearest the letter, by "visiting the iniquity of the fathers upon the sons" is not meant that the sons suffer the punishment of the iniquity of their fathers, for this is contrary to the Divine (Deut. xxiv. 16), but that the evil of the fathers increases, and thus is passed down into the children by heredity, and that thereby there is a successive accumulation of evil (n. 2910, 3701, 4317,

<summary>This page explains that graven images were forbidden because they destroy the spiritual representation of the Divine and sever communication with heaven. It further clarifies that "visiting the iniquity of the fathers upon the sons" refers to the hereditary transmission and increase of evil and falsity, rather than the literal punishment of children for their parents' sins.</summary>

8550, 8551). But in the spiritual sense, by "fathers" are not meant fathers, but evils; nor by "sons" are meant sons, but falsities. Therefore, by the above words is signified the continual proliferation of falsity derived from evil.

8877. *Upon the third and upon the fourth generation.* That this signifies in a long series and their conjunction, is evident from the signification of "three," as what is full from beginning to end (see n. 2788, 4495, 7715), and thus what is in a long series; therefore, "sons of the third generation" are falsities in a long series—that "sons" are falsities may be seen just above (n. 8876). It is also evident from the signification of "four," as conjunction, in the same way as "two" (n. 1686, 5194, 8423); therefore, "sons of the fourth generation" are falsities conjoined in a long series. That this is the signification of "sons of the third and fourth generation" must necessarily appear as a paradox or foreign to the sense of the Word; but it should be known that numbers do not signify numbers in the internal sense, but things (n. 482, 487, 575, 647, 648, 755, 813, 1963, 1988, 2075, 2252, 3252, 4264, 4495, 4670, 5265, 6175).

8878. *Of them that hate Me.* That this signifies those who absolutely reject the Divine of the Lord, is evident from the signification of "those who hate God," as those who are in evil and consequently in falsity; for these are the ones who reject the Divine of the Lord, and so far as they are in evil and consequently in falsity, they not only reject it, but also hold it in hatred. That it is the Divine of the Lord which they reject is because those who are in evil are not in heavenly light, but in natural light, and eventually in sensual light which is from the corporeal. From this light they cannot see the Human of the Lord as anything other than human, for they cannot perceive what the <term>Divine Human</term> is, because they have an idea concerning the Divine that is altogether vacuous and empty. If they are told that the Divine Itself is Divine love, and that the Divine love is the <term>esse</term> of all life, and that the Lord from conception was that love,

8879. *And showing mercy unto thousands.* That this signifies good and truth to them forever is evident from the meaning of mercy as the influx of good and truth from the Lord and consequently spiritual life, which is given by means of regeneration (see n. 6180, 6307); for by the Lord, out of mercy, are given such things as belong to eternal life and happiness; and from the meaning of "a thousand" as "very much," and when it is said of the Divine mercy, as "forever" (n. 2575, 8715).

<meta>This page continues the explanation of how truths of faith become living when they move from the memory into the will, specifically focusing on the commandment against taking the Lord's name in vain.</meta>

<page-num>103</page-num>
<header>No. 8882.] CHAPTER XX. VER. 2—7.</header>

them and therefore does not do them, they do not become living. But when they are brought forth from the memory and are introduced through the intellectual part into the will—that is, by thought into affection and from there into action—they then become living and are truths of faith. This is achieved by the Lord when a person desists from evils—as was said just above (n. 8880).

8882. *You shall not take the name of [Jehovah] your God in vain.* That this signifies the profanations and blasphemies of the truth and good of faith is evident from the meaning of the name of God, as the whole complex by which the Lord is worshiped, thus all the truth and good of faith (see n. 2724, 3006, 6674); and from the meaning of taking in vain, as profaning and blaspheming. By taking the name of God in vain is specifically signified turning truth into evil—that is, believing that it is truth and yet still living in evil; and it is also turning good into falsity—that is, living holily and yet not believing. Each is profanation (n. 4601), for believing belongs to the understanding and living belongs to the will; and therefore, in those who believe differently than they live, the thought and will are divided. But because the will continually flows into the understanding—for the understanding is the form of the will, meaning the will manifests itself there in light—it follows that when a person believes one way and lives another, truth and evil, or good and falsity, are joined together. Thus, what belongs to heaven in a person is joined with what belongs to hell. This conjunction cannot be dissolved and the person be made healthy again, except by a pulling apart that carries away with it everything of spiritual life. For this reason, such people are cast into a hell the most grievous of all, where they are dreadfully tormented. This is what is meant by the words of the Lord in Matthew: *Every sin and blasphemy shall be*

Divine Human of the Lord, in the internal sense the conjunction of the Divine Human with the heavens, thus heaven, and so the marriage of good and truth there; "to keep it holy" signifies no profanation in any manner. 8885. *Remember.* That this signifies what is perpetual in the thought is evident from the meaning of *remembering*, when said of such a thing as must not in any way be forgotten, as what is perpetual in the thought. That is perpetual in the thought which universally reigns there; and that universally reigns with a person which is perpetually in their thought, even when they are meditating on other things or are engaged in business affairs. The thought of man involves many things together, for it is the form of many things which have entered successively. Those things which come to manifest perception are at the time in the center and thus in the light of the internal sight, while all other things are then at the sides round about. Those

<meta>This page continues the explanation of the internal sense of Exodus 20:8–11, specifically detailing how "remembering" the Sabbath relates to what "universally reigns" in a person's thought and will.</meta>

which are in the circumference are in obscurity, and are not manifested except when such objects occur as they are connected with. But the things which are still more remote and are not on the same plane but tend downward, are such as the person has rejected and holds in aversion. Such things are evils and falsities with the good, and goods and truths with the evil.

<margin>2</margin> In the thought itself of man are those things which are perpetually there—that is, which universally reign there—which are his inmost parts. From these, man regards those things which are not perpetually there (that is, those things which do not yet universally reign) as outside of himself, and also as beneath himself, and as not yet in affinity. From these, he can then choose and join to himself those things which agree with the inmost; from the joining and final association of these, the inmost things—those which universally reign—are strengthened. This is accomplished by new truths with the good, and by new falsities, or by wrong applications of truths, with the evil.

<margin>3</margin> It is further to be known that what universally reigns is what has been introduced into the will itself; for the will itself is the inmost of man, because it is formed from his love. For whatever a man loves, this he wills, and what he loves above all things, this he inmostly wills. But the understanding serves to manifest before others the things which man wills—that is, which he loves—and serves also to bend the wills of others toward compliance by ideas formed in various ways. When this is the case, love or affection flows from the will into the intellectual ideas also, and by a certain kind of inspiration, gives them life and moves them. These intellectual ideas with the good make one with affections which are of the will; but it is otherwise with the evil. For with these, the thought and the will inmostly indeed agree, since the understanding thinks the evil which the will desires as a falsity agreeing with evil; but this agreement does not appear before men in the world, for from childhood the evil learn to speak one

8886. *The sabbath day.* That this signifies in the highest sense the union of the Divine itself and the Divine Human of the Lord; in the internal sense the conjunction of the Divine Human with the heavens, and thus heaven, and so the marriage of good and truth there, is evident from what was shown before (n. 8495). Because such things are signified by the sabbath, it was therefore most holy in the representative church, and something that was to be perpetually thought of—that is, something that was to reign universally. That this constitutes the life of man can be seen above (n. 8853–8858, 8885). The Israelitish nation indeed did not think about the union of the Divine and the Divine Human of the Lord, nor about His union with heaven, nor about the conjunction of good and truth in heaven, which were the things signified by the sabbath, because they were entirely in externals without an internal. But they were commanded to keep the sabbath most holy so that those Divine and heavenly things might be represented in heaven. How it was with them is plain from what was shown above concerning that nation and concerning the representation of a church among them (n. 3147, 3479, 3480, 3881, 4208, 4281, 4288, 4289, 4293, 4307, 4444, 4580, 4680, 4825, 4844, 4847, 4899, 4912, 6304, 6306, 7048, 7051, 8301).

8888. *Six days you shall labor, and do all your work.* That this signifies the combat which precedes and prepares for that marriage is evident from the signification of *six days* as states of combat—that *six* stands for combat may be seen above (n. 720, 737, 900), and *days* for states (n. 23, 487, 488, 493, 2788, 4850, 5672, 5962, 6110, 8426); and from the signification of *laboring and doing one’s work* as doing those things which are necessary to life—here those that belong to spiritual life, that is, life in heaven.

Therefore also it is that the seventh day signifies a holy state. That "day" means state may be seen above (n. 8888), and that "seven" signifies what is holy (n. 395, 433, 716, 881, 5265).

By *beast* is signified affection in general, and by *stranger within the gates* factual knowledge in general; thus, each and every thing is included. 

The reason *son* represents the intellectual part is because *son* signifies truth, for truths constitute the intellect; and *daughter* represents the voluntary part because *daughter* signifies good, for various forms of good constitute the will. That *son* is truth, and thus the intellectual part, may be seen above (n. 489, 491, 533, 1147, 2623, 2803, 2813, 3373, 3704, 4227); also that *daughter* means good, and thus the voluntary part (n. 489-491, 2362, 3024). Therefore, *stranger within the gates* stands for factual knowledge in general, for factual knowledge is "at the gates"—that is, at the entrance to the truths which belong to the church.

But who cannot see, if he ponders on the details of the description, that the creation of the universe is not meant there; for such things are described there as may be known from common sense not to have been so—as that there were days before the sun and the moon, and that there was light and darkness, and that herbage and trees sprang up; and yet that the light was furnished by those luminaries, and distinction was made between the <margin>3</margin> light and darkness and thus days were made. In what follows in the history there are also similar things, which are scarcely acknowledged by anyone who thinks interiorly to be possible—as that the woman was made from the rib of the man; also that two trees were set in paradise, the fruit of one of which it was forbidden to eat; and that a serpent from one of them spoke with the wife of the man, who had been the wisest of mortal creatures, and by his speech, from the mouth of the serpent, deceived them

...both; and that the whole human race, even to so many thousands of thousands, was consequently condemned to hell. These and other such things in that history must, at first thought, appear paradoxical to those who entertain any doubt concerning the sanctity of the Word, and afterward lead them to deny the Divine within it. Nevertheless, it should be known that each and every thing in that history, even to the smallest iota, is Divine and contains within it <term>arcana</term> which are as plain as clear daylight to the angels in the heavens. The reason for this is that the angels do not see the meaning of the Word according to the letter, but according to what is within—namely, what is spiritual and celestial, and within these, the Divine. Angels, when the first chapter of Genesis is read, do not perceive any other creation than the new creation of man, which is called <term>regeneration</term>. This regeneration is described in that history: by paradise is meant the wisdom of the man created anew; by the two trees in the midst of it are meant the two faculties of that man—namely, the will for good, represented by the tree of life, and the understanding of truth, represented by the tree of knowledge. And the reason it was forbidden to eat of this latter tree was because the man who is regenerated or created anew must no longer be led by the understanding of truth, but by the will for good; otherwise, the new life within him perishes (see n. 202, 337, 2454, 2715, 3246, 3652, 4448, 5895, 5897, 7877, 7923, 7992, 8505, 8506, 8510, 8512, 8516, 8539, 8643, 8648, 8658, 8690, 8701, 8722). Consequently, by Adam (or man) and by Eve his wife, a new church was meant; and by the eating from the tree of knowledge, the fall of that church from good to truth was meant—consequently, from love for the Lord and toward the neighbor to faith without those loves. This happened through reasoning from their own intellect, which reasoning is the serpent, as may be seen above (n. 195—197, 6398, 6399, 6949, 7293). ...a history framed to contain heavenly and Divine things within it, according to the accepted manner in the Ancient Churches. This manner of writing also extended from there to many outside that Church, who similarly devised histories and included <term>arcana</term> within them, as is clear from the most ancient writers. In the Ancient Churches, it was known what things in the world signified in heaven, and historical events were not as important to them as describing heavenly things. These heavenly things occupied their minds because they thought more interiorly than people do today; thus, they had communication with angels and found enjoyment in connecting these things. However, they were led by the Lord regarding what should be held sacred in the churches; therefore, the things they wove together were in
<margin>5</margin> full correspondence. From this, it is evident what is meant by "heaven and earth" in the first verse of the first chapter of Genesis: namely, the internal and external church. That these are signified by "heaven and earth" is also evident from passages in the prophets where mention is made of a "new heaven and a new earth," by which a new church is meant—as may be seen above (n. 82, 1411, 1733, 1850, 2117, 2118, 3355, 4535). From all this, it is now clear that by the phrase, *In six days Jehovah made heaven and earth and the sea*, is signified the <term>regeneration</term> and <term>vivification</term> of those things in the internal and in the external man.

Therefore, at that time, the Lord has labor, for He fights for man against the hells which assault. But as soon as the good of love has been implanted, the combat ceases and rest follows, for he is then introduced into heaven and is led by the Lord according to the laws of order there, and thus in peace. These things are signified by the rest of Jehovah on the seventh day.

8897. *Honor your father and your mother.* That this signifies love for good and truth—in the supreme sense for the Lord and for His kingdom—is evident from the meaning of honoring as loving, because honor in the spiritual sense is love. This is because in heaven people love one another, and when someone loves, they also honor, for in honor there is love. Honor without love is refused in heaven—indeed, it is rejected—because it is without life from good. This is also evident from the meaning of father as good (see n. 3703, 5581, 5902, 6050, 7833, 7834), and in the supreme sense the Lord as to Divine good (n. 15, 1729, 2005, 2803, 3704, 7499, 8328). The reason the Lord in the supreme sense is the father is because He gives new life to man, and thereby man becomes a son and heir of His kingdom. It is also evident from the meaning of mother as truth (n. 3703, 5581), and in the supreme sense the Lord as to Divine truth, and thus His kingdom, since the Divine truth which proceeds from the Lord makes heaven. That the Divine truth of the Lord makes heaven is because the Lord, as to Divine good, is the Sun in the other life, and as to Divine truth, He is the light. This Divine light from the Lord as the Sun is what illuminates angelic minds, fills them with intelligence and wisdom, and makes them angels of light. Divine good is within Divine truth, just as heat from the sun is within the light during the spring and summer in the world.

That such things are signified by the commandment concerning honoring parents may seem strange, because the meaning is remote from the literal sense. But it should be known that the commandments of the Decalogue are rules of life both for those who are in the world and for those who are in heaven—the literal sense or the external sense for those who are in the world, and the spiritual or internal sense for those who are in heaven—and therefore both senses, external as well as internal, are for those who, while they are in the world, are also in heaven; that is, for those who are in the good of life according to the truths of doctrine. That the commandments of the Decalogue are also for those who are in heaven is plain from the internal sense of all things in the Word, and clearly from this: that the things which Jehovah God—that is, the Lord—Himself speaks, are not only for people or for the world, but also for

<meta>This page continues the explanation of how the Ten Commandments apply to both the natural world and the spiritual world (heaven), specifically focusing on the fourth commandment regarding honoring parents.</meta>

angels, and indeed for the whole of heaven, since the Divine truth proceeding from the Lord flows through heaven and reaches even to man. This is the case with these ten commandments, which the Lord Himself spoke from <margin>2</margin> Mount Sinai. Since these commandments were spoken not only for those who are in the world, but also for those who are in heaven, they could not be perceived in the same way by both—take, for example, this commandment, that they should honor father and mother so that their days might be long upon the land which Jehovah God would give them; for in heaven, parents and children do not come together in the same way they do on earth. Therefore, for father there they have the Lord, and for mother His kingdom. Nor can it be said of those in heaven that their days would be "long," because they live there for eternity; nor can "land" be understood there, as in this commandment, to mean the land of Canaan, but instead the heavenly Canaan or heaven. Since father and mother signify the Lord and His kingdom, this commandment is the fourth in order and exceeds in holiness those that follow. The commands concerning the worship of Jehovah—that is, the Lord—are first and second because they are the most holy. Next is the command concerning the Sabbath because, in its highest sense, it signifies the union of the Divine itself and the Divine Human in the Lord. After this follows the command concerning honoring parents because it signifies love for the Lord and, consequently, the love of what is good and true proceeding from the Lord. Because these things are signified by this commandment, contempt for parents is listed among the crimes signified by shedding blood (Ezek. 22:6, 7), and for this reason, stubborn and rebellious sons were stoned (Deut. 21:18-21).

8901. Verse 13. *Thou shalt not kill* signifies not taking away spiritual life from anyone, also not extinguishing faith and charity, and likewise not holding the neighbor in hatred.

8902. *Thou shalt not kill.* That this signifies not taking away spiritual life from anyone, also not extinguishing faith and charity, and likewise not holding the neighbor in hatred, is evident from the meaning of "killing" as depriving someone of spiritual life. That killing means this in the internal sense is because that sense deals with spiritual life, or the life of heaven within man; and spiritual life, or the life of heaven within man, is the life of faith and of charity. Therefore, by "not to kill" is also signified not extinguishing faith and charity in anyone. The reason why "not to kill" in the internal sense also means not to hold the neighbor in hatred is that he who holds someone in hatred continually desires to kill him, and indeed would actually kill him if not prevented by the fear of punishment, the loss of life, of reputation, and the like. For hatred is from evil, the opposite of charity, and desires nothing but the murder of him whom it hates—in the world the murder of his body, and in the other life the murder of his soul. Lord in Matthew: *You have heard that it was said to those of old time, "You shall not kill; and whoever kills shall be in danger of the judgment": but I say to you, that whoever is angry with his brother without cause shall be in danger of the judgment; and whoever says to his brother, "Raca," shall be in danger of the council; and whoever says, "You fool," shall be in danger of the hell of fire* (v. 21, 22). Hatred against the neighbor is meant by being angry with the brother without cause, and the degrees of its increase are described by saying to him, "Raca," and by calling him a fool; that anger is a turning away from charity, and is of evil—thus that it is hatred—may be seen above (n. 357, 4164, 5034, 5798, 5887, 5888).

<margin>2</margin> That to kill is, in the internal sense, to take away spiritual life from anyone—consequently to extinguish faith and charity—is evident from almost all the passages in the Word where killing or slaying is mentioned—as in Isaiah: *Behold, the day of Jehovah comes, cruel and with indignation and the heat of anger; to make the earth a desolation; and He shall destroy its sinners out of it. Nor is it to be believed that the Israelite nation was so stupid as to be the only ones ignorant of what all other nations on the globe knew. In this, the Word differs from, is separated from, and is preeminent over all other writing.

And hardly anyone knows that there is a correspondence between the two, and indeed of such a nature that the image of the one is presented in the other, or that the spiritual is represented in the natural; consequently, the spiritual is like a soul and the natural is like its body, and thus by influx and conjunction from them, they constitute a unity, just as in the regenerated person, the internal man (which is also called the spiritual) and the external man (which is also called the natural) constitute one.

<margin>2</margin> Because such things are unknown today, it cannot therefore be known what is signified by committing adultery, beyond being unlawfully joined in respect to the body. Since these things, as was said, are unknown today, it is permitted to explain the reason why committing adultery in the spiritual sense signifies perverting those things which belong to the doctrine of faith and charity—thus, adulterating goods and falsifying truths. The reason, which is unknown today, is this: marriage love descends from the marriage of good and truth, which is called the heavenly marriage. The love which flows in from the Lord, and which exists between good and truth in heaven, is turned into marriage love on the earth, and this happens through correspondence. For this reason, the falsification of truth is fornication and the perversion of good is adultery in the internal sense. For this reason also, those who are not in the good and truth of faith cannot be in genuine marriage love; likewise, those who take their life's enjoyment in adulteries can no longer receive anything of faith. I have heard it said by angels that as soon as anyone commits adultery on the earth and takes enjoyment in it, heaven is closed to him—that is, he refuses to receive anything of faith and charity from heaven anymore. That today in the kingdoms where the church exists, adulteries...

That Babylon means those who pervert the truths and goods of the church for the sake of self-dominion and self-gain, and this even to the point of profanation, is evident from the meaning of Babel (n. 1182, 1283, 1295, 1304, 1306–1308, 1321, 1322, 1326, 1327). Therefore it is that Babylon is called a prostitute and the mother of prostitutes. Those who know nothing of the internal sense will suppose that the kings of the earth who committed whoredom with her signify literal kings on the earth or kingdoms; however, they do not signify kings or kingdoms, but the truths of faith of the church—to commit whoredom with these is to pervert them. That kings are the truths of faith can be seen above (n. 1672, 2015, 2069, 3009, 4575, 4581, 4966, 5044, 6148), and that the earth is the church (n. 566, 662, 1067, 1262, 1413, 1607, 1733, 1850, 2117, 2118, 2928, 3355, 4447, 4535, 5577, 8011, 8732).

From this, it is plain to see why adulteries are so wicked in themselves and are called abominations: namely, because they correspond to the marriage of falsity and evil, which is the infernal marriage. On the other hand, genuine marriages are holy because they correspond to the marriage of good and truth, which is the heavenly marriage. Indeed, genuine marriage love descends from the marriage of good and truth—thus out of heaven, or through heaven from the Lord—whereas the love of adultery is from the marriage of falsity and evil—thus from hell, or from the devil.

8905. Verse 13. *Thou shalt not steal* signifies that one's spiritual goods are not to be taken away from them, and that what belongs to the Lord is not to be attributed to oneself.

8906. *Thou shalt not steal.* That this signifies that one's spiritual goods are not to be taken away from them, and that what belongs to the Lord is not to be attributed to oneself, is evident from the meaning of stealing as taking away spiritual goods from anyone. Stealing has this meaning because, in the spiritual sense, riches and wealth are the knowledges of good and truth—in general, all those things which belong to faith and charity, which constitute the spiritual life in a person. Therefore, to take these possessions away from anyone is, in the spiritual sense, to steal. And because all spiritual goods—that is, all things of faith and charity—are from the Lord alone, and not at all from man, stealing also signifies attributing to oneself what belongs to the Lord. Those who do this are also called thieves and robbers in John: *Truly, I say to you, he who does not enter the sheepfold by the door, but climbs up some other way, is a thief and a robber. But he who enters by the door is the shepherd of the sheep.* . . .

### [No. 8906.] CHAPTER XX. VER. 13.

*the moon was darkened, and the stars withdrew their light* (ii. 1-10). Here the subject is the desolation of the church, when falsities break in and destroy truths; these falsities are the thieves who climb up into houses and enter through the windows. Who can help wondering why it is said that the day of Jehovah will be like the appearance of horses, and that then as horsemen they shall run, shall run back and forth in the city, shall run on the wall, shall climb up into the houses, shall enter in through the windows, the earth shall quake, the heavens shall tremble, the sun and the moon shall be darkened, and the stars shall withdraw their light? Anyone who knows nothing of the <term>internal sense</term> and who in their heart doubts the holiness of the Word will say that these are mere expressions without a Divine meaning hidden within them, and perhaps will call them empty sayings. But he who believes that the Word is most holy because it is Divine, and who moreover knows that there is an <term>internal sense</term> which treats of the church, of heaven, and of the Lord Himself, will confess that the various expressions in the passage above each have their own specific meaning. Therefore, the meaning of the expressions and the sayings in the passage will be briefly explained.

<margin>3</margin> The day of Jehovah is the final state or final time of the church, when truth no longer exists, but falsity takes the place of truth; the fire which devours before it is the lust of evil, the flame which burns after it is the lust of falsity resulting from it; the appearance of horses is the <term>intellectual</term> reasoning from falsity as if it were truth, the horsemen who run are those who reason, the chariots are the <term>doctrines</term> of falsity; a city is the doctrine itself, the wall upon which they run is essential falsity; the houses into which they climb are the <term>voluntary</term> part of man, and the windows by which they enter are <term>intellectual things</term>; the thief is the falsity which takes away truth; the earth which will be moved before Him is the church, as are the heavens which will tremble; the sun is love to the Lord, the moon is

8907. Verse 13. *You shall not bear false witness against your neighbor* signifies that good is not to be called evil nor truth falsity; thus, conversely, neither is evil to be called good nor falsity truth.

8908. *You shall not bear false witness against your neighbor.* That this signifies that good is not to be called evil nor truth falsity—thus, conversely, neither is evil to be called good nor falsity truth—is evident from the significance of false witness, as the confirmation of falsity. That by "witness" is meant confirmation may be seen above (n. 4197); and that falsehood is the falsity of faith will be plain from what follows. To bear witness against a neighbor is to speak in such a manner against anyone; for by "neighbor" is signified everyone, and particularly everyone who is in good, and in an abstract sense good itself (n. 3419, 5025, 6704, 6706-6711, 6818, 8123). Therefore, "You shall not bear false witness against your neighbor," in the internal sense, is not to say to anyone what is false—that is, not to say that good is evil and truth falsity, nor the converse.

<margin>2</margin> How this is shall be briefly explained. All with whom self-love or the love of the world reigns—that is, who have eminence or honors or wealth or gain as an end—feel no concern in saying and persuading that this unjust thing is just and this just thing is unjust, and thus acting as false witnesses. These same persons neither have conscience, nor do they know that it is a matter of conscience to call what is just "just" for no other reason than because it is just—that is, from the love of what is just. They who are of this character in the world are also of the same character in the other life, but with the difference that they do not then call what is just "unjust," but the good which is of faith they call evil, and truth they call falsity; for what is just in the civil world corresponds to what is good and true in the spiritual world. And they do this without conscience and also without shame, because they have been inured and habitu-
<margin>3</margin> ated to it in the life of the body. Frequent mention is made in the Word of falsehood, or a lie, and by this is signified everywhere in the internal sense the falsity and evil of faith; and by false witness, which is also called the witness of violence, is signified the confirmation of falsity, whether it be before a judge, or before anyone else, or before oneself by persuading oneself inwardly in thought (Exod. 23:1, and following verses; Lev. 19:11, 12; Deut. 19:16–20). That falsehood in the spiritual sense is the evil and falsity of faith is evident from the following passages—in John: *You are of your father the devil, and the lusts of your father you will to do. He was a murderer from the beginning, and stood not in the truth, because there is no truth in him. When he speaks a lie, he speaks of his own: for he is a liar, and the father of it* (John 8:44). A lie here stands for the falsity of faith; for the subject is the Jews, in that they would not acknowledge the Lord. The devil here in the spiritual sense is falsity, and the

<summary>This passage explains that those who allow their selfish desires to dominate their will eventually blind their understanding, leading them to call good "evil" and truth "falsity." In the spiritual sense, "bearing false witness" or "lying" signifies the confirmation of the falsity and evil of faith.</summary>
