<meta>This page continues the interpretation of the Tenth Commandment, transitioning from a discussion of falsehood to the spiritual significance of coveting and the relationship between the will and the understanding.</meta>

*and anything that is your neighbor’s* signifies that one must beware of the love of self and of the world, and thus lest the evils which are included in the preceding commandments become part of the will, and so come forth.

8910. *You shall not covet your neighbor’s house, you shall not covet your neighbor’s wife, and his manservant, and his maidservant, and his ox, and his ass, and anything that is your neighbor’s.* That this signifies that one must beware of the love of self and of the world, and thus lest the evils which are included in the preceding commandments become part of the will and so come forth, is evident from the meaning of coveting, or lusting after, as willing from an evil love. That coveting or lusting after has this meaning is because all lust is from some love; for nothing is lusted after unless it is loved, and therefore covetousness or lust is the continuation [*continuum*] of love—in this case of the love of self or of the world—and is as it were the life of the love’s breathing. For what an evil love breathes is called lust, but what a good love breathes is called desire. Love itself belongs to the other part of the mind which is called the will, for whatever a person loves, this he wills; but covetousness or lust belongs to both the will and the understanding, though it is properly of the will in the understanding. From this it is plain why it is that by the words, *You shall not covet the things that are your neighbor’s*, it is signified that one must beware lest they become part of the will; for the things which become part of the will are appropriated to the man, since the will is the man him-
<margin>2</margin>self. It is believed in the world that thought is the man; but there are two things which constitute the life of man: understanding and will. To the understanding belongs thought, and to the will affection which is of love. Thought without affection which is of love does not make up anything of the life in man, but thought from affection which is of the love, thus understanding from the will. That these two are distinct from each other is plain to everyone

<summary>This page explains that the Tenth Commandment warns against allowing the love of self and the world to enter the will through covetousness. It establishes that while thought belongs to the understanding, true life and character are determined by the will and its affections.</summary>

Therefore, it is that the things which enter into the thought of man, and not through the thought into the will, do not defile him, but only the things which enter through the thought into the will. These things defile him because they are then appropriated to him and become his own; for the will, as already said, is the man himself. These things cannot defile the man because they are sent back; for man cannot stop thinking evil, only from doing it. <header>No. 8910.] CHAPTER XX. VER. 14.</header>

*better for you that one of your members perish, and not your whole body be cast into Gehenna (v. 29, 30).* From these words it is further evident in what manner the Lord spoke—namely, from the Divine, as in other passages throughout the Word. Thus, He expressed internal and heavenly things through external or natural things according to correspondences. In this case, He expressed an affection for evil or its lust by the right eye causing one to stumble, and an affection for falsity or its lust by the right hand causing one to stumble. For the eye corresponds to faith—the left eye to the truth of faith and the right eye to the good of faith, or, in the opposite sense, to the evil which belongs to faith. Thus, the right eye causing one to stumble refers to the lust for evil (n. 4403-4421, 4523-4534). But the hand corresponds to the power which belongs to truth; the right hand to the power of truth from good, or, in the opposite sense, to the power of falsity from evil. Thus, the right hand causing one to stumble refers to its lust (n. 3091, 3563, 4931-4937, 8281). Gehenna is the hell of lusts. Everyone can see that in this passage "the right eye" does not mean the physical right eye, nor is it meant that the eye should be plucked out; also that "the right hand" does not mean the physical right hand, nor that it is to be cut off. Instead, something else is meant, which cannot be known unless it is known what is signified by the eye, what specifically by the right eye, as well as what by the hand and specifically by the right hand, and likewise what by causing to stumble. Nor can it be known what is signified by those expressions except from the internal sense.

<meta>This page continues the exposition of the Ten Commandments, concluding the explanation of the internal meaning of not coveting and beginning a discussion on the nature of the human will and its relationship to action and judgment.</meta>

<header>EXODUS. [No. 8910.</header>

...are included in the preceding commandments become matters of the will and so emerge. The fact that not coveting things that belong to the neighbor signifies that one must beware of the love of self and of the world is because all the evils of lust spring from those loves as if from their fountainheads (n. 2045, 7178, 7255, 7366—7377, 7488, 8318, 8678).

8911. From what has been said so far, it may be evident how it is with man and his life—namely, that a man is as his will is, and that he remains so after death, because death is not the end of life but its continuation. Since, therefore, a man is as his will is—because, as was said above, the will is the man himself—then being judged according to his deeds signifies being judged according to his will. For will and deed are not at variance; the deed is in the will and the will is in the deed, provided that external restraints do not prevent them—such as fears of the law, the loss of honor, of gain, of reputation, and of life. It is just as it is with effort and motion. Motion is nothing other than continuous effort; for when the effort ceases, motion ceases, since there is nothing essential in motion except effort. The learned know this, for it is an acknowledged and confirmed theorem. Effort in man is the will, and motion in him is action; they are called this in man because in him effort and motion are living. To be judged according to the will is the same thing as being judged according to love; it is also the same as being judged according to the ends of life, and likewise being judged according to the life itself. For the will of man is his love, it is his end of life, and it is his very life itself. That this is the case is evident from the words of the Lord quoted above: that whoever looks at a woman to lust after her has already committed adultery with her in his heart (Matthew 5:27, 28). Also, to kill a man is not only to do so in act but also to will to do it, as is signified by being angry with him and treating him with insult (Matthew 5:21).

<summary>This page explains that coveting signifies the love of self and the world, and establishes that a person’s true nature is defined by their will, which persists after death. It argues that being judged by deeds is essentially being judged by the will and love that drive those actions.</summary>

<meta>This page continues the discussion on how the will relates to human judgment and defines the internal spiritual meaning of the various items mentioned in the commandment against coveting.</meta>

<header>No. 8913.] CHAPTER XX. VER. 15 — 17.</header>

Man is judged in fact according to his deeds, but only to the extent and in the manner that his deeds have proceeded from his will.

8912. It must be explained briefly what is meant in the internal sense by the things mentioned here—namely, house, wife, male servant, female servant, ox, and donkey—which are not to be coveted. These represent all the goods and truths of faith in one complex, which are not to be taken away from anyone and to which no injury is to be done. They are the same things that, in the internal sense, are signified by keeping the Sabbath day holy, honoring father and mother, not killing, not committing adultery, not stealing, and not bearing false witness. All these things have been shown above to be, in the internal sense, matters of love and faith. By house is meant all good in general; by wife, all truth in general; by male servant, affection for spiritual truth; by female servant, affection for spiritual good; by ox, affection for natural good; and by donkey, affection for natural truth. These are the things that are not to be coveted—that is, which are not to be taken away from anyone and are not to be harmed. The reason these things are meant in the internal sense is that the Word in that sense is for those who are in heaven, because those who are in heaven do not perceive the Word naturally, but spiritually. Thus, they do not perceive a house, a wife, a male servant, a female servant, an ox, or a donkey, but the spiritual things that correspond to them, which are the goods of love and the truths of faith. In short, the external sense or the literal sense is for those who are in the world, but the internal sense is for those who are in heaven, and also for those in the world so far as they are at the same time in heaven—that is, so far as they are in charity and faith.

"And they said to Moses" signifies complaint; "Speak with us" signifies the reception of truth in an accommodated form, which they would thus obey; "and do not let God speak with us" signifies truth in a form not accommodated; "lest we die" signifies that in this way the life of heaven in them would perish. "And Moses said to the people" signifies information; "Do not fear: for God has come to test you" signifies that the life of heaven should not perish, and that this is only so that it may be known that it really exists and what its quality is; "and that His fear may be before your faces, so that you do not sin" signifies holy fear for the Divine resulting from this, and thus the preservation of spiritual life.

The reason is that Divine truth, or the Word in its internal form, is as it is in heaven—and thus as it is in the light there—but in its external form, it is as it is in the world, and thus as it is in the light there; and the light of the world, compared with the light of heaven, is like a cloud or like smoke compared with a flame. Divine truth, or the Word in its internal form, is its internal sense, and in its external form, it is its external or literal sense. That this literal sense is called a cloud may be seen in the preface to Genesis 18 and elsewhere (nos. 4060, 4391, 5922, 6343, 6752, 8106, 8781); and that it is called smoke is because smoke signifies the obscurity of truth (no. 8819). The fact that the mountain appeared to be smoking before the sons of Israel was not because the Divine there was of such a quality, but because the Divine appears to everyone according to the quality of the person who sees it. The quality of those who saw it then was that they made everything of worship consist in externals and nothing in internals; consequently, they understood the Word only according to the sense of the letter. Therefore, the Divine—in regard to the truth being proclaimed—could not appear to them as anything other than smoke, that is, as something obscure. This is also why it is said that they "stood afar off," which signifies that they were remote from internal things—but more will be said on this subject below.

8917. *And when the people saw, they were moved.* That this signifies the tremor that occurs when Divine truths are received, is evident from the meaning of being "moved" as tremor—in this case, a tremor such as occurs when Divine truths are received (concerning which tremor, see above, nos. 5459, 8816).

<meta>This page continues the internal sense analysis of Exodus 20, explaining the spiritual significance of the people "standing afar off" and the nature of space and time in the spiritual world.</meta>

### No. 8918.] CHAPTER XX. VER. 15—17.

For the internal of man is in the light of heaven, and his external in the light of the world; or what is the same, the soul or spirit of man is in heaven, but his body in the world. Heaven is nearer to the Divine than the world is, because in heaven the Divine of the Lord reigns and is all in all. Regarding the further meaning of *afar off*, it should be known that "afar off" in the spiritual sense does not refer to space, but to the Divine, and thus to good and truth. Distance from the very good itself which proceeds from the Divine causes the appearance of distance in heaven. The angelic societies there appear distinct, and even at a distance from one another; but this idea of space arises from the distance from the good and truth which are from the Divine of the Lord, as already said. This must necessarily appear as a paradox, even absurd, to many in the world. The reason is that thoughts and the ideas derived from them in man are founded upon space and time, so much so that man cannot think without them. Therefore, if you remove time and space from the thought of man, he perceives almost nothing. Nevertheless, angels in heaven think without any idea of time and space at all, and with such fullness that their thoughts exceed the thoughts of man in intelligence and wisdom a thousand, even a million times; and, what is wonderful, if an idea derived from time and space interferes with them, shadow and darkness immediately come over their minds, because they then fall from the light of heaven into the light of nature, which to them is darkness. <margin>2</margin> That there are not spaces and times in the other life, but states—or that the appearances of space and time arise from the variations of state regarding good and truth—may be seen above (nos. 2625, 2837, 3356, 3387, 3404, 4321, 4882, 5605, 7381). From this it is now plain what standing *afar off*

It contrasts human thought, which is limited by space and time, with angelic thought, which operates through "states" of being and achieves far greater wisdom.</summary>

signifies in the spiritual sense, namely, remoteness from heaven where the Divine is, and here remoteness from internal things. This is because, as was said above, that nation which then stood afar off from Mount Sinai was very remote from internal things, for it was in external things only, and it made everything of Divine worship consist of these. Moreover, that nation was permitted to do so because it could thus represent heavenly and Divine things; for an external is required in order to be a representative, and this is also possible without the internal (n. 3147, 3670, 4208, 4281, <margin>3</margin> 4288, 4307, 8588). *Afar off* signifies remoteness from good and truth, which are from the Divine—and thus remoteness from internal things—also in the following passages in Luke: *In hell the rich man lifted up his eyes . . . and sees Abraham afar off, and Lazarus in his bosom . . . and Abraham said to him, Between us and you there is a great gulf fixed; so that those who would pass from here to you may not be able; neither may those who are there cross over to us (xvi. 23, 26).* By Abraham here, Abraham himself is not meant, for he is not known in heaven; rather, in the supreme sense, the Lord is meant, and in the respective sense, those in heaven who are in the good of love and faith in the Lord (n. 1834, 1876, 1965, 1989, 2011, 3245, 3305, 6098, 6185, 6276, 6894). Those who are in hell are said to see those who are in heaven *afar off* because they are in a state most remote from good and truth. The great gulf between them is the remoteness itself from good, which indeed <margin>4</margin> creates an appearance of an intervening gulf. Those who think from an idea of space, as all people in the world do, perceive only that hell is far distant from man, and that heaven is likewise. But the case is different. Hell and heaven are near to man—indeed, they are in man: hell in an evil person and heaven in a good person. Moreover, after death everyone enters into that hell or into that heaven in which he has been while in the world. But then the state is changed; the hell which was not perceived

<meta>This page continues the explanation of section 8920, focusing on how Divine truth is adapted or "accommodated" as it descends from the highest heavens to humanity on earth.</meta>

-># EXODUS.<-
<meta>[No. 8920.</meta>

2 This is truth in an accommodated form. Concerning truth in an accommodated form, it should be known that Divine truth, when it descends through the heavens to men, as the Word has descended, is accommodated along the way for everyone—both for those who are in the heavens and for those who are on the earth. But Divine truth exists in a very different form in the heavens than it does in the world. In the heavens, it is in the form of the internal sense of the Word; in the world, it is in the form of the sense of its letter. Indeed, in the heavens themselves, it exists in various forms: in one form in the inmost or third heaven, in another form in the middle or second heaven, and in still another in the first or outmost heaven. The form of Divine truth—that is, the perception, thought, and utterance of it—in the inmost or third heaven so transcends what is in the middle or second heaven that it cannot be understood there, for it is so Divine and exalted. It contains countless things which cannot be expressed in the second heaven, as it consists entirely of changes of state regarding the <term>affections of love</term>. But the form of Divine truth in the middle or second heaven similarly transcends that in the first or outmost heaven, and even more so the form of Divine truth in the world. For this reason, the things uttered in those heavens are such as no human mind has ever perceived, nor any ear heard—as those who have been elevated into heaven know from experience.

3 Those who do not know this suppose that in the heavens they think and speak no differently than they do on earth; but they suppose this because they do not know that the <term>interiors</term> of man are in a more exalted state than his <term>exteriors</term>, and that the thought and speech of those who are in the heavens is heavenly and spiritual, while that of those on the earth is natural. The difference between these is so great that it cannot be described in words—but concerning these kinds of speech, see what has been said before (n. 1634–1650, 1757–1759, 1876, 2157, 2472, 2476, 3342–3345, 4104, 4609, 5225, 5287).

<summary>This page explains that Divine truth is accommodated to different levels of heaven and earth, taking on various forms from the purely spiritual affections of the highest heaven to the literal sense of the Word on earth. It highlights the vast difference between heavenly/spiritual thought and natural thought, noting that the higher forms are incomprehensible to the lower levels.</summary>
<keywords>truth Divine, internal sense, sense of the letter, three heavens, accommodations of truth, spiritual vs natural, affections of love, interiors and exteriors</keywords>

From these things it is also plain that, unless <term>Divine truth</term> or <term>the Word</term> were presented in an accommodated form, it could not be understood; for if it were in a more exalted form than the state of <term>perception</term>, it would not reach the understanding, and thus not faith. Therefore it is that <term>Divine truth</term> was given to man in such a form as <term>the Word</term> is in its literal sense; for if it were presented as it is in heaven, no man would understand it, and at first sight and first <term>apperception</term> would reject it, because it would not fit into the things that belong to natural light. Moreover, it would be full of <term>arcana</term> such as could in no way enter into any human idea, because they are altogether opposed to the appearances and fallacies which are derived from the world through the external senses—to say nothing of the deeper <term>arcana</term> which lie hidden within those <term>arcana</term> in manifold series, and can be expressed in no way but by variations and changes of state of heavenly light and flame, by which angelic speech and thought are affected.

8921. *And let not God speak with us.* That this signifies truth in a form not accommodated, is evident from what precedes; for by <term>Moses</term> is signified <term>Divine truth</term> below heaven joined to <term>Divine truth</term> in heaven, thus mediating between the Lord and the people, and consequently <term>Divine truth</term> in an accommodated form, as was just now shown above (n. 8920). Therefore by the words, "Let not God speak with us," is signified <term>Divine truth</term> in a form not accommodated; for by "God" is here meant the Lord with respect to <term>Divine truth</term> in heaven (n. 8805), and consequently the <term>Divine truth</term> itself. And this is the reason why it is said "God," not "<term>Jehovah</term>"; for in <term>the Word</term> where "God" is named, the Lord with respect to <term>Divine truth</term> is meant, but where "<term>Jehovah</term>" is named the Lord with respect to <term>Divine good</term> is meant (n. 2586, 2769, 2807, 2822, 3921, 4295, 4402, 7091, 7268, 7873, 8301, 8867).

8924. *Do not fear: for God has come to test you.* That this signifies that the <term>life of heaven</term> should not perish, but that this happens only so that it may be known that it really exists and what its nature is, is evident from the meaning of "not fearing"—namely, that they should die—as meaning that they should perish in respect to the <term>life of heaven</term> (see n. 8922); and from the meaning of "to test you," as meaning to teach that there is a <term>life of heaven</term> and what its nature is. The reason this is signified by testing is that all <term>spiritual temptation</term> teaches and confirms such truths within man. For temptations are intended so that faith in truth and affection for truth, and afterward affection for good, may be implanted and rooted, and thus man may receive new life, which is the <term>life of heaven</term>. For temptations are battles against evils and falsities, and when man conquers these, he is confirmed; for

Concerning holy fear, which is signified in the Word by the fear of God, it should be known that this fear is love, but love such as the love of little children toward parents, of parents toward their children, and of spouses to each other, who fear to do anything which displeases, and thus in any manner harms love. Such fear is implanted in love during a person's regeneration. This fear, since it is in agreement with love and can be within—and actually is within or united to—love, is called therefore holy fear, and is the fear of sinning or doing anything contrary to the commandments, thus contrary to the Lord. But this fear differs with everyone according to the quality and extent of the love—see what has been shown above (n. 2826, 3718, 3719, 5459, 7280, 7788).

8928. *And Moses drew near to the thick darkness where God was.* That this signifies the continued conjunction of the truth of spiritual good with truth Divine, is evident from the representation of Moses, as truth Divine below heaven conjoined to truth Divine in heaven, and consequently mediating (see nos. 8760, 8787, 8805), thus the truth of spiritual good; for this is truth Divine below heaven, in which truth the spiritual church is, which is represented by the sons of Israel—this truth is represented by Moses as the head of that church (no. 7041); from the signification of drawing near, as conjunction, for to draw near to the Divine is to be conjoined to it; and from the signification of thick darkness, as truth Divine in respect to those who are of the spiritual church, and also in respect to that people over whom Moses was in charge as their leader. That truth Divine is thick darkness to that church and that people is because they are not in any light regarding Divine truths. <margin>2</margin> First, regarding those who belong to the spiritual church, they believe that they are in light; but they are in obscurity, even in thick darkness, concerning Divine truth. This is evident because they do not know from any internal perception that what the church says is true, but only because the church says so. What the church says they confirm for themselves, whether it is false or true; and he who has no internal perception regarding Divine truth is in thick darkness, or what is the same thing, to him Divine truth is thick darkness. For example, those who are of the spiritual church do not know, nor are they willing to know, that there is an internal sense of the Word; and if by chance they were to believe it, this would not be from any internal perception that it is so, but from persuasion from some other source. <margin>3</margin> To take another example: those who are of the spiritual church say that faith is the only means of salvation, even without charity

That heaven is where the Divine is—that is, where the Lord is—is plain from this: that Mount Sinai, from which the Lord spoke, is here called heaven. This is also the reason why Mount Sinai signifies heaven, from which comes 
<margin>3</margin> Divine truth (no. 8805). That all things of the Word are signified is because Jehovah, or the Lord, then began to reveal the Word which was to serve the human race for doctrine and life, first through Moses and afterward through the prophets. So that they might know that the Word was from the Divine through heaven, the Lord Himself willed to come down and with a living voice proclaim the ten commandments; thus, He showed that what was to follow in the law—that is, in the Word—was likewise by influx from the Divine through heaven.

8932. *You shall not make with Me gods of silver, and gods of gold.* That this signifies that they should abstain altogether from such things as in their external form appear as true and good, but in their internal form are false and evil, is evident from the meaning of "making gods" as worshipping—for whoever makes gods for himself does it for the sake of worship; from the meaning of silver as truth, and therefore in the opposite sense, falsity; and from the meaning of gold as good, and therefore in the opposite sense, evil (see nos. 113, 1551, 1552, 2954, 5658, 6914, 6917, 7999). That those things are meant which appear true and good in the external form but in the internal are false and evil is because it is said, *You shall not make them with Me*, that is, with Jehovah God. For Divine truth itself and Divine good itself are in internals and are likewise in externals; however, in externals, Divine truth and good are in representative types, for external things in a type refer to and represent internal things. External things are false and evil whenever they are separated from internal things and yet are considered holy or are worshipped; nevertheless, they still appear true and good because they represent what is true and good. These things are signified by making

for example all the ceremonies of the Jewish Church, such
as the sacrifices, incense, washings, and many others. These
in their external form were true and good, not of themselves,
but because in their representative type they had reference to or represented
internal truths and goods, which are of love and faith in
the Lord. When such things in their external form were
accounted holy, and especially when they were worshipped—
as by the Jews and Israelites when they became idolaters
and applied them to the worship of foreign gods—they
then retained nothing of the truths and goods which in
their representative type they referred to or represented, except for the
appearance, because in their internal form they were falsities
and evils. It was similar with all the other things which **3**
were among that people types representing heavenly
and Divine things. For as soon as the external things

other gods, they became idols which they worshipped,
or gods of silver and of gold which they made alongside Je-
hovah God; for then in external form they appeared as
true and good, but in the internal they were false and evil.

In general, gods of silver and of gold are all the falsities **4**

Worship from good was represented by an altar of earth, but worship from truth was represented by an altar of stones; both kinds of altars are mentioned here. These two things from which worship is performed are called faith and charity; worship from truth relates to faith, and worship from good to charity. As for worship from faith and worship from charity, or from truth and from good, the case is this: before a person is regenerated, they are in worship from truth, but when they are regenerated, they are in worship from good. For before a person is regenerated, they are led by truth to good—that is, by faith to charity—but when they are regenerated, they are in good and from that in truth; that is, they are in charity and from that in faith (n. 8516, 8539, 8643, 8648, 8658). These two kinds of worship are what are represented by altars of earth and of stone. An altar is the primary representative of the worship of the Lord, because burnt offerings and sacrifices were offered on it, and these were the things in which the Divine worship of the Hebrew nation—and thus of the Israelite and Jewish nations—principally consisted (see n. 923, 1343, 2180, 2805, 2807, 2830, 3519, 6905).

8936. *And shall sacrifice on it your burnt offerings and your thank offerings.* That this signifies worship specifically according to the state of each person’s spiritual life is evident from the signification of burnt offerings and sacrifices. When truths are hatched from the <term>proprium</term> or from self-intelligence, they rule over the truths that are from the Divine, as these are used only to confirm them; yet the contrary should be the case: namely, that truths from the Divine should rule, and those from self-intelligence should serve. Those that are from the <term>proprium</term> or from self-intelligence are called truths, but they are not truths; they only appear as truths in their external form, for they are made to look like truths by applying the literal sense of the Word and through reasonings, while in their internal form they are falsities. Their nature and quality may be seen above (n. 8932). There are in the <margin>**3**</margin> world two religious systems that originate from self-intelligence—one in which the love of self and of the world is everything. Some from this religion acknowledge the Word, but they apply it to confirm their own ideas, thus making it serve [them]. Some, however, do not acknowledge the Word; instead, they place the Divine in nature. From this it may be evident what is signified in the spiritual sense by the statute,

**4** that an altar should not be built of hewn stones. By "hewn stone" is signified that which comes from self-intelligence, as also in the following passages—In Isaiah: *The people shall know—Ephraim and the inhabitant of Samaria—who say in arrogance and pride, "The bricks have fallen, but we will build with hewn stone"* (ix. 9, 10). In Jeremiah: *When I cry and call for help, He shuts out my prayer. He has fenced up my ways with hewn stone; He has made my paths crooked* (Lam. iii. 8, 9). In Amos: *Because you trample upon the poor and take from him a tax of wheat, you have built houses of hewn stone, but you shall not live in them* (v. 11). In these passages, "hewn stone" stands for such things in matters of faith as are from self-intelligence.

**5** Because such was the significance of hewn stone, the altar first erected in the land of Canaan by the sons of Israel after they had passed over the Jordan,

was built of unhewn stones; for by the passage over the Jordan, introduction into the kingdom of the Lord was represented, which is brought about by the truths of faith. Of this altar it is written in Joshua: *Joshua built an altar to Jehovah, the God of Israel, on Mount Ebal, as Moses the servant of Jehovah commanded the sons of Israel . . . an altar of whole stones, upon which no man had lifted up iron* (8:30, 31; see also Deut. 27:1-8).

<margin>6</margin>
In the same way, the temple of Jerusalem was built of whole, unhewn stones, of which it is written in the First Book of Kings: *As for the house itself, while it was being built, it was built of whole stone as it was brought; for there was neither hammer nor axe nor any tool of iron heard in the house while it was being built* (6:7); for the temple of the Lord represented the Lord in terms of Divine truth. That the Lord was represented by the temple, He Himself teaches in John (2:19, 21, 22); and the fact that He was represented in terms of Divine truth was because this truth was taught there; for this reason it was also built of stones, since stones signified Divine truth (no. 8940); and for this reason the Lord Himself was called the Stone of Israel (no. 6426).

<margin>7</margin>
From these things it is now clear what was signified by the stone of the altar, and also by the stone of the temple; likewise what was signified by the stones being whole and unhewn—namely, that religion was to be formed by truths from the Lord, and thus from the Word, and not from self-intelligence. Truths which are from self-intelligence are also described this way in Isaiah: *The graven image a workman melts, and the goldsmith spreads it over with gold, and casts silver chains. . . . He seeks a skillful workman to prepare a graven image* (40:19, 20). The graven image stands for the religion that comes from the <term>proprium</term>, which is set up to be worshiped as Divine (see no. 8869); the workman stands for those who hatch and devise ideas from the <term>proprium</term>; and that these ideas may appear like truths is described by his spreading it over with gold,

8942. *For if you lift up your tool upon it.* That this signifies if they are from the <term>proprium</term>, is evident from the signification of a tool as truth devised, and thus from the <term>proprium</term>; for the tool is made of iron, by which stones are cut and fashioned into form. Here, therefore, it is the <term>proprium</term> of man that fashions the things which belong to religion so that they may appear in the form of truth. Instead of "tool," the term "iron" is sometimes used, and sometimes "axe" (see Deut. 27:5; Josh. 8:30, 31; 1 Kings 6:7; Isa. 44:11, 12; Jer. 10:3), and by these instruments are signified such things as belong to <term>self-intelligence</term> and devise <note>fiction for truth</note>.

8943. *You shall pollute it.* That this signifies that in such a case there will be no worship, is evident from the meaning of polluting, as causing there to be no worship. For that which is derived from <term>self-intelligence</term> is in itself void of life, even spiritually dead, for the <term>proprium</term> of man is nothing but evil; therefore if Divine worship is performed from the <term>proprium</term>, that worship is no different than the worship of an idol, carved or cast, in which there is no spirit—that is, no life. But that which is from the Word is alone useful for Divine worship, since it is in itself living. For inwardly in everything of the Word there is a <term>spiritual sense</term>, which treats of the kingdom of the Lord, and within that sense is the Divine, since the Word in its <term>inmost sense</term> treats of the Lord alone; from this comes the holiness and the life of the Word, and from no other source. The Word is like a Divine man; the <term>literal sense</term> is, as it were, his body, but the <term>internal sense</term> is, as it were, his soul; from this it is clear that the <term>literal sense</term> has life through the <term>internal sense</term>. It appears as if the <term>literal sense</term> vanishes or dies because of the <term>internal sense</term>, when on the contrary it does not vanish, much less die, but lives through the <term>internal sense</term>. From these things it is now evident that truly Divine worship exists from those things which are of the Word, and never from those things which are of <term>self-intelligence</term>. Therefore, the phrase *if you lift up a tool upon the altar you shall pollute it* signifies that if you devise things pertaining to Divine worship from <term>self-intelligence</term>, and do not derive them from the Word, there is no worship.

8944. It is believed in the world that man, by the light of nature and thus without revelation, can know many things pertaining to religion—such as that there is a God, that He is to be worshiped, and also that He is to be loved, likewise that man will live after death, and many other truths which depend upon these. And yet this belief is derived from self-

It has also been shown that many who have written upon natural theology, and from the light of their nature have skillfully confirmed those things which related to the doctrine of their church, deny those same things in their hearts in the other life more than others do; they also deny the <term>Word</term> itself, which they attempt to destroy utterly; for in the other life, hearts speak. It has also been shown that these same people can receive nothing of <term>influx</term> out of heaven, but only from the hells. Hence it was clear what the quality of the light of nature is without revelation, and consequently, what the quality is of that which comes from <term>self-intelligence</term>. For the more interior it is in heaven, the more pure and holy it is. Those who are in a more impure state are kept in a lower sphere, where their impurities are not perceived and do not appear, because they are in a grosser good and in more obscure truth. **3** It sometimes happens that those who come into heaven desire to enter an interior heaven, believing that they will enjoy greater joy there. In this way, they are instructed about the life of heaven and about the state of their own life by comparison. They also learn that no one has heaven merely by being received or admitted—as is the common opinion in the world—and that a person acquires the capacity to live hereafter with those in heaven through their life in the world (see what has already been said and shown on this subject from experience in n. 3938, 4225, 4226, 4299, 4674, 5057, 5058, 7186, 7519, 8794, 8797). 8946. *That your nakedness be not discovered there.* That this signifies the idea of thought concerning them, which is thus full of falsities and will then be revealed, is evident from the meaning of nakedness as being destitute of truths (see n. 5433), and thus an idea of thought full of falsities; and from the meaning of "being discovered" as being revealed or manifested. For the more interior the advance into the heavens, the purer the good and the purer the truth. Therefore, so that the falsities of the thought and the evils of the will do not come into view but remain hidden, they are kept in lower regions where they are in a relatively obscure light. From this it may also be clear what was meant by the saying that no one can see Jehovah and live; for Jehovah is pure love, and from Him comes pure light, and to be seen in these is to perish. Therefore, even the angels in heaven are veiled with a cloud (n. 6849), and all who are in hell are veiled in thick clouds (n. 3340, 8137, 8138, 8814, 8819); for in that realm, clouds are falsities.

### THE SPIRITS AND INHABITANTS OF THE PLANET SATURN.

8947. The spirits from this planet, and likewise the planet itself, appear in front at a considerable distance, on a level with the lower part of the knees; and when the eye is opened in that direction, a multitude of spirits from that earth comes into view. They are seen on this side of that earth and to the right.

8948. I have also been allowed to talk with them and thereby learn their character compared with others. They are upright and modest. And because they regard themselves as small compared to others, they also appear small in the next life; for everyone’s appearance in the next life depends on their disposition and their life.

8949. In worship they are very humble, for during worship they regard themselves as nothing. They worship our Lord and acknowledge Him as the only God. For the Lord sometimes appears to those who are on that earth in an angelic form—and thus as a man—and then the Divine beams forth from His face and affects their minds. The inhabitants also, when they reach adulthood, speak with spirits, and by these they are instructed concerning the Lord, how He is to be worshipped, and how they ought to live.

8950. When anyone tries to seduce the spirits of that earth and draw them away from faith in the Lord, or from humility toward Him and from uprightness of life, they say that they wish to die. Then little knives are seen in their hands, with which they seem to want to pierce their chests. When they are asked why they do so, they say that they would rather die than be led away from the Lord. The spirits of our earth sometimes laugh at these things and pester them with questions about why they do so. But they answer that they know very well that they are not going to

<summary>This page describes the humble and upright nature of the spirits from Saturn, who worship the Lord in human form and receive instruction directly from spirits. They demonstrate an extreme devotion to the Lord, claiming they would rather die than be led away from their faith and humility.</summary>

8951. There are also some on that earth who call their light at night, which is great, the Lord; but these are separated from the rest and are not tolerated among them. This light at night proceeds from the great belt which encompasses that earth at a distance, and from the moons which are called the satellites of Saturn.

8952. They were asked about that great belt, which appears from our earth to be elevated above the horizon of that planet and to vary its positions. They said that it does not appear to them as a belt, but only as a snowy light in the heaven in various directions.

8953. The inhabitants and spirits of that earth correspond to that in man which is in the middle between the spiritual sense and the natural sense, but they withdraw from the natural and draw near to the spiritual. Therefore, those spirits often seem to themselves to be carried away or caught up into heaven and afterward let back, doing so alternately. For whatever belongs to the spiritual sense is in heaven, but whatever belongs to the natural sense is below heaven.

8954. There are no cities there, nor kingdoms, but they live divided into families, each family apart from others—specifically, husband and wife together with their children. When the children marry, they are separated from the house of their parents and have no more concern for it. Therefore, the spirits from that earth are seen in pairs.

8955. Everyone on that earth, unlike those on ours, knows that they will live after death. For this reason, they also place no value on their bodies, except so far as may be necessary for the life which they say is to remain and serve the Lord. Because of this, they do not bury the bodies of the dead but cast them out and cover them with branches of trees from the forest.

<header>EXODUS. [No. 8958.</header>

## CHAPTER TWENTY-FIRST.

### THE DOCTRINE OF CHARITY.

8958. Those who are being regenerated undergo temptations.

8959. Temptations are spiritual combats within man. For they are combats between the evil which is within him from hell and the good which is within him from the Lord.

8960. Temptation is brought about by evil spirits who dwell with man in his evils and falsities; these spirits excite his evils and accuse him. But angels from the Lord who dwell in his goods and truths call forth the truths of faith which are with him and defend him.

8961. The contest in temptations concerns the dominion of the evil which is with man from hell, or of the good which is with him from the Lord. The evil which wills to rule is in the natural or external man, but the good is in the spiritual or internal man. Hence, in temptations there is a further contest for the dominion of the one man over the other. If evil conquers, then the natural man rules over the spiritual; if good conquers, then the spiritual man rules over the natural.

8962. These combats are waged by truths of faith which are from the Word. From these, man must fight against evils and falsities; if he fights from anything other than these, he does not conquer, because the Lord is not in other truths.

8963. Because the combat is waged by truths of faith which are from the Word, man is not admitted into combat until he is in the <term>knowledges</term> <note>Technical term referring to specific spiritual cognitions or facts of faith.</note> of truth and of good, and has gained some spiritual life from them. Therefore, these combats do not take place with man until he has reached years of maturity.

<vocab>regeneration, temptations, spiritual combats, evil, good, hell, the Lord, evil spirits, falsities, angels, truths of faith, natural man, external man, spiritual man, internal man, the Word, knowledges, maturity</vocab>

<summary>This text introduces the spiritual nature of temptations, describing them as a struggle for dominion between the natural and spiritual man. It explains that these combats are fought using truths from the Word and only occur once a person has reached spiritual and natural maturity.</summary>

<meta>This page continues the "Doctrine of Charity" from Chapter 21, specifically expanding on the conditions and results of spiritual temptations (sections 8964–8968).</meta>

<page-num>189</page-num>
<header>No. 8968.] CHAPTER XXI.</header>

8964. He who does not have truths of faith from the Word within himself by which he may fight—and thus who does not have any spiritual life in himself derived from those truths—is not admitted into any combat, because he would then yield; and if man yields, his state after temptation becomes worse than his state before temptation, for evil has then acquired power over good, and falsity over truth.

8965. Because faith is rare today, since the church is at its end, few today undergo any spiritual temptations. Consequently, it is scarcely known what they are and to what end they lead.

8966. Temptations lead to confirming the truths of faith, and also to implanting them and instilling them in the will, so that they may become goods of charity. For man, as already said, fights from the truths of faith against evils and falsities; and because his mind is then in truths, when he conquers he confirms himself in them and implants them; he also regards the evils and falsities which assaulted him as an enemy and rejects them from himself. Moreover, through temptations the lusts belonging to self-love and the love of the world are subdued, and man becomes humble. Thus he is rendered fit to receive the life of heaven from the Lord—the new life that belongs to the regenerated man.

8967. Since by temptations the truths of faith are confirmed and the goods of charity are implanted, and also the lusts of evil are subdued, it follows that through temptations the spiritual or internal man acquires dominion over the natural or external man—thus the good belonging to charity and faith prevails over the evil belonging to self-love and the love of the world. When this is accomplished, man has enlightenment and perception of what is true and what is good, as well as what is evil and false; thereby he has intelligence and wisdom, which afterward increase day by day.

to the good of charity, he then undergoes temptations; but when he is in the good of charity, temptations cease, for then he is in heaven.

8969. In temptations, man ought to fight against evils and falsities as if by himself, but still believe that he fights from the Lord. If he does not believe this during the temptation itself, because at that time he is in obscurity, he should still believe it after the temptation. If a man, after temptation, does not believe that the Lord alone has fought and conquered for him, then he has undergone only external temptation; such temptation does not penetrate deeply, nor does it cause anything of faith and charity to take root.

### CHAPTER XXI.

1. Now these are the judgments which you shall set before them.
2. When you buy a Hebrew servant, he shall serve six years; and in the seventh year he shall go out free, for nothing.
3. If he came in by himself, he shall go out by himself; if he is the husband of a wife, then his wife shall go out with him.
4. If his master has given him a wife, and she has borne him sons or daughters, the wife and her children shall belong to his master, and he shall go out by himself.
5. And if the servant shall plainly say, "I love my master, my wife, and my children; I will not go out free":
6. Then his master shall bring him to God, and shall bring him to the door, or to the doorpost; and his master shall bore through his ear with an awl, and he shall serve him forever.
7. And when a man sells his daughter to be a maidservant, she shall not go out as the male servants do.

### CONTENTS.

8970. In this chapter, in the internal sense, there is a description of those who injure or destroy the truth of faith or the good of charity in themselves or in others, what their punishment is, and what the restitution is. Such things are involved in the judgments or laws in this chapter concerning servants, concerning death or injury caused to companions or servants, also concerning oxen that gore with the horn, and concerning a pit.

### INTERNAL SENSE.

8971. That the Word is holy, indeed most holy, is known to everyone within the church. This is not only acknowledged but is also perceived by those who are in the truths of faith and in a life according to them, for they are kept continually in the idea of what is holy when they read the Word. But those who are not in the truths of faith and in a life according to them do not acknowledge, much less perceive, anything of holiness in the Word. When these

And those who in their heart deny the sanctity of the Word also say to themselves when they read it that the writings of men are more elegant, because they are composed in a more elegant style in their literal sense. This has been attested to me by living experience from those in the other life who in their heart denied that the Word was inspired by the Divine. But when it was said to them that the Word is holy and Divine down to every iota and every smallest detail, they were astounded, wondering how this could be. And when it was further said to them, and also shown to the life, that everything in the Word contains a spiritual sense which does not appear in the letter, and that this sense of the Word is present with the angels in heaven when the Word is read by man, they then acknowledged it because it was shown to them; but they said that they did not know this in the world, and because they did not know it, they were therefore without blame. But when these same individuals were examined, it was observed that they had lived for every pleasure without any restraints from conscience, and for this reason had denied in their heart the Divine, heaven and hell, a life after death, and all other matters of faith, and that this was the reason why they had not acknowledged the sanctity of the Word. And it was further attested that all those who have been in the truths of faith and in a life according to them have considered the Word holy, and have also perceived it as such within themselves while reading it. From this, those who had denied it were convinced that the cause was not in the Word, but in themselves. For in the case of those who are in a life of good, the <term>interiors</term> are open into heaven, from where the holiness of the Word flows in from angels; but in the case of those who are in a life of evil, the <term>interiors</term> are closed toward heaven and are open into hell, from where the opposite flows in.

8972. *Verse 1.* *Now these are the judgments which you shall set before them* signifies exterior truths, such as should be in the civil state where there is a representative church, and which flow from the internal truths which belong to order in the heavens; that these things are signified by the judgments which were to be set before the sons of Israel is evident from the meaning of judgments as truths (see n. 2235, 6397, 7206, 8685, 8695). The reason why judgments are truths is that all judgment is carried out by truths. Therefore, by doing judgment in the Word is signified doing truth, that is, judging according to truths. But by judgments, in the plural, are signified civil laws, thus exterior truths such as exist in the civil state. It is said "where there is a representative church," for the reason that interiorly they contain and involve in them those truths which belong to order in the heavens, as may be evident <margin>2</margin> from their internal sense. The laws which were enacted and commanded to the sons of Israel by the Lord were distinguished into commandments, judgments, and statutes. Those were called commandments which related to the life, those judgments which related to the civil state, and those statutes which related to worship. As to judgments in particular, they are such laws as are contained in this chapter, and also in several chapters that follow. They served for laws in the church wherein the internal things which belong to heaven and the church were represented by external things. But they do not serve for laws in the church wherein internal things are no longer represented by external—as in the Christian Church. The reason is

...that internal things have been revealed to the person of this Church, and therefore communication with heaven is effected through internal things, and not through external things as before. This is the reason why the person of the Christian Church is not bound to observe those laws called judgments and statutes in their external form, but in their internal form. Still, a sanctity remains in them because they contain holy things within them, just as do each and every thing commanded in the Word concerning sacrifices. Although these laws are abolished, they are still holy parts of the Word because of the Divine things which are in them and which they represented. For when they are read by a Christian, the Divine things within them that were represented are perceived in the heavens and fill angels with what is holy; at the same time, by influx from the angels, they fill the person who reads, especially if he himself then thinks of the Divine things within them.

From this it is plain that the Word, even of the Old Testament, is most holy.

"servant" is predicated of those who are in truth and not in corresponding good, and in general is predicated of truth in respect to good (n. 3409). "Hebrew" is predicated of those things which belong to the church, and of those things which are of some service—that it is predicated of those things which belong to the church may be seen above (see n. 5136, 5236, 6675, 6684), and also that it is predicated of those things which are of some service (n. 1703, 1741, 5013). Since the following text treats the menservants and maidservants of the sons of Israel, it must be explained what the statutes concerning them involve in the internal sense. Every person can see that the statutes contain within them the arcana of heaven, because they were spoken and commanded orally by Jehovah to Moses on Mount Sinai, and because they follow immediately after the words of the decalogue. Without such arcana, they would be merely civil and statutory laws like the laws of other nations on the earths, in which there is no arcanum of heaven. But the arcana contained here are not manifest except to the angels in the heavens, and consequently not to men except through the internal sense; for this sense teaches how angels perceive the Word, and consequently it teaches the arcana which are within the Word. What these arcana are, and their quality, will be plain in the following explanation of **2** each particular. So that a general idea may be had, it shall be briefly told what is specifically meant by Hebrew servants in the internal sense. In the spiritual church, which the sons of Israel represented, there are two kinds of people: there are those who are in the truth of faith and not in the corresponding good of life, and there are those who are in the good of charity and in corresponding truth of faith. Those who are in the good of charity and in the corresponding truth of faith are those who constitute the church itself, and are the people of the internal church. In the internal sense of the Word, these are the ones who are called the sons of Israel. These are of themselves free, because they are in good; for

<meta>This page continues the explanation of the spiritual differences between those who are truly free and those represented by Hebrew servants, following the discussion on the internal sense of Exodus 21.</meta>

<page-num>201</page-num>

Those who are led by the Lord through good are free (n. 892, 905, 2870-2893). But those who are in the truth of faith and not in a corresponding good of life are people of the external spiritual church. These are the ones who, in the internal sense of the Word, are meant by Hebrew servants. They are represented by servants because the things of the external church are nothing more than matters of service by comparison. The situation is similar with the truth of faith in respect to the good of charity, for the truth of faith serves to introduce a person of the church into the good of charity.

<margin>3</margin>
It is also important to know that anyone who considers everything of the church, and thus everything of salvation, to consist in the truth of faith and not in the good of charity, and who also does good from obedience alone and not from affection which comes from love, cannot be regenerated as those can who are in the good of charity—that is, those who do good from the affection of love. They may indeed be reformed, but not regenerated. Their reformation is treated here in the internal sense in the laws concerning menservants and maidservants. The <term>arcana</term> of that reformation are not known to anyone today, for the reason that an almost total ignorance prevails within the church regarding what the truth of faith does for salvation and what the good of charity does. Indeed, it is not known what charity is, or that charity and faith must form a marriage together so that anything of the church may exist in a person; for the marriage of good and truth is the church itself, because it is heaven in man (n. 2173, 2618, 2728, 2729, 2803, 3132, 3155, 4434, 4823, 5194, 5502, 6179).

8975. *Six years he shall serve.* That this signifies a state of labor and of some conflict and of the resulting confirmation of truth, is evident from the meaning of "six years" as states of labor and conflict; that "six" signifies labor and conflict may be seen above (n. 737, 900, 8888), and that "years" signify states (n. 487, 488, 493, 893, 7839). That

It defines the "six years" of service as a spiritual state of labor and conflict through which faith is confirmed.</summary>

The reason that the confirmation of truth is also signified is that spiritual truth, which is called the truth of faith, is confirmed by labor and combat. It says "somewhat of combat" because those who are in the truth of faith but not in the corresponding good of life are not admitted into any grievous combat—that is, into temptation—since they would fail. For the Lord cannot flow in through any good within them and thus defend them against the evils and falsities that invade during temptations. They are only external men, and whatever flows in from the Lord must flow in through the internal man into the external. When men are not in the good of charity, the internal man is not open; for good is what opens the internal man and what dwells within it.

8976. *And in the seventh year he shall go out free for nothing.* That this signifies a state of truth confirmed without exertion on his part is evident from the meaning of the seventh year as a state of the conjunction of good and truth. For the seventh year signifies the same thing as the seventh day or the Sabbath; that this signifies the conjunction of good and truth, or the heavenly marriage—and thus the state of peace that follows a state of servitude—may be seen above (n. 8494, 8495, 8510, 8888, 8890, 8893). But in this passage, since it deals with those who are in truth and not in the corresponding good of life, the seventh year signifies a state of confirmed truth. The reason is that with such people, there is no conjunction of truth and good as there is with those who are in the good of charity (who are understood in the representative sense by the sons of Israel); instead of this conjunction, there is a confirmation of truth. The meaning of the passage is further evident from the meaning of *for nothing* [*gratis*] as being without exertion on their part; for the truth of faith within them, when they are in labor and in some combat, is confirmed by the Lord without any exertion of theirs. The same is signified by "for nothing," or

Who cannot see that in this statute there is an <term>arcanum</term> which cannot be known except by someone to whom it is revealed? For it appears in its external form as contrary to Divine justice that a wife given to a servant should remain the master’s when the servant leaves his service, seeing that a wife ought to belong to her husband forever. Such also are many other things which were commanded to the sons of Israel by Jehovah, such as that they should ask the Egyptians for vessels of gold and silver and for clothing, and thus should spoil them, besides other things of a similar nature spoken of in their respective places. But these things, though they appear in their external form contrary to Divine justice, as we have said, are still not so; for they flow from the laws of Divine order in the heavens, which are the very laws of justice itself. However, these laws are not clear unless they are unfolded from the literal sense by means of the internal sense. The law from which this statute flows is that spiritual good cannot be conjoined to those who have been in the externals of the church from infancy, but only adjoined as long as they are in combat, and that after combat it recedes.

The reason they are this way is that in their past life they have indulged in worldly loves; and when these loves have taken root, they do not allow spiritual good to be joined to truth, for these loves are completely opposed to that good. 

Nevertheless, spiritual good may occupy the thought when those loves become inactive, as happens when one is in anxiety, in misfortunes, and in sicknesses, and the like. Then an affection for doing good out of charity flows in, but this affection serves only to confirm and root the truths of doctrine more deeply; it cannot be joined to truth. The reason is that the flowing affection of charity fills only the intellectual part of the mind, but does not enter into its voluntary part; and that which does not enter into the voluntary part is not appropriated, and thus is not joined, since the conjunction of good and truth in a person is achieved when truth enters the will. Consequently, when a person wills truth and acts from that will, truth first becomes good—or what is the same thing, faith becomes charity. 

This cannot be **4** achieved with those who have indulged in the loves of the world from infancy, even though they possess the truth of their church’s doctrine; for their voluntary part is possessed by those loves, which are completely opposed to and reject spiritual good. <meta>This page continues the commentary on Exodus 21:5, specifically concluding the explanation of why truth is represented as a manservant and beginning the interpretation of the servant's declaration of love for his family.</meta>

...one who is in truth without corresponding good. The reason why truth is called a manservant, and not the person who is in such truth, is that abstract speech—that is, speech separate from man—is angelic speech. For in heaven they think about a thing without person, because when a person is also thought of, the society which is related to such a thing is called up, and thus the thought is directed there and fixed. For in heaven where there is thought, there is presence; and presence would draw to itself the thoughts of those who are in the society, and would thus disturb the influx from the Divine there. It is different when they think abstractly concerning a thing; then the thought spreads in every direction according to the heavenly form produced by the influx proceeding from the Divine, and this occurs without disturbance to any society. For it infuses itself into the general spheres of the societies, and in that case does not touch or move any individual member in the society; thus, it does not divert anyone from the freedom of thinking according to influx from the Divine. In short, abstract thought can pervade the whole heaven without stopping anywhere, but thought limited to a person or to a place is fixed and halted there.

8986. *I love my master, my wife, and my children.* That this signifies enjoyment of the remembrance of spiritual goods is evident from the meaning of loving—which, in this case, is the enjoyment of remembrance (discussed below); from the meaning of master, as the spiritual good which is the source (see above, n. 8981); from the meaning of woman or wife, as good joined by the spiritual (n. 8981); and from the meaning of children, as the goods and truths derived from them (see n. 8982); therefore, by master, wife, and children considered together, spiritual goods are signified. The reason that enjoyment of the remembrance of such goods is signified by loving is that those who were represented by Hebrew

Such people cannot be affected by truth for the sake of good, but for the sake of enjoyment. Therefore, by "loving" here, because it is said of such people, is signified the enjoyment of remembrance.

8987. *I will not go out free.* That this signifies the enjoyment of obedience is evident from the meaning of "going out free" as a state after combat, which is a state of confirmed and implanted truth only—as mentioned above (n. 8976, 8980, 8984). For the service which lasted six years, and is called a week in Genesis (29:27, 28), signifies labor or some combat, such as those experience who are in truths and not in corresponding good, and who are meant by "Hebrew menservants" in the spiritual sense. These are of such a nature that they cannot be regenerated, but only reformed. For "to be regenerated" is said of those who, by truths which are called truths of faith, allow themselves to be led by the Lord to the good of spiritual life; but "to be reformed" is said of those who, by the truths of faith, cannot be led to the good of spiritual life, but only to the enjoyment of natural life. Those who allow themselves to be **2** regenerated act from affection according to the precepts of faith; but those who do not allow themselves to be regenerated, but only to be reformed, do not act from affection, but from obedience. The difference is this: Those who act from affection act from the heart, and thus from freedom; they also perform truth for the sake of truth and good for the sake of good, and thus they exercise charity for the sake of the neighbor. But those who act from obedience do not act from the heart in this way, and consequently not from freedom. If they seem to themselves to act from the heart and from freedom, it is for the sake of some measure of self-glory which causes it to be perceived as such; and they do not perform truth for the sake of truth, nor good for the sake of good, but for the sake of the enjoyment arising from

But within the church today the knowledge of this distinction has been lost. The reason is that the church today is claimed and said to be based on faith, and not on charity; and few know what faith is, most people believing that faith consists in knowing those things which the doctrine of the church teaches, and in being persuaded that they are true, but not that it consists in living according to these truths. Life according to these truths they call moral life, which they separate from the doctrine of the church and name moral theology. But the learned believe that faith is confidence or trust that they are saved by the Lord’s suffering for them and redeeming them from hell; and they say that those are saved who have this confidence, and thus by faith alone. But such people do not consider that there cannot be the confidence <margin>4</margin>of faith except with those who live a life of charity. These are the reasons why knowledge has been lost concerning the difference between those who are in truths of faith and not in the corresponding good of life, and those who are in a good of life corresponding with the truths of faith; and because that knowledge has been lost, what has now been said concerning those who are in truths and not in good, who are signified by Hebrew menservants, cannot but appear strange.

That these things are signified is plain from what follows in this verse, in which the state of those who are in truths and not in corresponding good is described—namely, that it is a state of perpetual obedience. For those who are in this state are in servitude in respect to those who are in good corresponding with truths; since these latter, because they act from good, act from affection, and those who act from affection act from the will, and thus of themselves; for whatever belongs to the will in man is his <term>*proprium*</term>, since the <term>*esse*</term> of man’s life is his will. But those who act only from obedience do not act from their own will, but from the will of their master, and thus not of themselves but of another; therefore they are relatively in servitude. To act from truths and not from good is to act solely from the intellectual part of the mind; for truths relate to the intellectual and goods to the voluntary part, and to act from the intellectual and not from the voluntary is to act from that which stands outside and serves, since the understanding is given to man to receive truths and to introduce them into the will, so that they may become goods; for truths are called goods when they become part of the will.

<margin>2</margin>But to serve the Lord, by acting according to His commandments and thus by obeying Him, is not to be a servant, but is to be free, for the truest freedom of man consists in being led by the Lord (n. 892, 905, 2870, 2872); because the Lord inspires into the very will of man the good from which he is to act, and though it is from the Lord, it is still perceived as if it were from oneself, and thus done in freedom. All who are in the Lord have this freedom, and it is joined with inexpressible happiness.

This is also true with good and truth in heaven, or among the angels, and also in the church among men.

The good in them is the *esse* itself, and the truth is the *existere* from it; or what is the same, love to the Lord and love toward the neighbor are the very *esse* of heaven and of the church, but faith is the *existere* from them.

From this it is clear why God is also the Divine order, since it is the Divine truth proceeding from the Lord which creates the order in heaven, so much so that Divine truth is order itself.

That Divine truth is order may be seen above (n. 1728, 1919, 7995, 8700).

Moreover—and it is an <term>arcanum</term>—the angels themselves appear in a human form in the heavens entirely according to the truths which belong to them in good, with a beauty and brightness according to the quality of the good from truths.

In the same way, the souls of people in the church appear in heaven.

This is an effect of the Divine truth itself proceeding from the Lord, as may be evident from what has been shown about heaven as the Greatest Man, and about its correspondence with

8990. *And his master shall bore his ear through with his awl.* That this signifies a representation of obedience is evident from the meaning of the *ear* as obedience (see n. 2542, 3869, 4551, 4652-4660); and from the meaning of *boring through with an awl*—namely, at a door or at a doorpost—as attaching, and here (because obedience is involved) as ascribing. Therefore it follows that *he shall serve him forever*, that is, he shall obey. From this it is clear that the boring of the ear through with an awl at the door or at the doorpost by his master represents obedience.

<margin>2</margin> How this is may be evident from what precedes: namely, that those who are in truths alone and not in corresponding good—that is, who are in faith and not in charity—are not free, but servants. For those who act from good or charity are free, because they act from themselves; to act from good or charity is to act from the heart, that is, from the will, and thus from what is one's own. For that which belongs to the will is one's own, and that which is done from the will is said to come from the heart. But those who are only in the truths of faith and not in the good of charity are relatively servants, for they do not act of themselves; they do not have good within themselves from which they may act, but it is outside of themselves, and they act only as often as they remember it. Those who are of this character even to the end of life remain in that state after death; they cannot be brought to a state of acting from the affection of charity (thus from good), but they act from obedience. These, in the Greatest Man (which is heaven), constitute those things that serve the internal parts, such as membranes and skins (n. 8977, 8980).

<margin>3</margin> From these things it may be evident how the case is with faith alone, and thus with those who from

death. Doctrine puts faith in the first place and the good of charity in the second place, or even in the last place. Those who actually—that is, in their life itself—regard faith in this way are "Hebrew servants" in the representative sense. From these same considerations, it may also be concluded how it is for those who make salvation consist entirely of the truths of faith and not at all of the good of charity; namely, that actually, or in their life itself, they cannot enter heaven. This is because good reigns in heaven and not truth without good; neither is truth truly truth, nor faith truly faith, except

<margin>4</margin> with those who are in good. That the master’s boring of the ear with an awl represents obedience is also plain from this: to affix the ear to the door is to cause attention to be given to those things which the master, who is in the chamber, commands. Thus, it is to hear continually and, consequently, to obey—in the spiritual sense, the things which "good" wills and commands; for the master of the servant represents spiritual good (n. 8981, 8986). Because the ear signifies hearing, which relates to obedience, the expression "to pluck the ear"—meaning to make a person attentive and to help them remember—has flowed into human speech from its origin in the spiritual world. Similarly, the expressions to "hear" and "hearken" to anyone mean to obey. For the internal sense of many expressions has flowed from correspondences out of the spiritual world—for example, when we speak of spiritual light and the resulting sight as faith, or of spiritual fire and the life from it as love.

<margin>5</margin> The boring of the ear was performed with an awl because an awl signifies the same thing as a stake, pin, or nail—namely, an affixing or joining to something, and in the spiritual sense, an assignment to something. But the awl was an instrument of service or labor; therefore, it serves to represent the assignment of the servant to perpetual obedience. That stakes, pins, or nails signify an affixing or joining is manifest from the pas-

passages where they are named — as in Isaiah (xxii. 23; xxxiii. 20; xli. 7; liv. 2; Jer. x. 4; Exod. xxvii. 19; xxxviii. 31; Num. iii. 37; iv. 32).

8991. *And he shall serve him forever.* That this signifies eternity is evident from the meaning of serving as obeying, for servants are those who obey and masters are those who command; that those represented by menservants are those who have done good from obedience, but not from the affection of charity, is evident from what precedes; and from the meaning of "forever" as eternity. In the literal sense, "forever" here signifies service to his master even to the end of his life, but in the internal or spiritual sense it signifies what is eternal, because it signifies the state after death. It is said "to eternity" because those who do good from the obedience of faith and not from the affection of charity, who are represented by menservants, can never be brought to a state of good in the other life—that is, to act from good; for everyone's life remains with them after death. As a person is when they die, so they remain—according to the common saying, "As the tree falls, so it lies"; not that they are exactly as they were in the hours of death, but rather as they have become from the whole course of their life by the time they die. Therefore, those who during their life in the world have acquired the habit of doing good from obedience only, and not from charity, remain so for eternity. They are indeed perfected in regards to obedience, but they do not reach any degree of charity.

8992. Verses 7-11. *And when a man sells his daughter to be a maidservant, she shall not go out as the menservants go. If she is displeasing to her master, so that he will not marry her, then he shall let her be redeemed: he shall have no power to sell her to a foreign people, seeing he has dealt treacherously with her. And if he has betrothed her to his son, he shall deal with her according to the custom of daughters. If he*
