How this is so cannot be known unless it is known how things stand with the internal man in respect to the external man. The internal man cannot live a spiritual life unless the external man is in agreement. Therefore, a person cannot be regenerated unless the natural man is also regenerated. From this it follows that if the truth of faith in the natural or external man has been ex-
<margin>2</margin> tinguished, it can no longer serve the internal. It is the same as with external sight in respect to internal sight. If the external sight has been injured, it can no longer serve the internal; for if the external sight distorts objects, the internal cannot see by means of it except with distortion. Or it is like the rest of the members of the body which are subject to the will, such as the arms, the hands, the fingers, and the feet. If these are distorted, then the will cannot act through them except in a distorted way. It is similar in the case of the natural or external man in respect to the internal. If outward truths in the external or natural man are perverted or extinguished, the internal man cannot see truth, and thus cannot think and perceive except pervertedly or falsely. From these examples it is plain why the natural man must be regenerated in order for the person to be regenerated—see also what has been said above on this subject (n. 3286, 3321, 3469, 3493, 3573, 3620, 3623, 3679, 4588, 4618, 4667, 5165, 5168, 5427, 5428, 5477, 6299, 6564, 8742-8747, 9043).

For without a hidden cause, why would it be ordained that servants should be let go free for the sake of an eye or a tooth, and not for the sake of other body parts when they are injured or destroyed? But the hidden reason becomes apparent when it is known what is signified in the spiritual sense by an eye and by a tooth. When this is known, the reason for the statute is revealed.

9065. *And when an ox shall gore a man or a woman with its horn.* That this signifies if affection for evil in the <term>natural</term> should injure the truth or the good of faith, is evident from the signification of goring with the horn as injuring, for a horn signifies the power of falsity from evil (see n. 2832) and goring signifies injuring; from the signification of an ox, as affection for good in the <term>natural</term> (n. 2180, 2566, 2781, 2830, 5913, 8937), and so in the opposite sense, affection for evil in the <term>natural</term>; from the signification of man, as the truth of faith (n. 9034); and from the signification of woman, as the good of faith (see n. 4823, 6014, 8337).

9066. *So that they die.* That this signifies even so as to destroy them, is evident without explanation.

9067. *The ox shall certainly be stoned.* That this signifies the punishment for the destroyed truth and good of faith, is evident from the signification of being stoned (see n. 5156, 7456, 8575, 8799).

the truth and the good of the faith of the church; and from the signification of flesh, as the good of celestial love, and in the opposite sense the evil of self-love (n. 3813, 7850, 8409, 8431).

9069. *But the owner of the ox shall be quit.* That this signifies that the evil is not from the <term>internal man</term>, because it is from the <term>voluntary</term> and not from the <term>intellectual</term>, is evident from the signification of the owner of the ox, as the <term>internal</term> or spiritual man, since by the ox is signified affection for evil in the <term>natural</term> or external man (n. 9065); and thus the owner or lord of the ox means the <term>internal man</term> — for the <term>internal man</term> is lord of the external or <term>natural</term> man, since he can rule over the affections for evil in the <term>natural</term>, and does rule when the <term>natural</term> is subordinate, as it is with the <term>regenerate</term>; and from the signification of quit or guiltless, as without blame. The reason is said to be that the evil came forth from the <term>voluntary</term> and not from the <term>intellectual</term>; for evil from the <term>voluntary</term> and not at the same time from the <term>intellectual</term> does not condemn, since man does not see it, and thus does not consider whether it is evil, and therefore he is not conscious of it. Such evil is evil derived hereditarily, before man is instructed that it is evil, and also after he has been instructed and is only in external life or the life of the body, and not at the same time in the internal life which is of the understanding. For to see and to understand that a thing is evil and still to do it makes a man guilty, as the Lord teaches in John: *The Pharisees said, "Are we also blind?" Such is the evil which is signified by an ox thrusting with the horn a man or a woman before the owner of the ox knew that he was accustomed to thrusting with the horn. <meta>catchword: In the</meta>

9070. *But if the ox was accustomed to goring with its horn in the past.* That this signifies if an affection for evil has existed for a long time, is evident from the signification of an ox accustomed to goring with its horn, as an affection for evil (see n. 9065); and from the signification of "in the past," as a preceding state and time (n. 6983, 7114), thus what has existed before and for a long time.

9072. *And he has not kept it in.* That this signifies no restraint, is evident from the signification of "keeping in," when said of evil of the <term>voluntary</term> which has passed into the light of the <term>intellectual</term>, as restraining or restraint; for the understanding is given to man so that he may see evil and may then restrain it.

9073. *But that it has killed a man or a woman.* That this signifies if it has destroyed the truth and the good of faith, is evident from the signification of "killing," as destroying; and from the signification of "man," as the truth of faith, and of "woman" as the good of faith (see n. 9065).

9074. *The ox shall be stoned.* That this signifies the punishment for destroyed truth, is evident as above (see n. 9067).

9075. *And his owner also shall die.* That this signifies the damnation of the internal man is evident from the signification of the "owner of the ox" as the internal man (see n. 9069); and from the signification of "being put to death" or "dying" as damnation—of which we have spoken above (n. 9008). The reason the ox was to be stoned and its owner was to die if it had been known to him that the ox was accustomed to goring with its horn and he had not kept it in, is because the spiritual meaning of this judgment or law is that the person who knows a thing to be evil and does not restrain it is guilty; for he approves it, and thus extinguishes the light of truth and, along with it, the faith of truth in his conception. When this is extinguished, good from the Lord is not received; therefore the internal man cannot be opened, and consequently he cannot be given spiritual life, which is the life of truth and the good of faith. When a person is in such a state, he then lives a natural life, which is the life of the external man. But this life without spiritual life is dead; thus ensues damnation (see n. 7494).

9076. *If expiation be laid on him.* That this signifies that he may be freed from damnation is evident from the signification of "expiation" as being free from damnation. For expiations were laid upon those who did evil not intentionally or with deceit; these were various and were in such cases called "ransoms" or "redemptions" of the soul, for by them the life was redeemed. But those external things signified internal things—namely, expiation signified liberation from damnation; thus ransom or redemption signified the improvement of spiritual life by actual repentance. Inasmuch as expiation signified liberation from damnation, it therefore also signified the pardon of sins, and thus cleansing from sins.

9089. *And the dead* [*beast*] *shall be his.* That this signifies that the evil or the falsity shall remain with him is evident from the signification of *dead* (when it is said of good or truth in the natural man, which is signified by an ox or ass) as evil or falsity. For when good dies, evil takes its place, and when truth dies, falsity takes its place—because what is dead means evil and falsity, and therefore those who will evil and believe falsity are called "dead" (see n. 7494). It is also evident from the signification of *being his* as remaining with him. The situation is this: if anyone destroys good or truth in himself or in others by means of falsity, he does it from evil—thus from the <term>voluntary</term> by means of the <term>intellectual</term>—for all evil belongs to the <term>voluntary</term> and all falsity belongs to the <term>intellectual</term>. That which is done by both remains, for it infects the whole life of the person. It is different if evil proceeds from the <term>voluntary</term> and not at the same time from the <term>intellectual</term> (n. 9009). From this, it is clear what the basis of this statute is: "the dead beast shall be his."

9090. *And when a man’s ox shall hurt the ox of his companion with a thrust.* That this signifies two truths whose affections are diverse, where the affection of one injures the affection of the other, is evident from the signification of "hurting" as the injury of truth—as mentioned above (n. 9057); from the signification of an "ox" as an affection for good, and in the opposite sense, an affection for evil in the natural man—as also mentioned above (n. 9065); and from the signification of a "man" as truth (n. 9034). Therefore, by "a man and his companion," two truths are signified. The reason that diverse affections are signified is because it is "oxen"—by which affections are signified—that injure each other; for things that are diverse injure each other,
<margin>2</margin> but things that are not diverse do not. Someone who does not know how it is with representatives and correspondences may wonder how an ox can signify an affection for good or for evil in a person, since an ox is a beast. But let them know that all beasts signify things that belong to some affection or inclination. In the spiritual world this is very well known, for in that world beasts of various kinds are frequently seen, such as oxen, bullocks, cows, horses, mules, asses, sheep, goats, kids, and lambs, as well as evil beasts like tigers, panthers, bears, dogs, hogs, and serpents, and also beasts that are never seen on the earth, besides birds of
<margin>3</margin> various kinds. That such things are seen there is beyond the belief of those who believe that nothing exists which they do not see with their physical eyes. Likewise, these same people do not believe that there are any spirits or angels, much less that they appear to themselves as people, that they see one another, speak with one another, and touch one another. The reason is that such persons are so sensual and corporeal that they believe only bodies are alive. Therefore, as already said, such things are beyond their belief. He who divides these two parts dissipates the things which belong to one part, for one must live from the other; therefore the other also perishes. It is the same in the case of him who divides truth from good, or what is the same, faith from charity. He who does this destroys both. In a word, all things which ought to be united in

Divine <term>arcanum</term>. The <term>arcanum</term> was that Divine truths were scattered by the Jews, for the Lord was the Divine truth; and hence He is called the Word (John 1). The Word is Divine truth; His garments represented truths in their external form, and His tunic represented truths in their internal form; the dividing of the garments represented the scattering of the truths of faith by the Jews. That garments are truths in an external form may be seen above (nos. 2576, 5248, 5954, 6918), and also that a tunic is truth in an internal form (no. 4677). Truths in the external form are such as the truths of the Word in its literal sense, while truths in the internal form are such as the truths of the Word in its spiritual sense. The division of the garments into four parts signified total scattering, in the same way as the division in Zechariah (14:4), and in other passages; likewise the division into two parts, as we read concerning the veil of the temple (Matt. 27:51; Mark 15:38). The splitting of the rocks at that time also (Matt. 27:51) represented the scattering of all things of faith, for a rock represents the Lord regarding faith, and thus it represents faith from the Lord.

9094. *And the dead also they shall divide.* That this signifies that the harmful affection shall also be scattered is evident from the meaning of "dead," as evil and falsity—mentioned above (no. 9008)—therefore, by a dead ox is signified an affection for evil and falsity in the natural man, and thus a harmful affection, because evil causes harm by means of falsity; and from the meaning of "dividing," as scattering (no. 9093). How it is in respect to the things contained in this verse in the internal sense can hardly be explained to human understanding. These things are such as can be understood by angels, and only to some extent by humans. For angels see the <term>arcana</term> of the Word in the light which comes from the Lord, in which light countless things are presented to view that cannot be expressed in spoken words, and do not even enter into the ideas of thought among people as long as they live in the physical body. In people, the light of heaven flows into the light of the world, and thus into such things there as either extinguish, reject, or darken, and thereby dim the light of heaven. The cares of the world and of the body are such things, especially those which flow from the loves of self and of the world. Therefore, the things belonging to angelic wisdom are for the most part unutterable and also incomprehensible. <margin>2</margin> Nevertheless, a person enters into such wisdom after laying aside the body—that is, after death—but only the person who has received the life of faith and charity from the Lord while in the world; for the capacity for receiving angelic wisdom lies within the good of faith and charity. That the things angels see and think in the light of heaven are ineffable has also been granted to me to know through much experience; for when I have been elevated into that light, I seemed to myself to understand everything the angels spoke there; but when I was let down from there into the light of the external or natural man, and desired to recall the things I had heard there while in this light, I could not express them in words, nor even comprehend them through ideas of thought—except for a few, and those few were indeed obscure. From this it is manifest that the things seen and heard in heaven are such as "eye has not seen, nor ear heard." Such are the things that lie concealed inmostly in the internal sense of the Word; and it is the same with the things contained in the internal sense in this and the following verses. The things contained within that can be explained to the understanding are these: all truths in man have life from affections that belong to some love. <margin>3</margin> Truth without life from love is like sound flowing from the mouth without an idea, or like the sound of an automaton. Hence it is plain that the life of a person's understanding comes from the life of their will; consequently, the life of truth comes from the life of good, for truth refers to the understanding and

If, therefore, there are two truths which do not live from the same general affection, but from different affections, they must necessarily be dissipated, for they are in collision with each other. And when truths are dissipated, their affections are also dissipated; for there is a general affection under which all truths in a person are associated; this general affection is good. These are the things that can be said concerning what is signified in the internal sense by the oxen of two men, one of which hurts the other with a thrust so that it dies, and by the statute that in such a case the live ox should be sold and its silver divided, and the dead ox should also be divided.

<margin>4</margin> Who that belongs to the church does not know that Divine things are contained in the whole and in every part of the Word? But who can see Divine things in these laws concerning oxen and donkeys falling into a pit, and concerning oxen goring with their horns, if they are regarded and explained only according to the literal sense? Yet they are Divine even in the literal sense if they are regarded and unfolded at the same time according to the internal sense; for in that sense every single part of the Word treats of the Lord, of His Kingdom, and His church, and thus of Divine things. For anything to be Divine and holy, it must deal with Divine and holy things: the subject matter determines this. The worldly and civil affairs—such as the judgments, the statutes, and the laws promulgated by the Lord from Mount Sinai, which are contained in this and the following chapters of the book of Exodus—are Divine and holy by inspiration; yet inspiration is not dictation, but is influx from the Divine. That which flows in from the Divine passes through heaven and there becomes celestial and spiritual; but when it enters the world, it becomes of the world, though inwardly containing the celestial and the spiritual. From this it is plain from what source and where the Divine is in the Word, and what inspiration is.

9095. *Or if it be known that the ox was wont to thrust with the horn in time past.* That this signifies if it were previously known that such was its inclination, is evident from the significance of "if it were known," or attested, as having passed into the intellectual part <note>The understanding or intellect.</note> (see n. 9071), for what has passed there from the will <note>The voluntary part of the mind.</note> has become known; and from the significance of "that the ox was wont to thrust with the horn in time past," as meaning that such had been the inclination heretofore—as also mentioned above (n. 9070).

9096. *And his owner hath not kept him in.* That this signifies and if he has not kept it under restraint, is evident from the significance of "keeping in," as keeping under restraint—namely, the inclination for evil in the natural self, which otherwise would injure the truth of faith. It means keeping under restraint because "becoming known" signifies passing into the intellectual part (n. 9095), and the intellect is the faculty which perceives evil. That which is perceived may be restrained and kept in bonds, not by the intellect itself, but through the intellect by the Lord. For the Lord flows into those things in a person which are known to him, but not into those things which are unknown to him. By "keeping in bonds" is meant to hold in and to restrain. Bonds in the spiritual sense are nothing else than the affections of love, for these are what lead a person and what restrain him. If affections for evil lead him, there must be affections for truth originating in good to restrain him. Internal bonds in man are affections for truth and for good; these are also called bonds of conscience. But external bonds are the affections of self-love and of the love of the world, for these lead a person in external affairs. If the latter affections descend from internal bonds—which are affections for truth and for good—they are good, for then a person loves himself and the world, not for the sake of self and the world, but for the sake of good uses from himself and the world (n. 7819, 7820, 8995). But if the affections of self-love and of the love of the world do not descend from internal bonds, the affections

From this it may be known what is meant by internal bonds and by external bonds, of which frequent mention has been made. But bonds so called are not bonds except with reference to opposites; for he who does anything from affection which is of the love of good acts from freedom; but he who acts from affection which is of the love of evil seems to himself to act from freedom, but does not act from freedom, because he acts from the lusts which are from hell. He alone is free who is in affection for good, because he is led by the Lord. Therefore man has freedom in doing good and actual servitude in doing evil. He who believes that Christian liberty extends further is very greatly in error.

**9097.** *Repaying he shall repay ox for ox.* That this signifies restitution in its entirety is evident from the significance of repaying, as restoring—restoration in its entirety is signified by "repaying he shall repay"; and from the significance of an ox, as affection in the natural <note>The external or natural level of the mind.</note> (see n. 9065).

**9097½.** *And the dead shall be his.* That this signifies for the injuring affection, is evident from the significance of an ox, as an affection for evil; for by "the dead" is signified evil and falsity, according to what was said above (n. 9089). The nature of the things contained in the internal sense <note>The deeper spiritual meaning within the literal words of the Bible.</note> in this verse may be concluded from what was shown above (n. 9094).

# CHAPTER XXI. VER. 37.

**9098.** **Verse 37.** *When a man steals an ox, or a sheep, and kills it, or sells it; he shall repay five oxen for an ox, and four sheep for a sheep.* "When a man steals an ox, or a sheep" signifies one who takes away from anyone his good, whether exterior or interior; "and kills it" signifies if he has extinguished it; "or sells it" signifies or if he has alienated it; "he shall repay five oxen for an ox" signifies a corresponding punishment and much; "and four sheep for a sheep" signifies a corresponding punishment in full.

**9099.** *When a man steals an ox, or a sheep.* That this signifies one who takes away from anyone his good, whether exterior or interior, is evident from the significance of stealing, as taking away from anyone his spiritual goods (see n. 5135, 8906); from the significance of an ox, as affection for good in the natural <note>The "natural" refers to the external or worldly level of the human mind.</note>, thus exterior good (n. 2180, 2566, 2781, 2830, 5913, 8937); and from the significance of a sheep, as interior good; for the animals which belong to the flock signify those things which belong to interior good, and the animals which belong to the herd signify those things which belong to exterior good (n. 2566, 5913, 6048, 8937).

**9100.** *And kills it.* That this signifies if he has extinguished it, is evident without explanation.

**9101.** *Or sells it.* That this signifies or if he has alienated it, is evident from the significance of selling, as alienating (see n. 4098, 4752, 4758, 5886, 6143).

**9102.** *He shall repay five oxen for an ox.* That this signifies corresponding punishment and much, is evident from the significance of five, as some amount (see n. 4638, 5291), and also much (n. 5708, 5956); and from the significance of repaying, as amendment (n. 9087), and as restitution (n. 9097). That it also means punishment is because when alienated good is to be amended or restored, a person suffers painful things; for he is either left to his evil,

9103. **And four sheep for a sheep.** That this also signifies corresponding punishment in full is evident from the significance of *four* as conjunction. For four signifies the same thing as *two*, because the one number arises from the other as its double—that *two* means conjunction can be seen above (n. 5194, 8423), and thus *four* also (see n. 1686, 8877). From this, it follows that these numbers also mean "in full," because what is conjoined is full. It is also evident from the significance of *sheep* as interior good—see just above (n. 9099). Corresponding punishment is signified by "repaying," namely, "four sheep for a sheep" (n. 9102). 

Interior good is the good called charity in the interior man, and exterior good is charity in the exterior man. This latter good must live from the former, for the good of charity in the interior man is the good of spiritual life, and the good of charity in the exterior man is the good of natural life derived from it. The latter good is felt by a person as enjoyment, but the former good is not felt as a sensation, but rather as a perception of how things ought to be, which creates a contented mind. In the other life, spiritual good is also felt as a sensation.

above (n. 575, 647, 648, 755, 813, 1963, 1988, 2075, 2252, 3252, 4264, 4495, 4670, 5265, 6175); in this case, the things involving restoration are: the number five represents the restoration of exterior good and much more, and the number four represents the restoration of interior good in full. Interior good must be restored in full because this good constitutes the spiritual life of man; and unless spiritual life is restored in full, the exterior good that constitutes the natural life cannot be restored. That the external man is regenerated by means of the internal man may be seen above (n. 9043, 9046, 9061). But good in the external or natural man cannot be restored in full, because the injury remains there like a scar that has become callous. These are the things that those numbers involve. 

<margin>3</margin> A few more words, however, shall be said concerning the restoration of exterior good (which constitutes the natural life of man) by interior good (which constitutes his spiritual life). The natural part of man sees things in the light of the world, which is called natural light. Man gains this light for himself through the objects that enter by sight and hearing—that is, through objects belonging to the world. Thus, a person sees those things inwardly within himself, almost as the eye sees them. The objects that enter through those senses appear to him at first as pleasurable and enjoyable. Afterward, as the person matures from infancy, he distinguishes between different enjoyments. Therefore, he learns to discern, and does so more perfectly by degrees. When light from heaven flows into these things, then the person begins to see them spiritually, and first to discern between the useful and the non-useful. From this, he begins to have an insight as to what is true; for that which is useful to him is true to him, and that which is useless is not true. This insight increases according to the influx of the light of heaven, until at length he discerns not only between truths, but also between truths within these truths,

**4** In this way, man now has perception; but it is still not yet spiritual perception. Spiritual perception does not come from natural truths, but from spiritual truths. Spiritual truths are the truths which are called truths of faith. The reason why spiritual perception comes from these truths is that the light of heaven is Divine truth proceeding from the Lord, and this shines as light before the eyes of the angels, and also shines in their understandings, and gives them intelligence and wisdom, but with variety according to its reception in good. Therefore, the knowledges of spiritual things must be in a person’s natural part in order for there to be spiritual perception; and knowledges of spiritual things must come from revelation. When the light of heaven flows into such knowledges, it flows into its own; for, as was said, that light is the Divine truth proceeding from the Lord (n. 1053, 1521-1533, 1619-1632, 2776, 3138, 3167, 3195, 3222, 3223, 3341, 3636, 3643, 4180, 4302, 4408, 4415, 4527, 5400, 6032, 6313, 6608). In this way, man has intelligence and wisdom in such things as belong to eternal life, and these increase according to the reception of that light—that is, of the truths of faith in good; good is
**5** charity. That the natural or external man is regenerated and also corrected and restored through the internal man may be evident from what has just been said. For the things which are in the external or natural man live from the light of heaven, since this light is living light because it proceeds from the Lord, who is life itself; but they do not live from natural light, for this light in itself is dead. In order, therefore, that those things which are in natural light may live, there must be an influx of living light through the internal man from the Lord. Such influx adapts itself according to the knowledges of truth

<summary>This text explains that spiritual perception arises when Divine light flows through the internal man into spiritual truths (truths of faith) held in the natural mind. It emphasizes that the external man is regenerated and given life only through this influx of living light from the Lord, as natural light itself is dead.</summary>
<keywords>internal man, external man, spiritual perception, truths of faith, light of heaven, Divine truth, knowledges, regeneration, charity, influx</keywords>

<header>No. 9105.] CHAPTER XXI.</header>

are analogous and corresponding in the natural part, according to the willingness to comply within it. Thus it is plain that the external or natural part of man must be regenerated through his internal part. In the same way, the good in the natural part that has been taken away and alienated must be corrected and restored.

### CONTINUATION CONCERNING THE SPIRITS AND THE INHABITANTS OF THE PLANET SATURN.

9104. Some spirits from this earth traveled to the spirits of the planet Saturn, who, as already said, are far off at a great distance, for they appear at the edge of our solar world. The journey occurs in an instant, for distances in the other life are appearances arising from the diversity of states of life (see n. 2625, 2837, 3356, 3387, 3404, 4321, 4882, 5605, 7381), and conjunction is brought about by a state of desire to speak with another. This is why those who have shared some connection in the world—whether through love, friendship, or high regard—meet together in the other life when they so desire; but they are afterward separated according to the differences in their states of life.

9105. After the spirits of the planet Saturn had spoken with the spirits of our earth, they spoke with me from where they were by means of intermediate spirits. They said they were surprised that spirits from this earth so often came to them and asked what God they worship. Whenever they notice that the spirits of this earth make such inquiries, they answer that they are insane; for there cannot be a greater insanity than to ask what God anyone worships, since there is only one God for everyone in the universe. They said that these spirits are still more insane because they do not know that the Lord is the Only God and that He rules the universal heaven and, consequently, the entire world; for He who rules heaven also rules the world, because the world is ruled through heaven.

<summary>This page explains that the natural man is regenerated and restored through the internal man, and describes an encounter with spirits from Saturn who believe it is insane to ask what God someone worships, as there is only one Lord of the universe.</summary>
<keywords>regeneration, natural man, internal man, spirits of Saturn, solar world, states of life, Divine rule, universal heaven</keywords>

--- Page 334 ---
<meta>This page continues the discussion regarding the spirits of Saturn, specifically focusing on their interactions with spirits from other planets and the symbolic relationship between Saturn, Earth, and the internal and external man.</meta>

<page-num>328</page-num>
<header>EXODUS. [No. 9106.</header>

9106. They said further that another kind of spirits, who travel in groups, frequently come to them, wishing to know how things are with them, and that they learn from them what they know by various methods. Concerning these, they said that they are not insane except in this: that they have such a great desire to know, for no other purpose than simply to know. They were afterward instructed that these spirits are from the planet Mercury; that knowledge and the intelligence derived from it alone delight them, and not so much the uses resulting from them—unless, indeed, knowledge itself is considered a use to them (see n. 6811, 6815, 6921-6932, 7069-7079, 7170-7177).

9107. I have been permitted to know from clear experience in what respects the spirits of our earth and the spirits of the planet Saturn differ, and then at the same time to know how the spiritual or internal man and the natural or external man are in conflict and at war with each other when the latter is not in faith and charity. For the spirits of the planet Saturn correspond, in the <term>Greatest Man</term>, to that sense which is midway between the spiritual and natural sense, but moving away from the natural and toward the spiritual (n. 8953). The spirits of our earth, on the other hand, correspond to the natural and corporeal sense. Consequently, they correspond to the external man, while those of Saturn correspond to the internal man. How great a conflict and struggle there is between them, when the external or natural man does not receive spiritual life through faith and love to the Lord, has been shown by this experience.

9108. The spirits of the planet Saturn came into view from a distance, and at the time many spirits from our earth were present. Upon seeing those from Saturn, these spirits became as if insane and began to harass them by instilling unworthy thoughts concerning faith and also concerning the Lord. And while they were attacking them with words, they also threw themselves into their midst and, out of the insanity they were in, tried to do them harm.

<summary>This page describes the interactions between spirits of Saturn, Mercury, and Earth, using these groups to illustrate the conflict between the internal (spiritual) man and the external (natural) man. It notes that spirits from Earth often harass those from Saturn when they lack faith and charity.</summary>
<keywords>spirits, Mercury, Saturn, Greatest Man, spiritual man, natural man, internal man, external man, faith, charity, Lord, conflict</keywords>

--- Page 335 ---
<meta>This page continues the description of the interaction between spirits from Earth and spirits from Saturn, illustrating the differences between the natural and internal man.</meta>

<page-num>329</page-num>
<header>No. 9110.] CHAPTER XXI.</header>

But the spirits of Saturn were not at all afraid, because they were secure and also at peace; but those from our earth, when they were in the midst of them, began to breathe with difficulty and thus to be in anguish, and so they threw themselves out—one in one direction and another in another—and disappeared.

9109. Those standing by perceived from this the nature of the external or natural man when separated from the internal, especially when he enters a spiritual sphere, which is the sphere of life of the internal man—namely, that he is insane. For a sphere that flows from the life of his love surrounds everyone (see n. 1048, 1053, 1316, 1504–1512, 4464, 5179, 6206, 7454). The reason is that the natural man, when separated from the spiritual, has wisdom only from the world and not at all from heaven; and he who is wise only from the world believes nothing except what the senses grasp, and what he believes, he believes from the fallacies of the senses and, consequently, from falsehoods. Therefore, spiritual things are nothing to him, to the extent that he can hardly endure hearing mention made of the spiritual. This is also why he does not understand what the internal man is, and therefore does not believe that an internal man exists. Therefore, such people are insane when they are held in a spiritual sphere. It is different while they are living in the world. Then they either think naturally about spiritual things, or they turn away their ear—that is, they hear but do not pay attention. That such people experience pain and blindness when they are taken up into a spiritual sphere may be seen above (8797).

9110. From the experience above, it was also made evident that the natural man cannot introduce himself into the spiritual—that is, he cannot ascend. But when a person is in faith and thereby in spiritual life and thinks, then the spiritual man descends—that is, he thinks in the natural. For there is spiritual influx—that is, influx from the spiritual world into the natural—but not the reverse. That physical influx is alto-

<summary>This page describes how spirits from Earth, representing the natural man, experience distress and "insanity" when entering the spiritual sphere of the spirits of Saturn. It concludes that spiritual influx moves from the internal/spiritual man to the natural man, and not the other way around.</summary>
<keywords>spirits of Saturn, natural man, internal man, spiritual sphere, spiritual influx, physical influx, Swedenborg, spiritual life</keywords>

...together contrary to order and contrary to nature, and thus impossible, as may be seen above (n. 3721, 5119, 5259, 5779, 6322, 8237). Therefore, when the natural man who is separated from the internal comes into the sphere of spiritual life, he is seized first with blindness, then with insanity, and finally with anguish. It is indeed for this reason that those who are in hell have no desire to look toward heaven (n. 4225, 4226, 8137, 8265, 8945, 8946).

9111. At the close of the following chapter, an account shall be given of the spirits of the moon, and also why the Lord willed to be born on our earth and not on another.
